3
Braiding on
O_{\mathop{\mathrm{bd}},\Lambda_{0}}^{\mathop{\mathrm{bu}}}

In this section, we show that for
O_{\mathop{\mathrm{bd}},\Lambda_{0}}^{\mathop{\mathrm{bu}}}
, we may assign braiding.
We use the following notation.

Notation 3.1
.

For cones
\{{\Lambda_{1\alpha}}\}\subset{\mathcal{C}}_{{\mathop{\mathrm{bk}}}}^{U}

(resp.
\{{\Lambda_{l2\alpha}}\}\subset{\mathcal{C}}_{{\mathop{\mathrm{bd}}}}^{l}
) and
\{{\Lambda_{r2\beta}}\}\subset{\mathcal{C}}_{{\mathop{\mathrm{bd}}}}^{r}
,
we write
\{{\Lambda_{1\alpha}}\}\leftarrow_{r}\{{\Lambda_{r2\beta}}\}
(resp
\{{\Lambda_{l2\alpha}}\}\leftarrow_{r}\{{\Lambda_{r2\beta}}\}
) if
there exists an
\varepsilon>0
such that

\arg({\Lambda_{1\alpha}})_{\varepsilon}\cap\arg({\Lambda_{r2\beta}})_{\varepsilon}=\emptyset

(resp.
\arg({\Lambda_{l2\alpha}})_{\varepsilon}\cap\arg({\Lambda_{r2\beta}})_{\varepsilon}=\emptyset

) for all
\alpha,\beta
.
We also write
{\Lambda_{1}}\leftarrow_{r}{\Lambda_{r2}}
if
\{{\Lambda_{1}}\}\leftarrow_{r}\{{\Lambda_{r2}}\}
.
See Figure
10
for an example of
\Lambda_{l}\leftarrow\Lambda_{r}
.

For
{\Lambda_{1}}=\Lambda_{\bm{a},\theta,\varphi}\in{\mathcal{C}}_{{\mathop{\mathrm{bk}}}}^{U}
,

{\Lambda_{r2}}\in{\mathcal{C}}_{{\mathop{\mathrm{bd}}}}^{r}
and
t\in{\mathbb{R}}
, we set

{\Lambda_{1}}(t):={\Lambda_{1}}+t\bm{e}_{\theta}
,

{\Lambda_{r2}}(t):={\Lambda_{r2}}+t\bm{e}_{0}
.

Figure 9:

Figure 10:

We first investigate the asymptotic behaviors of our endomorphisms.

Lemma 3.1
.

Consider the setting in subsection
1.1
. Assume Assumption
1.7
.
Let
{\Lambda_{l1}}\in{\mathcal{C}}_{{\mathop{\mathrm{bd}}}}^{l}
and
{\Lambda_{r2}}\in{\mathcal{C}}_{{\mathop{\mathrm{bd}}}}^{r}
,
with
{\Lambda_{l1}}\leftarrow_{r}{\Lambda_{r2}}
.
Then for any
\sigma\in O^{r}_{\mathop{\mathrm{bd}}}
and
V_{\sigma,{\Lambda_{r2}}(t)}\in{\mathcal{V}}_{\sigma{\Lambda_{r2}}(t)}^{{\mathop{\mathrm{bd}}}}
,
t\geq 0
, we have

\displaystyle\lim_{t\to\infty}\left\|\left.\left(T_{\sigma}^{\mathrm{(l)}{\Lambda_{r2}}(t)V_{{\sigma},{\Lambda_{r2}}(t)}}-\mathop{\mathrm{id}}\nolimits\right)\right|_{\pi_{\mathop{\mathrm{bd}}}\left({\mathcal{A}}_{({\Lambda_{l1}})^{c}\cap\mathop{\mathrm{H}_{U}}}\right)^{\prime}}\right\|=0,

(3.1)

and

\displaystyle\lim_{t\to\infty}\sup_{\sigma\in O^{r}_{\mathop{\mathrm{bd},{\Lambda_{r2}}(t)}}}\left\|\left.\left(T_{\sigma}^{\mathrm{(l)}{\Lambda_{r2}}(t)\mathbb{I}}-\mathop{\mathrm{id}}\nolimits\right)\right|_{\pi_{\mathop{\mathrm{bd}}}\left({\mathcal{A}}_{({\Lambda_{l1}})^{c}\cap\mathop{\mathrm{H}_{U}}}\right)^{\prime}}\right\|=0.

(3.2)

Remark 3.2
.

If the Haag duality holds,
then we have

\displaystyle\pi_{\mathop{\mathrm{bd}}}\left({\mathcal{A}}_{({\Lambda_{l1}})^{c}\cap\mathop{\mathrm{H}_{U}}}\right)^{\prime}=\pi_{\mathop{\mathrm{bd}}}\left({\mathcal{A}}_{{\Lambda_{l1}}}\right)^{\prime\prime}.

(3.3)

Hence if
\Lambda_{r2}(t)\cap\Lambda_{l1}=\emptyset
(namely, if
t
is large enough)
then we have

\displaystyle\begin{split}T_{\sigma}^{\mathrm{(l)}{\Lambda_{r2}}(t)V_{{\sigma},{\Lambda_{r2}}(t)}}|_{\pi_{\mathop{\mathrm{bd}}}\left({\mathcal{A}}_{({\Lambda_{l1}})^{c}\cap\mathop{\mathrm{H}_{U}}}\right)^{\prime}}=\mathop{\mathrm{id}}\nolimits|_{\pi_{\mathop{\mathrm{bd}}}\left({\mathcal{A}}_{({\Lambda_{l1}})^{c}\cap\mathop{\mathrm{H}_{U}}}\right)^{\prime}},\end{split}

(3.4)

by (
2.1
).
The Lemma above is a “tailed version” of this.

Let
{\Lambda_{l1}}=\overline{\Lambda_{(a,0),\pi-\varphi,\varphi}}\in{\mathcal{C}}_{{\mathop{\mathrm{bd}}}}^{l}
.
Fix
\varepsilon>0
such that
\arg({\Lambda_{l1}})_{2\varepsilon}\cap\arg({\Lambda_{r2}})_{2\varepsilon}=\emptyset
.
Then there exists a
t_{1}\in{\mathbb{R}}_{+}
such that

\left(\overline{\Lambda_{(a+s,0),\pi-(\varphi+2\varepsilon),(\varphi+2\varepsilon)}}\right)\cap{\Lambda_{r2}}(t)=\emptyset
for all
t,s\in{\mathbb{R}}_{+}
with

t-s\geq t_{1}
.

By Assumption
1.7
, for any
s\geq R^{(l){\mathop{\mathrm{bd}}}}_{\varphi,\varepsilon}
,
we have

\displaystyle{\pi_{\mathop{\mathrm{bd}}}\left({\mathcal{A}}_{({\Lambda_{l1}})^{c}\cap\mathop{\mathrm{H}_{U}}}\right)^{\prime}}\subset_{2f^{(l){\mathop{\mathrm{bd}}}}_{\varphi,\varepsilon,\varepsilon}(s)}\pi_{\mathop{\mathrm{bd}}}\left({\mathcal{A}}_{\overline{\Lambda_{(a+s,0),\pi-(\varphi+2\varepsilon),(\varphi+2\varepsilon)}}}\right)^{\prime\prime}.

(3.5)

(See Appendix
A
for the notation
\subset_{\varepsilon}
).
By Lemma
2.1
, if
\left(\overline{\Lambda_{(a+s,0),\pi-(\varphi+2\varepsilon),(\varphi+2\varepsilon)}}\right)\cap{\Lambda_{r2}}(t)=\emptyset
,
(hence if
t-s\geq t_{1}
)
we have

\displaystyle\left.\left(T_{\sigma}^{\mathrm{(l)}{\Lambda_{r2}}(t)V_{{\sigma},{\Lambda_{r2}}(t)}}-\mathop{\mathrm{id}}\nolimits\right)\right|_{\pi_{\mathop{\mathrm{bd}}}\left({\mathcal{A}}_{\overline{\Lambda_{(a+s,0),\pi-(\varphi+2\varepsilon),(\varphi+2\varepsilon)}}}\right)^{\prime\prime}}=0.

(3.6)

Therefore, if
t-s\geq t_{1}
, we have

\displaystyle\left\|\left.\left(T_{\sigma}^{\mathrm{(l)}{\Lambda_{r2}}(t)V_{{\sigma},{\Lambda_{r2}}(t)}}-\mathop{\mathrm{id}}\nolimits\right)\right|_{{\pi_{\mathop{\mathrm{bd}}}\left({\mathcal{A}}_{({\Lambda_{l1}})^{c}\cap\mathop{\mathrm{H}_{U}}}\right)^{\prime}}}\right\|\leq 4f^{(l){\mathop{\mathrm{bd}}}}_{\varphi,\varepsilon,\varepsilon}(s).

(3.7)

This completes the proof of the first equality.
The proof of the second one is the same.

\square

The following Lemma gives an information on the support of
T_{\rho}^{\mathrm{(l)}\Lambda_{r0}V_{\rho,\Lambda_{0}}}

for
\rho\in O_{\mathop{\mathrm{bd}}}^{\mathop{\mathrm{bu}}}

Lemma 3.3
.

Consider the setting in subsection
1.1
.
Let
\Lambda_{0}\in{\mathcal{C}}_{{\mathop{\mathrm{bk}}}}^{U}
,
\Lambda_{r0}\in{\mathcal{C}}_{{\mathop{\mathrm{bd}}}}^{r}
,
{\Lambda_{l0}}\in{\mathcal{C}}_{{\mathop{\mathrm{bd}}}}^{l}
satisfying

\mathop{\mathrm{H}_{U}}\cap\Lambda_{0}\subset\Lambda_{r0}\cap{\Lambda_{l0}}
.
If
\rho\in O_{\mathop{\mathrm{bd}}}^{\mathop{\mathrm{bu}}}
and
V_{\rho,\Lambda_{0}}\in{\mathcal{V}}_{\rho\Lambda_{0}}^{\mathop{\mathrm{bu}}}\subset{\mathcal{V}}_{\rho\Lambda_{r0}}^{{\mathop{\mathrm{bd}}}U}
then

\displaystyle\left.T_{\rho}^{\mathrm{(l)}\Lambda_{r0}V_{\rho,\Lambda_{0}}}\right|_{\pi_{\mathop{\mathrm{bd}}}\left({\mathcal{A}}_{({\Lambda_{l0}})^{c}\cap\mathop{\mathrm{H}_{U}}}\right)^{\prime\prime}\cap{\mathcal{F}}_{{\mathop{\mathrm{bd}}}}^{U}}=\mathop{\mathrm{id}}\nolimits,

(3.8)

and

\displaystyle\left.T_{\rho}^{\mathrm{(l)}\Lambda_{r0}V_{\rho,\Lambda_{0}}}\pi_{\mathop{\mathrm{bd}}}\right|_{{\mathcal{A}}_{\left(({\Lambda_{l0}})^{c}\cup({\Lambda_{r0}})^{c}\right)\cap\mathop{\mathrm{H}_{U}}}}=\pi_{\mathop{\mathrm{bd}}}.

(3.9)

Remark 3.4
.

See Figure
10
. The pink part is
\Lambda_{r0}
, and the light-blue part is
\Lambda_{l0}
.
The cone
\Lambda_{0}
is the dark-blus part in the intersection of them.
Note that
T_{\rho}^{\mathrm{(l)}\Lambda_{r0}V_{\rho,\Lambda_{0}}}
is not defined on the whole

\pi_{\mathop{\mathrm{bd}}}\left({\mathcal{A}}_{({\Lambda_{l0}})^{c}\cap\mathop{\mathrm{H}_{U}}}\right)^{\prime\prime}
, but it is defined on

{\pi_{\mathop{\mathrm{bd}}}\left({\mathcal{A}}_{({\Lambda_{l0}})^{c}\cap\mathop{\mathrm{H}_{U}}}\right)^{\prime\prime}\cap{\mathcal{F}}_{{\mathop{\mathrm{bd}}}}^{U}}
.

Remark 3.5
.

We cannot replace
{\mathcal{F}}_{{\mathop{\mathrm{bd}}}}^{U}
by
{\mathcal{B}}_{l}
.
In fact this difference between
{\mathcal{B}}_{l}
and
{\mathcal{F}}_{{\mathop{\mathrm{bd}}}}^{U}
is the main point of
Assumption
6.11
.

By interchanging the role of left and right in Lemma
2.1
,
we can define an endomorphism
{T_{\rho}^{\mathrm{(r)}{\Lambda_{l0}}V_{{\rho},\Lambda_{0}}}}
of

{\mathcal{B}}_{r}:=\overline{\cup_{\Lambda_{r}\in{\mathcal{C}}_{{\mathop{\mathrm{bd}}}}^{r}}\pi_{\mathop{\mathrm{bd}}}\left({\mathcal{A}}_{\Lambda_{r}}\right)^{\prime\prime}}^{n}=\overline{\cup_{{\Lambda_{l}}\in{\mathcal{C}}_{{\mathop{\mathrm{bd}}}}^{l}}\pi_{\mathop{\mathrm{bd}}}\left({\mathcal{A}}_{{\Lambda_{l}}^{c}\cap\mathop{\mathrm{H}_{U}}}\right)^{\prime\prime}}

which is
\sigma
-weak continuous on any

\pi_{\mathop{\mathrm{bd}}}({\mathcal{A}}_{{({\Lambda_{l}})^{c}\cap\mathop{\mathrm{H}_{U}}}})^{\prime\prime}
,
{\Lambda_{l}}\in{\mathcal{C}}_{{\mathop{\mathrm{bd}}}}^{l}
, and

\displaystyle{T_{\rho}^{\mathrm{(r)}{\Lambda_{l0}}V_{{\rho},\Lambda_{0}}}}\pi_{\mathop{\mathrm{bd}}}=\mathop{\mathrm{Ad}}\nolimits\left(V_{\rho,\Lambda_{0}}\right)\rho=T_{\rho}^{\mathrm{(l)}\Lambda_{r0}V_{\rho,\Lambda_{0}}}\pi_{\mathop{\mathrm{bd}}}

(3.10)

and

\displaystyle\left.{T_{\rho}^{\mathrm{(r)}{\Lambda_{l0}}V_{{\rho},\Lambda_{0}}}}\right|_{\pi_{\mathop{\mathrm{bd}}}\left({\mathcal{A}}_{({\Lambda_{l0}})^{c}\cap\mathop{\mathrm{H}_{U}}}\right)^{\prime\prime}}=\mathop{\mathrm{id}}\nolimits_{\pi_{\mathop{\mathrm{bd}}}\left({\mathcal{A}}_{({\Lambda_{l0}})^{c}\cap\mathop{\mathrm{H}_{U}}}\right)^{\prime\prime}}.

(3.11)

By the
\sigma
-weak continuity and
(
3.10
), we have

{T_{\rho}^{\mathrm{(r)}{\Lambda_{l0}}V_{{\rho},\Lambda_{0}}}}|_{{\mathcal{F}}_{{\mathop{\mathrm{bd}}}}^{U}}=T_{\rho}^{\mathrm{(l)}\Lambda_{r0}V_{\rho,\Lambda_{0}}}|_{{\mathcal{F}}_{{\mathop{\mathrm{bd}}}}^{U}}
.
Combining this with (
3.11
),
we obtain (
3.8
).

By Lemma
2.1
, we have
T_{\rho}^{\mathrm{(l)}\Lambda_{r0}V_{\rho,\Lambda_{0}}}|_{\pi_{\mathop{\mathrm{bd}}}\left({\mathcal{A}}_{\left(({\Lambda_{r0}})^{c}\right)\cap\mathop{\mathrm{H}_{U}}}\right)}=\mathop{\mathrm{id}}\nolimits
. We also have
T_{\rho}^{\mathrm{(l)}\Lambda_{r0}V_{\rho,\Lambda_{0}}}|_{\pi_{\mathop{\mathrm{bd}}}\left({\mathcal{A}}_{\left(({\Lambda_{l0}})^{c}\right)\cap\mathop{\mathrm{H}_{U}}}\right)}=\mathop{\mathrm{id}}\nolimits
from (
3.8
).
Hence we obtain (
3.9
).

\square

Lemma 3.6
.

Consider the setting in subsection
1.1
, and assume Assumption
1.6
.
Let
({\Lambda_{1}},{\Lambda_{r1}})\in\mathcal{PC}
,
{\Lambda_{r2}}\in{\mathcal{C}}_{{\mathop{\mathrm{bd}}}}^{r}

with
{\Lambda_{1}}\leftarrow_{r}{\Lambda_{r2}}
.
Let
\rho\in O_{\mathop{\mathrm{bd}}}^{\mathop{\mathrm{bu}}}
.
Then the following hold.

(i)

For any
V_{\rho,{\Lambda_{1}}}\in{\mathcal{V}}_{\rho{\Lambda_{1}}}^{\mathop{\mathrm{bu}}}
,

\displaystyle\lim_{t\to\infty}\left\|\left.\left(T_{\rho}^{\mathrm{(l)}{\Lambda_{r1}}V_{\rho,{\Lambda_{1}}}}-\mathop{\mathrm{id}}\nolimits\right)\right|_{\pi_{\mathop{\mathrm{bd}}}\left({\mathcal{A}}_{\left({\Lambda_{r2}}(t)\right)^{c}\cap\mathop{\mathrm{H}_{U}}}\right)^{\prime}\cap{\mathcal{F}}_{{\mathop{\mathrm{bd}}}}^{U}}\right\|=0.

(3.12)

(ii)

For any

V_{\rho,{\Lambda_{1}}(t)}\in{\mathcal{V}}_{\rho{\Lambda_{1}}(t)}^{\mathop{\mathrm{bu}}}
,
t\geq 0

we have

\displaystyle\lim_{t\to\infty}\left\|\left.\left(T_{\rho}^{\mathrm{(l)}{\Lambda_{r1}}V_{\rho,{\Lambda_{1}}(t)}}-\mathop{\mathrm{id}}\nolimits\right)\right|_{{\pi_{\mathop{\mathrm{bd}}}\left({\mathcal{A}}_{\left({\Lambda_{r2}}\right)^{c}\cap\mathop{\mathrm{H}_{U}}}\right)^{\prime}\cap{\mathcal{F}}_{{\mathop{\mathrm{bd}}}}^{U}}}\right\|=0.

(3.13)

Remark 3.7
.

If the Haag duality holds, then we
have

\displaystyle\pi_{\mathop{\mathrm{bd}}}\left({\mathcal{A}}_{\left({\Lambda_{r2}}\right)^{c}\cap\mathop{\mathrm{H}_{U}}}\right)^{\prime}=\pi_{\mathop{\mathrm{bd}}}\left({\mathcal{A}}_{{\Lambda_{r2}}\cap\mathop{\mathrm{H}_{U}}}\right)^{\prime\prime}.

(3.14)

In this case, if
{{\Lambda_{1}}(t)}\cap{\Lambda_{r2}}=\emptyset
(i.e., if
t
is large enough),
then we have

\displaystyle T_{\rho}^{\mathrm{(l)}{\Lambda_{r1}}V_{\rho,{\Lambda_{1}}(t)}}|_{{\pi_{\mathop{\mathrm{bd}}}\left({\mathcal{A}}_{\left({\Lambda_{r2}}\right)^{c}\cap\mathop{\mathrm{H}_{U}}}\right)^{\prime}\cap{\mathcal{F}}_{{\mathop{\mathrm{bd}}}}^{U}}}=\mathop{\mathrm{id}}\nolimits|_{{\pi_{\mathop{\mathrm{bd}}}\left({\mathcal{A}}_{\left({\Lambda_{r2}}\right)^{c}\cap\mathop{\mathrm{H}_{U}}}\right)^{\prime}\cap{\mathcal{F}}_{{\mathop{\mathrm{bd}}}}^{U}}}

(3.15)

from Lemma
3.3
.
The above is its “tailed version”.

The cone
{\Lambda_{r2}}
is of the form
{\Lambda_{r2}}=\Lambda_{(a,0),\varphi,\varphi}
.
Because
{\Lambda_{1}}\leftarrow_{r}{\Lambda_{r2}}
, there exists
\varepsilon>0
such that

\arg\left({\Lambda_{1}}\right)_{2\varepsilon}\cap\arg\left({\Lambda_{r2}}\right)_{2\varepsilon}=\emptyset
.

(i)
Fix
b\in{\mathbb{R}}
large enough so that

{\Lambda_{l}}:=\left(\Lambda_{(b,0),(\varphi+\varepsilon),(\varphi+\varepsilon)}\right)^{c}\cap\mathop{\mathrm{H}_{U}}\in{\mathcal{C}}_{{\mathop{\mathrm{bd}}}}^{l}
satisfies

\Lambda_{1}\subset{\Lambda_{l}}\cap{\Lambda_{r1}}
.
We apply Lemma
3.3
replacing

\Lambda_{0}
,
{\Lambda_{r0}}
,
{\Lambda_{l0}}
,
\rho
,
V_{\rho,\Lambda_{0}}
by

{\Lambda_{1}}
,
{\Lambda_{r1}}
,
{\Lambda_{l}}
,
\rho
,

V_{\rho,{\Lambda_{1}}}\in{\mathcal{V}}_{\rho{\Lambda_{1}}}^{\mathop{\mathrm{bu}}}
respectively.
Then we obtain

\displaystyle\left.T_{\rho}^{\mathrm{(l)}{\Lambda_{r1}}V_{\rho,{\Lambda_{1}}}}\right|_{\pi_{\mathop{\mathrm{bd}}}\left({\mathcal{A}}_{({\Lambda_{l}})^{c}\cap\mathop{\mathrm{H}_{U}}}\right)^{\prime\prime}\cap{\mathcal{F}}_{{\mathop{\mathrm{bd}}}}^{U}}=\mathop{\mathrm{id}}\nolimits.

(3.16)

By Assumption
1.6
, for any
t\geq 2R^{(r)\mathop{\mathrm{bd}}}_{\varphi,\frac{\varepsilon}{2}}
,
there exists
W_{t}\in{\mathcal{U}}({\mathcal{F}}_{{\mathop{\mathrm{bd}}}}^{U})
such that

\displaystyle\pi_{\mathop{\mathrm{bd}}}\left({\mathcal{A}}_{\left({\Lambda_{r2}}(t)\right)^{c}\cap\mathop{\mathrm{H}_{U}}}\right)^{\prime}\subset\mathop{\mathrm{Ad}}\nolimits\left(W_{t}\right)\left(\pi_{\mathop{\mathrm{bd}}}\left({\mathcal{A}}_{\Lambda_{(a+\frac{t}{2},0),\varphi+\varepsilon,\varphi+\varepsilon}}\right)^{\prime\prime}\right),

(3.17)

\displaystyle\left\|W_{t}-\mathbb{I}\right\|\leq{f_{\varphi,\frac{\varepsilon}{2},\frac{\varepsilon}{2}}^{(r){\mathop{\mathrm{bd}}}}\left(\frac{t}{2}\right)}.

(3.18)

If
t\geq\max\{2(b-a),2R^{(r)\mathop{\mathrm{bd}}}_{\varphi,\frac{\varepsilon}{2}}\}
, then

\Lambda_{((a+\frac{t}{2}),0),\varphi+\varepsilon,\varphi+\varepsilon}\subset\Lambda_{(b,0),\varphi+\varepsilon,\varphi+\varepsilon}=({\Lambda_{l}})^{c}\cap\mathop{\mathrm{H}_{U}}
,
and combining with (
3.17
), we have

\displaystyle\begin{split}\pi_{\mathop{\mathrm{bd}}}\left({\mathcal{A}}_{\left({\Lambda_{r2}}(t)\right)^{c}\cap\mathop{\mathrm{H}_{U}}}\right)^{\prime}\subset\mathop{\mathrm{Ad}}\nolimits\left(W_{t}\right)\left(\pi_{\mathop{\mathrm{bd}}}\left({\mathcal{A}}_{({\Lambda_{l}})^{c}\cap\mathop{\mathrm{H}_{U}}}\right)^{\prime\prime}\right).\end{split}

(3.19)

Now for any
t\geq\max\{2(b-a),2R^{(r)\mathop{\mathrm{bd}}}_{\varphi,\frac{\varepsilon}{2}}\}

and
x\in{\pi_{\mathop{\mathrm{bd}}}\left({\mathcal{A}}_{\left({\Lambda_{r2}}(t)\right)^{c}\cap\mathop{\mathrm{H}_{U}}}\right)^{\prime}\cap{\mathcal{F}}_{{\mathop{\mathrm{bd}}}}^{U}}
,
we have
\mathop{\mathrm{Ad}}\nolimits(W_{t}^{*})(x)\in\pi_{\mathop{\mathrm{bd}}}\left({\mathcal{A}}_{({\Lambda_{l}})^{c}\cap\mathop{\mathrm{H}_{U}}}\right)^{\prime\prime}\cap{\mathcal{F}}_{{\mathop{\mathrm{bd}}}}^{U}
and

\displaystyle\begin{split}&\left\|\left(T_{\rho}^{\mathrm{(l)}{\Lambda_{r1}}V_{\rho,{\Lambda_{1}}}}-\mathop{\mathrm{id}}\nolimits\right)(x)\right\|\leq\left\|\left(T_{\rho}^{\mathrm{(l)}{\Lambda_{r1}}V_{\rho,{\Lambda_{1}}}}-\mathop{\mathrm{id}}\nolimits\right)\left(\mathop{\mathrm{Ad}}\nolimits(W_{t}^{*})(x)\right)\right\|+2\left\|x-\mathop{\mathrm{Ad}}\nolimits(W_{t}^{*})(x)\right\|<4f_{\varphi,\frac{\varepsilon}{2},\frac{\varepsilon}{2}}^{(r){\mathop{\mathrm{bd}}}}\left(\frac{t}{2}\right)\left\|x\right\|,\end{split}

(3.20)

using (
3.16
)
This proves (i).

(ii)For each
s\geq R^{(r)\mathop{\mathrm{bd}}}_{\varphi,\frac{\varepsilon}{2}}
,
by Assumption
1.6
, there exists
\tilde{W}_{s}\in{\mathcal{U}}({\mathcal{F}}_{{\mathop{\mathrm{bd}}}}^{U})
such that

\displaystyle\pi_{\mathop{\mathrm{bd}}}\left({\mathcal{A}}_{\left({\Lambda_{r2}}\right)^{c}\cap\mathop{\mathrm{H}_{U}}}\right)^{\prime}\subset\mathop{\mathrm{Ad}}\nolimits\left(\tilde{W}_{s}\right)\left(\pi_{\mathop{\mathrm{bd}}}\left({\mathcal{A}}_{\Lambda_{(a-s,0),\varphi+\varepsilon,\varphi+\varepsilon}}\right)^{\prime\prime}\right),

(3.21)

\displaystyle\left\|\tilde{W}_{s}-\mathbb{I}\right\|\leq{f^{(r){\mathop{\mathrm{bd}}}}_{\varphi,\frac{\varepsilon}{2},\frac{\varepsilon}{2}}(s)}

(3.22)

For each
s\geq 0
, there exists a
t_{0}(s)\geq 0
such that

{\Lambda_{1}}(t)\cap{\Lambda_{(a-s,0),\varphi+\varepsilon,\varphi+\varepsilon}}=\emptyset
for all
t\geq t_{0}(s)
.

Applying Lemma
3.3
replacing

\Lambda_{0}
,
{\Lambda_{r0}}
,
{\Lambda_{l0}}
,
\rho
,
V_{\rho,\Lambda_{0}}
by

{\Lambda_{1}}(t)
,
{\Lambda_{r1}}
,
\left(\Lambda_{(a-s,0),\varphi+\varepsilon,\varphi+\varepsilon}\right)^{c}\cap\mathop{\mathrm{H}_{U}}
,
\rho
,

V_{\rho,{\Lambda_{1}}(t)}\in{\mathcal{V}}_{\rho{\Lambda_{1}}(t)}^{\mathop{\mathrm{bu}}}
respectively, for
t\geq t_{0}(s)
,
we obtain

\displaystyle\begin{split}\left.T_{\rho}^{\mathrm{(l)}{\Lambda_{r1}}V_{\rho,{\Lambda_{1}}(t)}}\right|_{\pi_{\mathop{\mathrm{bd}}}\left({\mathcal{A}}_{{\Lambda_{(a-s,0),\varphi+\varepsilon,\varphi+\varepsilon}}}\right)^{\prime\prime}\cap{\mathcal{F}}_{{\mathop{\mathrm{bd}}}}^{U}}=\mathop{\mathrm{id}}\nolimits.\end{split}

(3.23)

Now for any
x\in{{\pi_{\mathop{\mathrm{bd}}}\left({\mathcal{A}}_{\left({\Lambda_{r2}}\right)^{c}\cap\mathop{\mathrm{H}_{U}}}\right)^{\prime}\cap{\mathcal{F}}_{{\mathop{\mathrm{bd}}}}^{U}}}
,

s\geq R^{(r)\mathop{\mathrm{bd}}}_{\varphi,\frac{\varepsilon}{2}}
and

t\geq t_{0}(s)

and we have
\mathop{\mathrm{Ad}}\nolimits(\tilde{W}_{s}^{*})(x)\in\pi_{\mathop{\mathrm{bd}}}\left({\mathcal{A}}_{\Lambda_{(a-s,0),\varphi+\varepsilon,\varphi+\varepsilon}}\right)^{\prime\prime}\cap{\mathcal{F}}_{{\mathop{\mathrm{bd}}}}^{U}
and

\displaystyle\begin{split}&\left\|\left(T_{\rho}^{\mathrm{(l)}{\Lambda_{r1}}V_{\rho,{\Lambda_{1}}(t)}}-\mathop{\mathrm{id}}\nolimits\right)(x)\right\|\\
&\leq\left\|\left(T_{\rho}^{\mathrm{(l)}{\Lambda_{r1}}V_{\rho,{\Lambda_{1}}(t)}}-\mathop{\mathrm{id}}\nolimits\right)\left(\mathop{\mathrm{Ad}}\nolimits(\tilde{W}_{s}^{*})(x)\right)\right\|+2\left\|\mathop{\mathrm{Ad}}\nolimits(\tilde{W}_{s}^{*})(x)-x\right\|\leq{4f^{(r){\mathop{\mathrm{bd}}}}_{\varphi,\frac{\varepsilon}{2},\frac{\varepsilon}{2}}(s)}\left\|x\right\|.\end{split}

(3.24)

Hence for any
s\geq R^{(r)\mathop{\mathrm{bd}}}_{\varphi,\frac{\varepsilon}{2}}
and

t\geq t_{0}(s)
,
we have

\left\|\left(T_{\rho}^{\mathrm{(l)}{\Lambda_{r1}}V_{\rho,{\Lambda_{1}}(t)}}-\mathop{\mathrm{id}}\nolimits\right)|_{{{\pi_{\mathop{\mathrm{bd}}}\left({\mathcal{A}}_{\left({\Lambda_{r2}}\right)^{c}\cap\mathop{\mathrm{H}_{U}}}\right)^{\prime}\cap{\mathcal{F}}_{{\mathop{\mathrm{bd}}}}^{U}}}}\right\|\leq{4f^{(r){\mathop{\mathrm{bd}}}}_{\varphi,\frac{\varepsilon}{2},\frac{\varepsilon}{2}}(s)}
,
and this proves (ii).

\square

Using the Lemmas we obtained, we obtain the following.

Lemma 3.8
.

Consider the setting in subsection
1.1
.
Assume Assumption
1.6
and Assumption
1.7
.
Let
\rho,\rho^{\prime}\in O_{\mathop{\mathrm{bd}}}^{\mathop{\mathrm{bu}}}
,
\sigma,\sigma^{\prime}\in O^{rU}_{\mathop{\mathrm{bd}}}
.
Let
{\Lambda_{1}},{\Lambda_{1}}^{\prime}\in{\mathcal{C}}_{{\mathop{\mathrm{bk}}}}^{U}
,
{\Lambda_{r2}},{\Lambda_{r2}}^{\prime}\in{\mathcal{C}}_{{\mathop{\mathrm{bd}}}}^{r}

with
\{{\Lambda_{1}},{\Lambda_{1}}^{\prime}\}\leftarrow_{r}\{{\Lambda_{r2}},{\Lambda_{r2}}^{\prime}\}
.
Let
{\Lambda_{r1}},{\Lambda_{r1}}^{\prime}\in{\mathcal{C}}_{{\mathop{\mathrm{bd}}}}^{r}
with

({\Lambda_{1}},{\Lambda_{r1}})\in\mathcal{PC}
,
({\Lambda_{1}}^{\prime},{\Lambda_{r1}}^{\prime})\in\mathcal{PC}
.
Let
V_{\rho,{\Lambda_{1}}(t_{1})}\in{\mathcal{V}}_{\rho{\Lambda_{1}}(t_{1})}^{\mathop{\mathrm{bu}}}\subset{\mathcal{V}}_{\rho{\Lambda_{r1}}}^{{\mathop{\mathrm{bd}}}}
,

V_{\rho^{\prime},{{\Lambda_{1}}}^{\prime}(t_{1}^{\prime})}\in{\mathcal{V}}_{\rho^{\prime}{{\Lambda_{1}}}^{\prime}(t_{1}^{\prime})}^{\mathop{\mathrm{bu}}}\subset{\mathcal{V}}_{\rho{\Lambda_{r1}}^{\prime}}^{{\mathop{\mathrm{bd}}}}
,

V_{\sigma,{\Lambda_{r2}}(t_{2})}\in{\mathcal{V}}_{\sigma{\Lambda_{r2}}(t_{2})}^{{\mathop{\mathrm{bd}}}U}
,

V_{\sigma^{\prime},{\Lambda_{r2}}^{\prime}(t_{2}^{\prime})}\in{\mathcal{V}}_{\sigma^{\prime}{\Lambda_{r2}}^{\prime}(t_{2}^{\prime})}^{{\mathop{\mathrm{bd}}}U}
,
for
t_{1},t_{1}^{\prime},t_{2},t_{2}^{\prime}\geq 0
.
Then for any

\displaystyle\begin{split}S_{1}^{(t_{1},t_{1}^{\prime})}\in\left(T_{\rho}^{\mathrm{(l)}{\Lambda_{r1}}V_{\rho,{\Lambda_{1}}(t_{1})}},T_{\rho^{\prime}}^{\mathrm{(l)}{\Lambda_{r1}}^{\prime}V_{\rho^{\prime},{\Lambda_{1}}^{\prime}(t_{1}^{\prime})}}\right)_{U},\\
S_{2}^{(t_{2},t_{2}^{\prime})}\in\left(T_{\sigma}^{\mathrm{(l)}{\Lambda_{r2}}V_{\sigma,{\Lambda_{r2}}(t_{2})}},T_{\sigma^{\prime}}^{\mathrm{(l)}{\Lambda_{r2}}^{\prime}V_{\sigma^{\prime},{\Lambda_{r2}}^{\prime}(t_{2}^{\prime})}}\right)_{U}\end{split}

(3.25)

with
\left\|S_{1}^{(t_{1},t_{1}^{\prime})}\right\|,\left\|S_{2}^{(t_{2},t_{2}^{\prime})}\right\|\leq 1
,
we have

\displaystyle\left\|S_{1}^{(t_{1},t_{1}^{\prime})}\otimes S_{2}^{(t_{2},t_{2}^{\prime})}-S_{2}^{(t_{2},t_{2}^{\prime})}\otimes S_{1}^{(t_{1},t_{1}^{\prime})}\right\|\to 0,\quad t_{1},t_{2},t_{1}^{\prime},t_{2}^{\prime}\to\infty.

(3.26)

Remark 3.9
.

Assume the Haag duality to hold. Suppose that there are cones

\Lambda\in{\mathcal{C}}_{{\mathop{\mathrm{bk}}}}^{U}

{\Lambda_{l}}\in{\mathcal{C}}_{{\mathop{\mathrm{bd}}}}^{l}
and
{\Lambda_{r}}\in{\mathcal{C}}_{{\mathop{\mathrm{bd}}}}^{r}
such that
{\Lambda_{1}},{\Lambda_{1}}^{\prime}\subset\Lambda\subset{\Lambda_{l}}
,

{\Lambda_{r2}},{\Lambda_{r2}}^{\prime}\subset{\Lambda_{r}}
and
{\Lambda_{l}}\cap{\Lambda_{r}}=\emptyset
.
If
\rho\in O_{\mathop{\mathrm{bd}{\Lambda_{1}}}}^{\mathop{\mathrm{bu}}}
,
\rho^{\prime}\in O_{\mathop{\mathrm{bd}{\Lambda_{1}}^{\prime}}}^{\mathop{\mathrm{bu}}}
,

\sigma\in O^{rU}_{\mathop{\mathrm{bd}{\Lambda_{r2}}}}
,
\sigma^{\prime}\in O^{rU}_{\mathop{\mathrm{bd}{\Lambda_{r2}}^{\prime}}}

then for any

\displaystyle\begin{split}S_{1}\in(\rho,\rho^{\prime})_{U}\subset\pi_{\mathop{\mathrm{bd}}}({\mathcal{A}}_{{\Lambda_{l}}^{c}})^{\prime}=\pi_{\mathop{\mathrm{bd}}}({\mathcal{A}}_{{\Lambda_{l}}})^{\prime\prime},\\
S_{2}\in(\sigma,\sigma^{\prime})_{U}\subset\pi_{\mathop{\mathrm{bd}}}({\mathcal{A}}_{{\Lambda_{r}}^{c}})^{\prime}\cap{\mathcal{F}}_{{\mathop{\mathrm{bd}}}}^{U}=\pi_{\mathop{\mathrm{bd}}}({\mathcal{A}}_{{\Lambda_{r}}})^{\prime\prime}\cap{\mathcal{F}}_{{\mathop{\mathrm{bd}}}}^{U},\end{split}

(3.27)

we have

\displaystyle S_{1}\otimes S_{2}-S_{2}\otimes S_{1}=S_{1}T_{\rho}^{\mathrm{(l)}{\Lambda_{r1}}\mathbb{I}}(S_{2})-S_{2}T_{\sigma}^{\mathrm{(l)}{\Lambda_{r2}}\mathbb{I}}(S_{1})=S_{1}S_{2}-S_{2}S_{1}=0,

(3.28)

by Lemma
3.3
, Lemma
2.1
.
This Lemma is a “tailed version ” of this.

By the assumption
\{{\Lambda_{1}},{\Lambda_{1}}^{\prime}\}\leftarrow_{r}\{{\Lambda_{r2}},{\Lambda_{r2}}^{\prime}\}
,
there exist an
\varepsilon>0
and
{{\hat{\Lambda}_{1}}}\in{\mathcal{C}}_{{\mathop{\mathrm{bk}}}}^{U}
,
{{\hat{\Lambda}_{r2}}}\in{\mathcal{C}}_{{\mathop{\mathrm{bd}}}}^{r}

such that

\displaystyle\begin{split}&\arg\left({\Lambda_{1}}\right)_{\varepsilon}\cup\arg\left({\Lambda_{1}}^{\prime}\right)_{\varepsilon}\subset\arg{{\hat{\Lambda}_{1}}},\quad\arg\left({\Lambda_{r2}}\right)_{\varepsilon}\cup\arg\left({\Lambda_{r2}}^{\prime}\right)_{\varepsilon}\subset\arg{{\hat{\Lambda}_{r2}}},\\
&({\hat{\Lambda}_{1}})_{\varepsilon}\cap({\hat{\Lambda}_{r2}})_{\varepsilon}=\emptyset,\quad\arg({\hat{\Lambda}_{1}})_{\varepsilon}\cap\arg({\hat{\Lambda}_{r2}})_{\varepsilon}=\emptyset,\\
&{{\hat{\Lambda}_{1}}}\subset\mathop{\mathrm{H}_{U}},\quad|\arg{\hat{\Lambda}_{1}}|+4\varepsilon<2\pi.\end{split}

(3.29)

By Lemma A.2 of
[
26
]
, for any
t\geq 0
fixed, there exists
t_{0}(t)\geq 0

such that

\displaystyle{\Lambda_{1}}(t_{1}),{\Lambda_{1}}^{\prime}(t_{1}^{\prime})\subset{\hat{\Lambda}_{1}}(t),\quad t_{1},t_{1}^{\prime}\geq t_{0}(t).

(3.30)

Similarly, for
t\geq 0
fixed, there exists
\tilde{t}_{0}(t)\geq 0
such that

\displaystyle{\Lambda_{r2}}(t_{2}),{\Lambda_{r2}}^{\prime}({t_{2}^{\prime}})\subset{\hat{\Lambda}_{r2}}(t),\quad t_{2},t_{2}^{\prime}\geq\tilde{t}_{0}(t).

(3.31)

By Lemma
3.3
(
3.9
), for any
t\geq 0
,
t_{1},t_{1}^{\prime}\geq t_{0}(t)
,

T_{\rho}^{\mathrm{(l)}{\Lambda_{r1}}V_{\rho,{\Lambda_{1}}(t_{1})}}\pi_{\mathop{\mathrm{bd}}}

and
T_{\rho^{\prime}}^{\mathrm{(l)}{\Lambda_{r1}}^{\prime}V_{\rho^{\prime},{\Lambda_{1}}^{\prime}(t_{1}^{\prime})}}\pi_{\mathop{\mathrm{bd}}}

are
\pi_{\mathop{\mathrm{bd}}}
on
{\mathcal{A}}_{({\hat{\Lambda}_{1}}(t))^{c}\cap\mathop{\mathrm{H}_{U}}}
.
Therefore,
as in Lemma 4.2 of
[
26
]
, we have

\displaystyle\begin{split}S_{1}^{(t_{1},t_{1}^{\prime})}\in\left(T_{\rho}^{\mathrm{(l)}{\Lambda_{r1}}V_{\rho,{\Lambda_{1}}(t_{1})}},T_{\rho^{\prime}}^{\mathrm{(l)}{\Lambda_{r1}}^{\prime}V_{\rho^{\prime},{\Lambda_{1}}^{\prime}(t_{1}^{\prime})}}\right)_{U}\subset\pi_{\mathop{\mathrm{bd}}}\left({\mathcal{A}}_{({\hat{\Lambda}_{1}}(t))^{c}\cap\mathop{\mathrm{H}_{U}}}\right)^{\prime}\cap{\mathcal{F}}_{{\mathop{\mathrm{bd}}}}^{U}\end{split}

(3.32)

for any
t\geq 0
,
t_{1},t_{1}^{\prime}\geq t_{0}(t)
.
Similarly, we have

\displaystyle S_{2}^{(t_{2},t_{2}^{\prime})}\in\left(T_{\sigma}^{\mathrm{(l)}{\Lambda_{r2}}V_{\sigma,{\Lambda_{r2}}(t_{2})}},T_{\sigma^{\prime}}^{\mathrm{(l)}{\Lambda_{r2}}^{\prime}V_{\sigma^{\prime},{\Lambda_{r2}}^{\prime}(t_{2}^{\prime})}}\right)_{U}\subset\pi_{\mathop{\mathrm{bd}}}\left({\mathcal{A}}_{({\hat{\Lambda}_{r2}}(t))^{c}\cap\mathop{\mathrm{H}_{U}}}\right)^{\prime}\cap{\mathcal{F}}_{{\mathop{\mathrm{bd}}}}^{U},

(3.33)

for any
t\geq 0
,
t_{2},t_{2}^{\prime}\geq\tilde{t}_{0}(t)
.
As in the proof of Lemma
3.6
(
3.17
),
by Assumption
1.6

there exists
T_{0}\geq 0
such that for any
t\geq T_{0}

we have

\displaystyle\begin{split}&\pi_{\mathop{\mathrm{bd}}}\left({\mathcal{A}}_{\left({\hat{\Lambda}_{r2}}(t)\right)^{c}\cap\mathop{\mathrm{H}_{U}}}\right)^{\prime}\subset\mathop{\mathrm{Ad}}\nolimits\left(W_{t}\right)\left(\pi_{\mathop{\mathrm{bd}}}\left({\mathcal{A}}_{\left(\left({\hat{\Lambda}_{r2}}\right)_{\varepsilon}\cap\mathop{\mathrm{H}_{U}}\right)+\frac{t}{2}\bm{e}_{1}}\right)^{\prime\prime}\right),\end{split}

(3.34)

with some
W_{t}\in{\mathcal{U}}({\mathcal{F}}_{{\mathop{\mathrm{bd}}}}^{U})
such that
\lim_{t\to\infty}\left\|W_{t}-\mathbb{I}\right\|=0
.
Note that

\left(\left({\hat{\Lambda}_{r2}}\right)_{\varepsilon}\cap\mathop{\mathrm{H}_{U}}\right)+\frac{t}{2}\bm{e}_{1}\subset({\hat{\Lambda}_{1}}(t))^{c}\cap\mathop{\mathrm{H}_{U}}

for any
t\geq 0
.
Therefore, from (
3.32
), we have

\displaystyle\begin{split}&\left\|\left[S_{1}^{(t_{1},t_{1}^{\prime})},S_{2}^{(t_{2},t_{2}^{\prime})}\right]\right\|\leq 4\left\|W_{t}-\mathbb{I}\right\|\end{split}

(3.35)

for any
t\geq 0
,
t_{1},t_{1}^{\prime}\geq t_{0}(t)
and
t_{2},t_{2}^{\prime}\geq\tilde{t}_{0}(t)
.
By Lemma
3.1
and (
3.32
), we have

\displaystyle\begin{split}\lim_{t_{1},t_{1}^{\prime},t_{2}\to\infty}\left\|\left(T_{\sigma}^{\mathrm{(l)}{\Lambda_{r2}}(t_{2})V_{{\sigma},{\Lambda_{r2}}(t_{2})}}-\mathop{\mathrm{id}}\nolimits\right)\left(S_{1}^{(t_{1},t_{1}^{\prime})}\right)\right\|=0.\end{split}

(3.36)

By Lemma
3.6
and (
3.33
), we have

\displaystyle\begin{split}\lim_{t_{2},t_{2}^{\prime},t_{1}\to\infty}\left\|\left(T_{\rho}^{\mathrm{(l)}{\Lambda_{r1}}V_{\rho,{\Lambda_{1}}(t_{1})}}-\mathop{\mathrm{id}}\nolimits\right)\left(S_{2}^{(t_{2},t_{2}^{\prime})}\right)\right\|=0.\end{split}

(3.37)

Combining them we obtain

\displaystyle\begin{split}&\lim_{t_{1},t_{1}^{\prime},t_{2},t_{2}^{\prime}\to\infty}\left\|S_{1}^{(t_{1},t_{1}^{\prime})}\otimes S_{2}^{(t_{2},t_{2}^{\prime})}-S_{2}^{(t_{2},t_{2}^{\prime})}\otimes S_{1}^{(t_{1},t_{1}^{\prime})}\right\|\\
&=\lim_{t_{1},t_{1}^{\prime},t_{2},t_{2}^{\prime}\to\infty}\left\|S_{1}^{(t_{1},t_{1}^{\prime})}T_{\rho}^{\mathrm{(l)}{\Lambda_{r1}}V_{\rho,{\Lambda_{1}}(t_{1})}}\left(S_{2}^{(t_{2},t_{2}^{\prime})}\right)-S_{2}^{(t_{2},t_{2}^{\prime})}T_{\sigma}^{\mathrm{(l)}{\Lambda_{r2}}(t_{2})V_{{\sigma},{\Lambda_{r2}}(t_{2})}}\left(S_{1}^{(t_{1},t_{1}^{\prime})}\right)\right\|\\
&=\lim_{t_{1},t_{1}^{\prime},t_{2},t_{2}^{\prime}\to\infty}\left\|\left[S_{1}^{(t_{1},t_{1}^{\prime})},S_{2}^{(t_{2},t_{2}^{\prime})}\right]\right\|=0.\end{split}

(3.38)

\square

The condition of cones in the previous Lemma can be relaxed.

Lemma 3.10
.

The statement of Lemma
3.8
holds even when
we replace the condition
\{{\Lambda_{1}},{\Lambda_{1}}^{\prime}\}\leftarrow_{r}\{{\Lambda_{r2}},{\Lambda_{r2}}^{\prime}\}
,
by the condition
{\Lambda_{1}}\leftarrow_{r}{\Lambda_{r2}}
,
{\Lambda_{1}}^{\prime}\leftarrow_{r}{\Lambda_{r2}}^{\prime}
.

Note that analogous to Lemma 4.9 of
[
26
]
, there are sequences of cones
\{{\Lambda_{1}}^{(i)}\}_{i=0}^{3}\subset{\mathcal{C}}_{{\mathop{\mathrm{bk}}}}^{U}
,

\{{\Lambda_{r2}}^{(i)}\}_{i=0}^{3}\subset{\mathcal{C}}_{{\mathop{\mathrm{bd}}}}^{r}
such that

\{{\Lambda_{1}}^{(i)},{\Lambda_{1}}^{(i+1)}\}\leftarrow_{r}\{{\Lambda_{r2}}^{(i)},{\Lambda_{r2}}^{(i+1)}\}
,
i=0,\ldots,2

and
{\Lambda_{1}}^{(0)}={\Lambda_{1}}
,
{\Lambda_{r2}}^{(0)}={\Lambda_{2r}}
,

{\Lambda_{1}}^{(3)}=\Lambda_{\bm{0},\frac{\pi}{2},\frac{\pi}{64}}
,

{\Lambda_{r2}}^{(3)}=\Lambda_{(0,0),\frac{\pi}{64},\frac{\pi}{64}}
.
Then the rest is the same as Lemma 4.10 of
[
26
]
.

\square

Noe we are ready to introduce a half-braiding.

Proposition 3.11
.

Consider the setting in subsection
1.1
and assume Assumption
1.6
,
1.7
.
Let
(\Lambda_{0},\Lambda_{r0})\in\mathcal{PC}
, and
\rho\in O_{\mathop{\mathrm{bd}},\Lambda_{0}}^{\mathop{\mathrm{bu}}}
,
\sigma\in O^{rU}_{\mathop{\mathrm{bd},\Lambda_{r0}}}
.
Then the norm limit

\displaystyle\begin{split}\iota^{(\Lambda_{0},\Lambda_{r0})}\left(\rho:\sigma\right):=\lim_{t_{1},t_{2}\to\infty}\left(V_{\sigma,{\Lambda_{r2}}(t_{2})}\otimes V_{\rho,{\Lambda_{1}}(t_{1})}\right)^{*}\left(V_{\rho,{\Lambda_{1}}(t_{1})}\otimes V_{\sigma,{\Lambda_{r2}}(t_{2})}\right)\in{\mathcal{F}}_{{\mathop{\mathrm{bd}}}}^{U}\end{split}

(3.39)

exists for any

{\Lambda_{1}}\in{\mathcal{C}}_{{\mathop{\mathrm{bk}}}}^{U}
,
{\Lambda_{r2}}\in{\mathcal{C}}_{{\mathop{\mathrm{bd}}}}^{r}

with
{\Lambda_{1}}\leftarrow_{r}{\Lambda_{r2}}
,
and
V_{\rho,{\Lambda_{1}}(t_{1})}\in{\mathcal{V}}_{\rho{\Lambda_{1}}(t_{1})}^{\mathop{\mathrm{bu}}}
,
t_{1}\geq 0
,

V_{\sigma,{\Lambda_{r2}}(t_{2})}\in{\mathcal{V}}_{\sigma{\Lambda_{r2}}(t_{2})}^{{\mathop{\mathrm{bd}}}U}
,
t_{2}\geq 0
,
and its value is independent of the choice of

{\Lambda_{1}}\in{\mathcal{C}}_{{\mathop{\mathrm{bk}}}}^{U}
,
{\Lambda_{r2}}\in{\mathcal{C}}_{{\mathop{\mathrm{bd}}}}^{r}

with
{\Lambda_{1}}\leftarrow_{r}{\Lambda_{r2}}
,
and
V_{\rho,{\Lambda_{1}}(t_{1})}\in{\mathcal{V}}_{\rho{\Lambda_{1}}(t_{1})}^{\mathop{\mathrm{bu}}}
,
t_{1}\geq 0
,

V_{\sigma,{\Lambda_{r2}}(t_{2})}\in{\mathcal{V}}_{\sigma{\Lambda_{r2}}(t_{2})}^{{\mathop{\mathrm{bd}}}U}
,
t_{2}\geq 0
.
Furthermore, we have

\displaystyle\iota^{(\Lambda_{0},\Lambda_{r0})}\left(\rho:\sigma\right)=\lim_{t_{2}\to\infty}\left(V_{\sigma,{\Lambda_{r2}}(t_{2})}\right)^{*}T_{\rho}^{\mathrm{(l)}\Lambda_{r0}\mathbb{I}}\left(V_{\sigma,{\Lambda_{r2}}(t_{2})}\right).

(3.40)

for any

{\Lambda_{r2}}\in{\mathcal{C}}_{{\mathop{\mathrm{bd}}}}^{r}
with
\Lambda_{0}\leftarrow_{r}{\Lambda_{r2}}

and

V_{\sigma,{\Lambda_{r2}}(t_{2})}\in{\mathcal{V}}_{\sigma{\Lambda_{r2}}(t_{2})}^{{\mathop{\mathrm{bd}}}U}
,
t_{2}\geq 0
.

Remark 3.12
.

From (
3.40
), what we are doing here can be understood as follows.
Recall that
V_{\sigma,{\Lambda_{r2}}(t_{2})}
sends
\sigma
particle from

\Lambda_{r0}
to “right infinity ”
{\Lambda_{r2}}(t_{2})
, as
t_{2}\to\infty
.
(For example, recall in Toric code
[
28
]
(Example
1.2
),
a transporter
V_{\pi_{\gamma^{\prime}}^{Z}\Lambda_{r2}(t_{2})}
is given
as a weak-limit of operators acting along the paths
\gamma_{n}
depicted in Figure

12
.)
When we do so, our
\sigma
particle may cross the string creating the
\rho
-particle.
What we are measuring with
\iota^{(\Lambda_{0},\Lambda_{r0})}\left(\rho:\sigma\right)

is the braiding which is caused at the cross.
Note that the limit was not needed in
[
28
]
to define the half-braiding.
This is because in Toric code, the Haag duality holds, and

\left(V_{\sigma,{\Lambda_{r2}}(t_{2})}\right)^{*}T_{\rho}^{\mathrm{(l)}\Lambda_{r0}\mathbb{I}}\left(V_{\sigma,{\Lambda_{r2}}(t_{2})}\right)
does not depend on
t_{2}
if it is large enough.

The first part is identical to the proof of Lemma 4.11
[
26
]
.
Because
V_{\sigma,{\Lambda_{r2}}(t_{2})}
etc belong to
{\mathcal{F}}_{{\mathop{\mathrm{bd}}}}^{U}
, by Lemma
2.1
,

\iota^{(\Lambda_{0},\Lambda_{r0})}\left(\rho:\sigma\right)
belongs to
{\mathcal{F}}_{{\mathop{\mathrm{bd}}}}^{U}
.

For any

{\Lambda_{r2}}\in{\mathcal{C}}_{{\mathop{\mathrm{bd}}}}^{r}
with
\Lambda_{0}\leftarrow_{r}{\Lambda_{r2}}

and

V_{\sigma,{\Lambda_{r2}}(t_{2})}\in{\mathcal{V}}_{\sigma{\Lambda_{r2}}(t_{2})}^{{\mathop{\mathrm{bd}}}U}
,
t_{2}\geq 0
,
set
{\Lambda_{1}}:=\Lambda_{0}\in{\mathcal{C}}_{{\mathop{\mathrm{bk}}}}^{U}
, and take any
V_{\rho,{\Lambda_{1}}(t_{1})}\in{\mathcal{V}}_{\rho{\Lambda_{1}}(t_{1})}^{\mathop{\mathrm{bu}}}
,
t_{1}\geq 0
.
Then we have

\displaystyle\begin{split}&\left(V_{\sigma,{\Lambda_{r2}}(t_{2})}\otimes V_{\rho,{\Lambda_{1}}(t_{1})}\right)^{*}\left(V_{\rho,{\Lambda_{1}}(t_{1})}\otimes V_{\sigma,{\Lambda_{r2}}(t_{2})}\right)\\
&=\left(V_{\sigma,{\Lambda_{r2}}(t_{2})}\right)^{*}T_{\sigma}^{\mathrm{(l)}{\Lambda_{r2}}(t_{2})V_{{\sigma},{\Lambda_{r2}}(t_{2})}}\left(V_{\rho,{\Lambda_{1}}(t_{1})}^{*}\right)V_{\rho,{\Lambda_{1}}(t_{1})}T_{\rho}^{\mathrm{(l)}\Lambda_{0}\mathbb{I}}\left(V_{\sigma,{\Lambda_{r2}}(t_{2})}\right).\end{split}

(3.41)

Because
{\Lambda_{1}}:=\Lambda_{0}
, we have

V_{\rho,{\Lambda_{1}}(t_{1})}\in\pi_{\mathop{\mathrm{bd}}}\left({\mathcal{A}}_{\Lambda_{0}^{c}\cap\mathop{\mathrm{H}_{U}}}\right)^{\prime}\cap{\mathcal{F}}_{{\mathop{\mathrm{bd}}}}^{U}
.
Because of
\Lambda_{0}\leftarrow_{r}{\Lambda_{r2}}
, from Lemma
3.1
, we have

T_{\sigma}^{\mathrm{(l)}{\Lambda_{r2}}(t_{2})V_{{\sigma},{\Lambda_{r2}}(t_{2})}}\left(V_{\rho,{\Lambda_{1}}(t_{1})}^{*}\right)V_{\rho,{\Lambda_{1}}(t_{1})}\to\mathbb{I}
,
t_{2}\to 0
in norm, uniformly in
t_{1}\geq 0
.
Hence we obtain (
3.40
).

\square

Figure 12:

The proof of the following Lemma, stating that
\iota^{(\Lambda_{0},\Lambda_{r0})}\left(\rho:\sigma\right)
is a
{\mathcal{F}}_{{\mathop{\mathrm{bd}}}}^{U}
-valued
morphism from
\rho\otimes\sigma
to
\sigma\otimes\rho

is identical to that of Lemma 4.13
[

.

Lemma 3.13
.

Consider the setting in subsection
1.1
and assume Assumption
1.6
,
1.7
.
Let
\rho\in O_{\mathop{\mathrm{bd}},\Lambda_{0}}^{\mathop{\mathrm{bu}}}
,
\sigma\in O^{rU}_{\mathop{\mathrm{bd},\Lambda_{r0}}}
and
(\Lambda_{0},\Lambda_{r0})\in\mathcal{PC}
.
Then we have

\iota^{(\Lambda_{0},\Lambda_{r0})}\left(\rho:\sigma\right)\in\left(T_{\rho}^{\mathrm{(l)}\Lambda_{r0}\mathbb{I}}T_{\sigma}^{\mathrm{(l)}\Lambda_{r0}\mathbb{I}},T_{\sigma}^{\mathrm{(l)}\Lambda_{r0}\mathbb{I}}T_{\rho}^{\mathrm{(l)}\Lambda_{r0}\mathbb{I}}\right)_{U}.

The proof of the following Lemma about the naturality of
\iota^{(\Lambda_{0},\Lambda_{r0})}
in the second variable
is identical to that of Lemma 4.14
[
26
]
,
using Lemma
3.10
.

Lemma 3.14
.

Consider the setting in subsection
1.1
and assume Assumption
1.6
,
1.7
.
Let
(\Lambda_{0},\Lambda_{r0})\in\mathcal{PC}
and
\rho\in O_{\mathop{\mathrm{bd}},\Lambda_{0}}^{\mathop{\mathrm{bu}}}
,
\sigma,\sigma^{\prime}\in O^{rU}_{\mathop{\mathrm{bd},\Lambda_{r0}}}
.
Then for any

S\in\left(T_{\sigma}^{\mathrm{(l)}\Lambda_{r0}\mathbb{I}},T_{\sigma^{\prime}}^{\mathrm{(l)}\Lambda_{r0}\mathbb{I}}\right)_{U}
,
we have

\displaystyle\iota^{(\Lambda_{0},\Lambda_{r0})}\left(\rho:\sigma^{\prime}\right)\left(\mathbb{I}_{\rho}\otimes S\right)=\left(S\otimes\mathbb{I}_{\rho}\right)\iota^{(\Lambda_{0},\Lambda_{r0})}\left(\rho:\sigma\right).

(3.42)

Remark 3.15
.

Note that
S
is required to be in
{\mathcal{F}}_{{\mathop{\mathrm{bd}}}}^{U}
, not
{\mathcal{B}}_{l}
.

For the first variable, the naturality holds for a wider class, i.e.,
{\mathcal{B}}_{l}
.

Lemma 3.16
.

Consider the setting in subsection
1.1
and assume Assumption
1.6
,
1.7
.
Let
(\Lambda_{0},\Lambda_{r0})\in\mathcal{PC}
and
\rho,\rho^{\prime}\in O_{\mathop{\mathrm{bd}},\Lambda_{0}}^{\mathop{\mathrm{bu}}}
,
\sigma\in O^{rU}_{\mathop{\mathrm{bd},\Lambda_{r0}}}
.
Then for any
R\in\left(T_{\rho}^{\mathrm{(l)}\Lambda_{r0}\mathbb{I}},T_{\rho^{\prime}}^{\mathrm{(l)}\Lambda_{r0}\mathbb{I}}\right)_{l}
,
we have

\displaystyle\iota^{(\Lambda_{0},\Lambda_{r0})}\left(\rho^{\prime}:\sigma\right)\left(R\otimes\mathbb{I}_{\sigma}\right)=\left(\mathbb{I}_{\sigma}\otimes R\right)\iota^{(\Lambda_{0},\Lambda_{r0})}\left(\rho:\sigma\right).

(3.43)

For any

{\Lambda_{1}}\in{\mathcal{C}}_{{\mathop{\mathrm{bk}}}}^{U}
,
{\Lambda_{r2}}\in{\mathcal{C}}_{{\mathop{\mathrm{bd}}}}^{r}

with
{\Lambda_{1}}\leftarrow_{r}{\Lambda_{r2}}
,

\displaystyle\begin{split}&\left\|\iota^{(\Lambda_{0},\Lambda_{r0})}\left(\rho^{\prime}:\sigma\right)\left(R\otimes\mathbb{I}_{\sigma}\right)-\left(\mathbb{I}_{\sigma}\otimes R\right)\iota^{(\Lambda_{0},\Lambda_{r0})}\left(\rho:\sigma\right)\right\|\\
&=\lim_{t_{1},t_{2}\to\infty}\left\|\begin{gathered}\left(V_{\sigma,{\Lambda_{r2}}(t_{2})}\otimes V_{\rho^{\prime},{\Lambda_{1}}(t_{1})}\right)^{*}\left(V_{\rho^{\prime},{\Lambda_{1}}(t_{1})}\otimes V_{\sigma,{\Lambda_{r2}}(t_{2})}\right)\left(R\otimes\mathbb{I}_{\sigma}\right)\\
-\left(\mathbb{I}_{\sigma}\otimes R\right)\left(V_{\sigma,{\Lambda_{r2}}(t_{2})}\otimes V_{\rho,{\Lambda_{1}}(t_{1})}\right)^{*}\left(V_{\rho,{\Lambda_{1}}(t_{1})}\otimes V_{\sigma,{\Lambda_{r2}}(t_{2})}\right)\end{gathered}\right\|\\
&=\lim_{t_{1},t_{2}\to\infty}\left\|\begin{gathered}V_{\rho^{\prime},{\Lambda_{1}}(t_{1})}R\left(V_{\rho,{\Lambda_{1}}(t_{1})}\right)^{*}\otimes V_{\sigma,{\Lambda_{r2}}(t_{2})}\mathbb{I}_{\sigma}\left(V_{\sigma,{\Lambda_{r2}}(t_{2})}\right)^{*}\\
-V_{\sigma,{\Lambda_{r2}}(t_{2})}\mathbb{I}_{\sigma}\left(V_{\sigma,{\Lambda_{r2}}(t_{2})}\right)^{*}\otimes V_{\rho^{\prime},{\Lambda_{1}}(t_{1})}RV_{\rho,{\Lambda_{1}}(t_{1})}^{*}\end{gathered}\right\|\\
&=\lim_{t_{1},t_{2}\to\infty}\left\|\begin{gathered}V_{\rho^{\prime},{\Lambda_{1}}(t_{1})}R\left(V_{\rho,{\Lambda_{1}}(t_{1})}\right)^{*}-T_{\sigma}^{\mathrm{(l)}{\Lambda_{r2}}(t_{2})V_{{\sigma},{\Lambda_{r2}}(t_{2})}}\left(V_{\rho^{\prime},{\Lambda_{1}}(t_{1})}RV_{\rho,{\Lambda_{1}}(t_{1})}^{*}\right)\end{gathered}\right\|\\
&=0.\end{split}

(3.44)

At the last equality, we used the fact

\displaystyle\begin{split}V_{\rho^{\prime},{\Lambda_{1}}(t_{1})}RV_{\rho,{\Lambda_{1}}(t_{1})}^{*}\in\pi_{\mathop{\mathrm{bd}}}\left({\mathcal{A}}_{{{{\Lambda_{1}}}(t_{1})}^{c}\cap\mathop{\mathrm{H}_{U}}}\right)^{\prime}\cap{\mathcal{B}}_{l}\end{split}

(3.45)

and Lemma
3.1
.

\square

Furthermore, we have the following.

Lemma 3.17
.

Consider the setting in subsection
1.1
and assume Assumption
1.6
,
1.7
.
Let
(\Lambda_{0},\Lambda_{r0})\in\mathcal{PC}
and
\rho\in O_{\mathop{\mathrm{bd}},\Lambda_{0}}^{\mathop{\mathrm{bu}}}
,
\sigma,\gamma\in O^{rU}_{\mathop{\mathrm{bd},\Lambda_{r0}}}
.
Then we have

\displaystyle\begin{split}\iota^{(\Lambda_{0},\Lambda_{r0})}\left(\rho:\sigma\otimes\gamma\right)=\left(\mathbb{I}_{\sigma}\otimes\iota^{(\Lambda_{0},\Lambda_{r0})}\left(\rho:\gamma\right)\right)\left(\iota^{(\Lambda_{0},\Lambda_{r0})}\left(\rho:\sigma\right)\otimes\mathbb{I}_{\gamma}\right).\end{split}

(3.46)

The proof is the same as
[
26
]
Lemma 4.17.

\square

Lemma 3.18
.

Let
(\Lambda_{0},\Lambda_{r0})\in\mathcal{PC}
.
Consider the setting in subsection
1.1
, and
assume Assumption
1.6
,Assumption
1.7
.
Then for any
\rho,\sigma\in O_{\mathop{\mathrm{bd}},\Lambda_{0}}^{\mathop{\mathrm{bu}}}
and
\gamma\in O^{rU}_{\mathop{\mathrm{bd},\Lambda_{r0}}}
, we have

\displaystyle\begin{split}&\iota^{(\Lambda_{0},\Lambda_{r0})}\left(\rho\otimes\sigma:\gamma\right)\\
&=\left(\iota^{(\Lambda_{0},\Lambda_{r0})}\left(\rho:\gamma\right)\otimes\mathbb{I}_{T_{\sigma}^{\mathrm{(l)}\Lambda_{r0}\mathbb{I}}}\right)\left(\mathbb{I}_{T_{\rho}^{\mathrm{(l)}\Lambda_{r0}\mathbb{I}}}\otimes\iota^{(\Lambda_{0},\Lambda_{r0})}\left(\sigma:\gamma\right)\right)\end{split}

(3.47)

For any
{\Lambda_{r2}}\in{\mathcal{C}}_{{\mathop{\mathrm{bd}}}}^{r}
with
\Lambda_{0}\leftarrow_{r}{\Lambda_{r2}}
and
V_{\gamma,{\Lambda_{r2}}(t)}\in{\mathcal{V}}_{\gamma{{\Lambda_{r2}}(t)}}^{{\mathop{\mathrm{bd}}}U}
,

t\geq 0

from Proposition
3.11
,
we have

\displaystyle\begin{split}&\left(\iota^{(\Lambda_{0},\Lambda_{r0})}\left(\rho:\gamma\right)\otimes\mathbb{I}_{T_{\sigma}^{\mathrm{(l)}\Lambda_{r0}\mathbb{I}}}\right)\left(\mathbb{I}_{T_{\rho}^{\mathrm{(l)}\Lambda_{r0}\mathbb{I}}}\otimes\iota^{(\Lambda_{0},\Lambda_{r0})}\left(\sigma:\gamma\right)\right)\\
&=\lim_{t\to\infty}V_{\gamma,{\Lambda_{r2}}(t)}^{*}T_{\rho}^{\mathrm{(l)}\Lambda_{r0}\mathbb{I}}\left(V_{\gamma,{\Lambda_{r2}}(t)}\right){T_{\rho}^{\mathrm{(l)}\Lambda_{r0}\mathbb{I}}}\left(V_{\gamma,{\Lambda_{r2}}(t)}^{*}T_{\sigma}^{\mathrm{(l)}\Lambda_{r0}\mathbb{I}}\left(V_{\gamma,{\Lambda_{r2}}(t)}\right)\right)\\
&=\iota^{(\Lambda_{0},\Lambda_{r0})}\left(\rho\otimes\sigma:\gamma\right).\end{split}

(3.48)

\square

Hence if we restrict our
\iota^{(\Lambda_{0},\Lambda_{r0})}
to
O_{\mathop{\mathrm{bd}},\Lambda_{0}}^{\mathop{\mathrm{bu}}}
, then it becomes a braiding, and
we obtain the following.

Theorem 3.19
.

Consider the setting in subsection
1.1
and assume
Assumption
1.6
, Assumption
1.7
,
Assumption
1.10
.
Let
(\Lambda_{0},\Lambda_{r0})\in\mathcal{PC}
.
Then the
C^{*}
-tensor category
C_{\mathop{\mathrm{bd}},\Lambda_{0}}^{\mathop{\mathrm{bu}}}
is braided with braiding

\displaystyle\begin{split}\epsilon_{C_{\mathop{\mathrm{bd}},\Lambda_{0}}^{\mathop{\mathrm{bu}}}}(\rho:\sigma):=\iota^{(\Lambda_{0},\Lambda_{r0})}\left(\rho:\sigma\right),\quad\rho,\sigma\in O_{\mathop{\mathrm{bd}},\Lambda_{0}}^{\mathop{\mathrm{bu}}}.\end{split}

(3.49)

Our
\iota^{(\Lambda_{0},\Lambda_{r0})}\left(-:\rho\right)
satisfies the following asymptotic property.

Lemma 3.20
.

Consider the setting in subsection
1.1
and assume Assumption
1.6
,
1.7
.
Let
(\Lambda_{0},\Lambda_{r0})\in\mathcal{PC}
and

{\Lambda_{1}},{\Lambda_{3}}\in{\mathcal{C}}_{{\mathop{\mathrm{bk}}}}^{U}
with
{\Lambda_{1}}\leftarrow_{r}{\Lambda_{3}}
,
{\Lambda_{1}},{\Lambda_{3}}\subset\Lambda_{0}
.
Then for any
\rho\in O_{\mathop{\mathrm{bd}},\Lambda_{0}}^{\mathop{\mathrm{bu}}}
and
V_{\rho,{\Lambda_{3}}}\in{\mathcal{V}}_{\rho{\Lambda_{3}}}^{\mathop{\mathrm{bu}}}

we have

\displaystyle\begin{split}\lim_{t\to\infty}\sup_{\sigma\in O_{\mathop{\mathrm{bd}{\Lambda_{1}}(t)}}^{\mathop{\mathrm{bu}}}}\left\|\iota^{(\Lambda_{0},\Lambda_{r0})}\left(\sigma:\rho\right)-V_{\rho,{\Lambda_{3}}}^{*}T_{\sigma}^{\mathrm{(l)}\Lambda_{r0}\mathbb{I}}\left(V_{\rho,{\Lambda_{3}}}\right)\right\|=0.\end{split}

(3.50)

Remark 3.21
.

Note in particular

\displaystyle\begin{split}\lim_{t\to\infty}\sup_{\sigma\in O_{\mathop{\mathrm{bd}{\Lambda_{1}}(t)}}^{\mathop{\mathrm{bu}}}}\left\|{V_{\rho,{\Lambda_{3}}}^{\prime}}^{*}T_{\sigma}^{\mathrm{(l)}\Lambda_{r0}\mathbb{I}}\left({V_{\rho,{\Lambda_{3}}}^{\prime}}\right)-V_{\rho,{\Lambda_{3}}}^{*}T_{\sigma}^{\mathrm{(l)}\Lambda_{r0}\mathbb{I}}\left(V_{\rho,{\Lambda_{3}}}\right)\right\|\end{split}

(3.51)

for any other
V_{\rho,{\Lambda_{3}}}^{\prime}\in{\mathcal{V}}_{\rho{\Lambda_{3}}}^{\mathop{\mathrm{bu}}}
.

Remark 3.22
.

See Figure
12
.
If
\rho\in O_{\mathop{\mathrm{bd}{\Lambda_{3}}}}^{\mathop{\mathrm{bu}}}
, we may take
V_{\rho,{\Lambda_{3}}}=\mathbb{I}

hence

V_{\rho,{\Lambda_{3}}}^{*}T_{\sigma}^{\mathrm{(l)}\Lambda_{r0}\mathbb{I}}\left(V_{\rho,{\Lambda_{3}}}\right)=\mathbb{I}
,
and (
3.50
) says
we have

\lim_{t\to\infty}\sup_{\sigma\in O_{\mathop{\mathrm{bd}{\Lambda_{1}}(t)}}^{\mathop{\mathrm{bu}}}}\left\|\iota^{(\Lambda_{0},\Lambda_{r0})}\left(\sigma:\rho\right)-\mathbb{I}\right\|=0

In our definition of
\iota^{(\Lambda_{0},\Lambda_{r0})}\left(\sigma:\rho\right)
, (roughly) we are moving the
\rho
particle
to right infinity, and measuring the braiding at the cross with
\sigma
’s string operator (recall Remark
3.12
).
If
\rho
is on the right of
\sigma
from the beginning (for example , if
\rho\in O_{\mathop{\mathrm{bd}{\Lambda_{3}}}}^{\mathop{\mathrm{bu}}}
and

{\Lambda_{3}}
is on the right of
{\Lambda_{1}}
), then there will be no
crossing. Therefore
\iota^{(\Lambda_{0},\Lambda_{r0})}\left(\sigma:\rho\right)\approx\mathbb{I}
(tail occurs because of the approximate Haag duality). That is what this Lemma is telling us.

For
\rho\in O_{\mathop{\mathrm{bd}},\Lambda_{0}}^{\mathop{\mathrm{bu}}}
and
V_{\rho,{\Lambda_{3}}}\in{\mathcal{V}}_{\rho{\Lambda_{3}}}^{\mathop{\mathrm{bu}}}
, we set

\displaystyle\tilde{\rho}:=\mathop{\mathrm{Ad}}\nolimits\left(V_{\rho,{\Lambda_{3}}}\right)\rho\in O_{\mathop{\mathrm{bd}{\Lambda_{3}}}}^{\mathop{\mathrm{bu}}}\subset O_{\mathop{\mathrm{bd}},\Lambda_{0}}^{\mathop{\mathrm{bu}}}.

(3.52)

Then, because
V_{\rho,{\Lambda_{3}}}\in(\rho,\tilde{\rho})_{U}
,
by Lemma
3.14
,
we have

\displaystyle\begin{split}\iota^{(\Lambda_{0},\Lambda_{r0})}\left(\sigma:\tilde{\rho}\right)\left(\mathbb{I}_{\sigma}\otimes V_{\rho,{\Lambda_{3}}}\right)=\left(V_{\rho,{\Lambda_{3}}}\otimes\mathbb{I}_{\sigma}\right)\iota^{(\Lambda_{0},\Lambda_{r0})}\left(\sigma:\rho\right),\end{split}

(3.53)

for each
\sigma\in O_{\mathop{\mathrm{bd}{\Lambda_{1}}(t)}}^{\mathop{\mathrm{bu}}}
.
Let
{\Lambda_{r2}},{\Lambda_{r4}}\in{\mathcal{C}}_{{\mathop{\mathrm{bd}}}}^{r}
with

\Lambda_{0}\leftarrow_{r}{\Lambda_{r2}}
,

{\Lambda_{1}}\leftarrow{\Lambda_{r4}}
,

{\Lambda_{3}}\subset{\Lambda_{r4}}
.
Then for any
V_{\tilde{\rho},{\Lambda_{r2}}(t_{2})}\in{\mathcal{V}}_{\tilde{\rho}{\Lambda_{r2}}(t_{2})}^{{\mathop{\mathrm{bd}}}U}
,

t_{2}\geq 0
, we have

V_{\tilde{\rho},{\Lambda_{r2}}(t_{2})}\in\pi_{\mathop{\mathrm{bd}}}\left({\mathcal{A}}_{{\Lambda_{r4}}^{c}\cap\mathop{\mathrm{H}_{U}}}\right)^{\prime}\cap{\mathcal{F}}_{{\mathop{\mathrm{bd}}}}^{U}

for
t_{2}\geq 0
large enough.
Hence we have

\displaystyle\begin{split}&\sup_{\sigma\in O_{\mathop{\mathrm{bd}{\Lambda_{1}}(t)}}^{\mathop{\mathrm{bu}}}}\left\|\iota^{(\Lambda_{0},\Lambda_{r0})}\left(\sigma:\tilde{\rho}\right)-\mathbb{I}\right\|=\sup_{\sigma\in O_{\mathop{\mathrm{bd}{\Lambda_{1}}(t)}}^{\mathop{\mathrm{bu}}}}\lim_{t_{2}\to\infty}\left\|V_{\tilde{\rho},{\Lambda_{r2}}(t_{2})}^{*}\left(T_{\sigma}^{\mathrm{(l)}{\Lambda_{r0}}\mathbb{I}}-\mathop{\mathrm{id}}\nolimits\right)\left(V_{\tilde{\rho},{\Lambda_{r2}}(t_{2})}\right)\right\|\\
&\leq\sup_{\sigma\in O_{\mathop{\mathrm{bd}{\Lambda_{1}}(t)}}^{\mathop{\mathrm{bu}}}}\left\|\left(\left.T_{\sigma}^{\mathrm{(l)}{\Lambda_{r0}}\mathbb{I}}-\mathop{\mathrm{id}}\nolimits\right)\right|_{\pi_{\mathop{\mathrm{bd}}}\left({\mathcal{A}}_{{\Lambda_{r4}}^{c}\cap\mathop{\mathrm{H}_{U}}}\right)^{\prime}\cap{\mathcal{F}}_{{\mathop{\mathrm{bd}}}}^{U}}\right\|.\end{split}

(3.54)

By the same argument as Lemma
3.6
, the last line
goes to
0
as
t\to\infty
.
Combining this and (
3.53
), we obtain the result.

\square

This property of
\iota^{(\Lambda_{0},\Lambda_{r0})}\left(\sigma:\rho\right)
will be relevant later.

4
A bulk-to-boundary map

In this section, we construct a map from bulk to boundary under the Assumption
1.3
.
Recall from Lemma 2.13 of
[
26
]
, for any

\theta\in{\mathbb{R}}
,
\varphi\in(0,\pi)
,
\rho\in O_{\mathop{\mathrm{bk}}}
,
\Lambda_{0}\in C_{(\theta,\varphi)}
,

V_{\rho,\Lambda_{0}}\in{\mathcal{V}}_{\rho\Lambda_{0}}^{{\mathop{\mathrm{bk}}}}
,
we may define an endomorphism
T_{\rho}^{(\theta,\varphi)\Lambda_{0}V_{\rho,\Lambda_{0}}}

on
{\mathcal{B}}_{(\theta,\varphi)}
.
We use this symbol for the rest of this paper.
Assumption
1.3
gives a
*
-isomorphism between
{\mathcal{F}}_{{\mathop{\mathrm{bk}}}}^{U}
and
{\mathcal{F}}_{{\mathop{\mathrm{bd}}}}^{U}
.

Lemma 4.1
.

Consider the setting in subsection
1.1
. Assume Assumption
1.3
.
Then there exists a
*
-isomorphism

\tau:{\mathcal{F}}_{{\mathop{\mathrm{bk}}}}^{U}\to{\mathcal{F}}_{{\mathop{\mathrm{bd}}}}^{U}
such that

\displaystyle\left.\tau\pi_{\mathop{\mathrm{bk}}}\right|_{{\mathcal{A}}_{\mathop{\mathrm{H}_{U}}}}=\pi_{\mathop{\mathrm{bd}}}.

(4.1)

By Assumption
1.3
, for each
\Lambda\in{\mathcal{C}}_{{\mathop{\mathrm{bk}}}}^{U}
, there exists
a
*
-isomorphism
\tau_{\Lambda}:\pi_{\mathop{\mathrm{bk}}}\left({{\mathcal{A}}_{\Lambda\cap\mathop{\mathrm{H}_{U}}}}\right)^{\prime\prime}\to\pi_{\mathop{\mathrm{bd}}}\left({{\mathcal{A}}_{\Lambda\cap\mathop{\mathrm{H}_{U}}}}\right)^{\prime\prime}

such that

\displaystyle\tau_{\Lambda}\pi_{\mathop{\mathrm{bk}}}(A)=\pi_{\mathop{\mathrm{bd}}}(A),\quad\text{ for all}\;A\in{{\mathcal{A}}_{\Lambda\cap\mathop{\mathrm{H}_{U}}}}.

(4.2)

From (
4.2
) and
\sigma
-weak continuity of
\tau_{\Lambda}
s, for any
\Lambda_{1},\Lambda_{2}\in{\mathcal{C}}_{{\mathop{\mathrm{bk}}}}^{U}

with
\Lambda_{1}\subset\Lambda_{2}
, we have

\tau_{\Lambda_{2}}|_{\pi_{\mathop{\mathrm{bk}}}\left({{\mathcal{A}}_{\Lambda_{1}\cap\mathop{\mathrm{H}_{U}}}}\right)^{\prime\prime}}=\tau_{\Lambda_{1}}.

By the upward-filtering property of
{\mathcal{C}}_{{\mathop{\mathrm{bk}}}}^{U}
, this means
that we can define an isometric
*
-homomorphism

\tau_{0}
from
{\mathcal{F}}_{{\mathop{\mathrm{bk}}}}^{U(0)}
onto
{\mathcal{F}}_{{\mathop{\mathrm{bd}}}}^{U(0)}

by

\displaystyle\tau_{0}(x)=\tau_{\Lambda}(x),\quad\text{if}\;\;x\in\pi_{\mathop{\mathrm{bk}}}\left({{\mathcal{A}}_{\Lambda\cap\mathop{\mathrm{H}_{U}}}}\right)^{\prime\prime},\quad\Lambda\in{\mathcal{C}}_{{\mathop{\mathrm{bk}}}}^{U}.

(4.3)

Because
\tau_{0}
is an isometry, we can extend it to an isometric
*
-homomorphism

\tau:{\mathcal{F}}_{{\mathop{\mathrm{bk}}}}^{U}\to{\mathcal{F}}_{{\mathop{\mathrm{bd}}}}^{U}
.
It is onto because
\tau({\mathcal{F}}_{{\mathop{\mathrm{bk}}}}^{U})\supset\tau_{0}({\mathcal{F}}_{{\mathop{\mathrm{bk}}}}^{U(0)})={\mathcal{F}}_{{\mathop{\mathrm{bd}}}}^{U(0)}

and
\tau({\mathcal{F}}_{{\mathop{\mathrm{bk}}}}^{U})
is closed.
Hence
\tau
is a
*
-isomorphism.
For any
A\in{\mathcal{A}}_{\mathop{\mathrm{H}_{U}},\mathop{\mathrm{loc}}}
, there exists
\Lambda\in{\mathcal{C}}_{{\mathop{\mathrm{bk}}}}^{U}
such that
A\in{\mathcal{A}}_{\Lambda\cap\mathop{\mathrm{H}_{U}}}
.
Hence we have

\displaystyle\tau\pi_{\mathop{\mathrm{bk}}}(A)=\tau_{0}\pi_{\mathop{\mathrm{bk}}}\left(A\right)=\pi_{\mathop{\mathrm{bd}}}(A).

(4.4)

As this holds for any
A\in{\mathcal{A}}_{\mathop{\mathrm{H}_{U}},\mathop{\mathrm{loc}}}
, the same is true for any
{\mathcal{A}}_{\mathop{\mathrm{H}_{U}}}
.

\square

In a word, this
\tau
will give a copy of the bulk theory at the boundary.
In order to carry it out, we would like to restrict our bulk endomorphisms to

{\mathcal{F}}_{{\mathop{\mathrm{bk}}}}^{U}
. The following Lemma will be used for that purpose.

Lemma 4.2
.

Consider the setting in subsection
1.1
.
We have

\displaystyle{\mathcal{G}}\cap\pi_{\mathop{\mathrm{bk}}}\left({\mathcal{A}}_{\mathop{\mathrm{H}_{D}}}\right)^{\prime}\subset{\mathcal{F}}_{{\mathop{\mathrm{bk}}}}^{U}.

(4.5)

For any
x\in{\mathcal{G}}\cap\pi_{\mathop{\mathrm{bk}}}\left({\mathcal{A}}_{\mathop{\mathrm{H}_{D}}}\right)^{\prime}
, by the definition of
{\mathcal{G}}
,
there exists a sequence of
\Lambda_{n}\in{\mathcal{C}}_{{\mathop{\mathrm{bk}}}}^{U}
and
x_{n}\in\pi_{\mathop{\mathrm{bk}}}\left({\mathcal{A}}_{\Lambda_{n}}\right)^{\prime\prime}
,
n\in{\mathbb{N}}

such that
\lim_{n\to\infty}\left\|x-x_{n}\right\|=0
.
Set
S_{n}:=\Lambda_{n}\cap\mathop{\mathrm{H}_{D}}
. Note that
S_{n}
is a finite set, because
\Lambda_{n}\in{\mathcal{C}}_{{\mathop{\mathrm{bk}}}}^{U}
.
We apply Lemma
B.1
replacing

\Gamma
,
S
,
({\mathcal{H}},\pi)
with
\Lambda_{n}
,
S_{n}
,
({\mathcal{H}}_{\mathop{\mathrm{bk}}},\pi_{\mathop{\mathrm{bk}}}|_{{\mathcal{A}}_{\Lambda_{n}}})
.
Then we obtain projections of norm
1

{\mathcal{E}}_{S_{n}}:{\mathcal{B}}({\mathcal{H}}_{\mathop{\mathrm{bk}}})\to\pi_{\mathop{\mathrm{bk}}}({\mathcal{A}}_{S_{n}})^{\prime}

such that
{\mathcal{E}}_{S_{n}}\left(\pi_{\mathop{\mathrm{bk}}}\left({{\mathcal{A}}_{\Lambda_{n}}}\right)^{\prime\prime}\right)=\pi_{\mathop{\mathrm{bk}}}\left({{\mathcal{A}}_{\Lambda_{n}\setminus S_{n}}}\right)^{\prime\prime}=\pi_{\mathop{\mathrm{bk}}}\left({{\mathcal{A}}_{\Lambda_{n}\cap H_{U}}}\right)^{\prime\prime}\subset{\mathcal{F}}_{{\mathop{\mathrm{bk}}}}^{U}
.
From this, for
x_{n}\in\pi_{\mathop{\mathrm{bk}}}\left({\mathcal{A}}_{\Lambda_{n}}\right)^{\prime\prime}
, we have

{\mathcal{E}}_{S_{n}}(x_{n})\in{\mathcal{F}}_{{\mathop{\mathrm{bk}}}}^{U}
.
Because
x\in\pi_{\mathop{\mathrm{bk}}}\left({\mathcal{A}}_{\mathop{\mathrm{H}_{D}}}\right)^{\prime}\subset\pi_{\mathop{\mathrm{bk}}}\left({\mathcal{A}}_{S_{n}}\right)^{\prime}
,
we have
{\mathcal{E}}_{S_{n}}(x)=x
.
Then,

\displaystyle\left\|x-{\mathcal{E}}_{S_{n}}(x_{n})\right\|=\left\|{\mathcal{E}}_{S_{n}}\left(x-x_{n}\right)\right\|\leq\left\|x-x_{n}\right\|\to 0.

(4.6)

Because
{\mathcal{E}}_{S_{n}}(x_{n})\in{\mathcal{F}}_{{\mathop{\mathrm{bk}}}}^{U}
, this means
x\in{\mathcal{F}}_{{\mathop{\mathrm{bk}}}}^{U}
.

\square

This Lemma guarantees that the restriction of
T_{\rho}^{(\frac{3\pi}{2},\frac{\pi}{2}),\Lambda_{0},V_{\rho,\Lambda_{0}}}
below to
{\mathcal{F}}_{{\mathop{\mathrm{bk}}}}^{U}

gives an endomorphism on
{\mathcal{F}}_{{\mathop{\mathrm{bk}}}}^{U}
.

Lemma 4.3
.

Consider the setting in subsection
1.1
.
Assume Assumption
1.4
.
Then for any
\Lambda_{0}\in{\mathcal{C}}_{{\mathop{\mathrm{bk}}}}^{U}
with
\Lambda_{0}\subset H_{U}
,

\rho\in O_{\mathop{\mathrm{bk}}}
,
V_{\rho,\Lambda_{0}}\in{\mathcal{V}}_{\rho\Lambda_{0}}^{{\mathop{\mathrm{bk}}}}
,
we have

\displaystyle T_{\rho}^{(\frac{3\pi}{2},\frac{\pi}{2}),\Lambda_{0},V_{\rho,\Lambda_{0}}}\left({\mathcal{F}}_{{\mathop{\mathrm{bk}}}}^{U}\right)\subset{\mathcal{F}}_{{\mathop{\mathrm{bk}}}}^{U}.

(4.7)

We first claim
T_{\rho}^{(\frac{3\pi}{2},\frac{\pi}{2}),\Lambda_{0},V_{\rho,\Lambda_{0}}}\left(\pi_{\mathop{\mathrm{bk}}}\left({\mathcal{A}}_{\Lambda\cap\mathop{\mathrm{H}_{U}}}\right)^{\prime\prime}\right)\subset\pi_{\mathop{\mathrm{bk}}}\left({\mathcal{A}}_{\mathop{\mathrm{H}_{D}}}\right)^{\prime}

for any
\Lambda\in{\mathcal{C}}_{{\mathop{\mathrm{bk}}}}^{U}
.
To see this, note that for any
A\in{\mathcal{A}}_{\mathop{\mathrm{H}_{D}}}\subset{\mathcal{A}}_{\Lambda_{0}^{c}}
, we have

T_{\rho}^{(\frac{3\pi}{2},\frac{\pi}{2}),\Lambda_{0},V_{\rho,\Lambda_{0}}}\left(\pi_{\mathop{\mathrm{bk}}}(A)\right)=\pi_{\mathop{\mathrm{bk}}}(A)
by Lemma 2.13 of
[
26
]
.
Therefore, for any

x\in\pi_{\mathop{\mathrm{bk}}}\left({\mathcal{A}}_{\Lambda\cap\mathop{\mathrm{H}_{U}}}\right)^{\prime\prime}
, and
A\in{\mathcal{A}}_{\mathop{\mathrm{H}_{D}}}
we have

\displaystyle\left[\pi_{\mathop{\mathrm{bk}}}(A),T_{\rho}^{(\frac{3\pi}{2},\frac{\pi}{2}),\Lambda_{0},V_{\rho,\Lambda_{0}}}(x)\right]=T_{\rho}^{(\frac{3\pi}{2},\frac{\pi}{2}),\Lambda_{0},V_{\rho,\Lambda_{0}}}\left(\left[\pi_{\mathop{\mathrm{bk}}}(A),x\right]\right)=0,

(4.8)

proving the claim.

Next, we claim that for any
\Lambda\in{\mathcal{C}}_{{\mathop{\mathrm{bk}}}}^{U}
,

T_{\rho}^{(\frac{3\pi}{2},\frac{\pi}{2}),\Lambda_{0},V_{\rho,\Lambda_{0}}}\left(\pi_{\mathop{\mathrm{bk}}}\left({\mathcal{A}}_{\Lambda\cap\mathop{\mathrm{H}_{U}}}\right)^{\prime\prime}\right)\subset{\mathcal{G}}
.
To see this, note from the upward-filtering property of
{\mathcal{C}}_{{\mathop{\mathrm{bk}}}}^{U}
,
for
\Lambda,\Lambda_{0}\in{\mathcal{C}}_{{\mathop{\mathrm{bk}}}}^{U}
, there exists a
\Lambda_{1}\in{\mathcal{C}}_{{\mathop{\mathrm{bk}}}}^{U}

such that
\Lambda,\Lambda_{0}\subset\Lambda_{1}
.
Then for any

A\in{\mathcal{A}}_{(\Lambda_{1})^{c}}\subset{\mathcal{A}}_{(\Lambda_{0})^{c}}
,
we have
T_{\rho}^{(\frac{3\pi}{2},\frac{\pi}{2}),\Lambda_{0},V_{\rho,\Lambda_{0}}}\left(\pi_{\mathop{\mathrm{bk}}}(A)\right)=\pi_{\mathop{\mathrm{bk}}}(A)
,
and the same argument as in (
4.8
) implies

T_{\rho}^{(\frac{3\pi}{2},\frac{\pi}{2}),\Lambda_{0},V_{\rho,\Lambda_{0}}}\left(\pi_{\mathop{\mathrm{bk}}}({\mathcal{A}}_{\Lambda\cap\mathop{\mathrm{H}_{U}}})^{\prime\prime}\right)\subset\pi_{\mathop{\mathrm{bk}}}\left({\mathcal{A}}_{\Lambda_{1}^{c}}\right)^{\prime}\subset{\mathcal{G}}
, proving the claim.
Here we used Lemma
1.5
.

Combining these with Lemma
4.2
, for any
\Lambda\in{\mathcal{C}}_{{\mathop{\mathrm{bk}}}}^{U}
,
we obtain

\displaystyle T_{\rho}^{(\frac{3\pi}{2},\frac{\pi}{2}),\Lambda_{0},V_{\rho,\Lambda_{0}}}\left(\pi_{\mathop{\mathrm{bk}}}\left({\mathcal{A}}_{\Lambda\cap\mathop{\mathrm{H}_{U}}}\right)^{\prime\prime}\right)\subset{\mathcal{G}}\cap\pi_{\mathop{\mathrm{bk}}}\left({\mathcal{A}}_{\mathop{\mathrm{H}_{D}}}\right)^{\prime}\subset{\mathcal{F}}_{{\mathop{\mathrm{bk}}}}^{U}.

(4.9)

By the definition of
{\mathcal{F}}_{{\mathop{\mathrm{bk}}}}^{U}
, we obtain
T_{\rho}^{(\frac{3\pi}{2},\frac{\pi}{2}),\Lambda_{0},V_{\rho,\Lambda_{0}}}\left({\mathcal{F}}_{{\mathop{\mathrm{bk}}}}^{U}\right)\subset{\mathcal{F}}_{{\mathop{\mathrm{bk}}}}^{U}
.

\square

Intertwiners between such endomorphisms are also in
{\mathcal{F}}_{{\mathop{\mathrm{bk}}}}^{U}
.

Lemma 4.4
.

Consider the setting in subsection
1.1
.
Assume Assumption
1.4
.Then for any
\rho,\sigma\in O_{\mathop{\mathrm{bk}}}
,

{\Lambda_{1}},{\Lambda_{2}}\in{\mathcal{C}}_{{\mathop{\mathrm{bk}}}}^{U}
with
{\Lambda_{1}},{\Lambda_{2}}\subset H_{U}
, we have

\left(T_{\rho}^{(\frac{3\pi}{2},\frac{\pi}{2}),{\Lambda_{1}},V_{\rho,{\Lambda_{1}}}},T_{\sigma}^{(\frac{3\pi}{2},\frac{\pi}{2}),{\Lambda_{2}},V_{\sigma,{\Lambda_{2}}}}\right)\subset{\mathcal{F}}_{{\mathop{\mathrm{bk}}}}^{U}
.

Because
T_{\rho}^{(\frac{3\pi}{2},\frac{\pi}{2}),{\Lambda_{1}},V_{\rho,{\Lambda_{1}}}}
and
T_{\rho}^{(\frac{3\pi}{2},\frac{\pi}{2}),{\Lambda_{2}},V_{\rho,{\Lambda_{2}}}}
are identity on
\pi_{\mathop{\mathrm{bk}}}\left({\mathcal{A}}_{(\Lambda_{1}\cup\Lambda_{2})^{c}}\right)

we have
\left(T_{\rho}^{(\frac{3\pi}{2},\frac{\pi}{2}),{\Lambda_{1}},V_{\rho,{\Lambda_{1}}}},T_{\sigma}^{(\frac{3\pi}{2},\frac{\pi}{2}),{\Lambda_{2}},V_{\sigma,{\Lambda_{2}}}}\right)\subset\pi_{\mathop{\mathrm{bk}}}\left({\mathcal{A}}_{(\Lambda_{1}\cup\Lambda_{2})^{c}}\right)^{\prime}
.
From the upward filtering property of
{\mathcal{C}}_{{\mathop{\mathrm{bk}}}}^{U}
and Lemma
4.2
,
we obtain
\pi_{\mathop{\mathrm{bk}}}\left({\mathcal{A}}_{(\Lambda_{1}\cup\Lambda_{2})^{c}}\right)^{\prime}\subset{\mathcal{F}}_{{\mathop{\mathrm{bk}}}}^{U}
.

\square

Now, we translate these bulk objects to boundary objects using the
*
-isomorphism

\tau
.

Lemma 4.5
.

Consider the setting in subsection
1.1
.
Assume Assumption
1.4
and Assumption
1.3
.
Let
(\Lambda_{0},\Lambda_{r0})\in\mathcal{PC}
, and
\tau:{\mathcal{F}}_{{\mathop{\mathrm{bk}}}}^{U}\to{\mathcal{F}}_{{\mathop{\mathrm{bd}}}}^{U}
the
*
-isomorphism given in
Lemma
4.1
.
Then the following hold.

(i)

For any
\rho\in O_{\mathop{\mathrm{bk},\Lambda_{0}}}
,

\displaystyle F_{0}^{(\Lambda_{0},\Lambda_{r0})}(\rho)=\tau\;T_{\rho}^{(\frac{3\pi}{2},\frac{\pi}{2}),{\Lambda_{0}},{\mathbb{I}}}\;\tau^{-1}\pi_{\mathop{\mathrm{bd}}}

(4.10)

belongs to
{O_{\mathop{\mathrm{bd}},\Lambda_{0}}^{\mathop{\mathrm{bu}}}}

and defines a map
F_{0}^{(\Lambda_{0},\Lambda_{r0})}:O_{\mathop{\mathrm{bk},\Lambda_{0}}}\to{O_{\mathop{\mathrm{bd}},\Lambda_{0}}^{\mathop{\mathrm{bu}}}}
.

(ii)

For any
\rho\in O_{\mathop{\mathrm{bk},\Lambda_{0}}}
,
(\Lambda,\Lambda_{r})\in\mathcal{PC}
and
V_{\rho,\Lambda}\in{\mathcal{V}}_{\rho\Lambda}^{{\mathop{\mathrm{bk}}}}
,

\tau(V_{\rho,\Lambda})
belongs to
{\mathcal{V}}_{F_{0}^{(\Lambda_{0},\Lambda_{r0})}(\rho)\Lambda}^{\mathop{\mathrm{bu}}}
.

(iii)

For any
\rho,\sigma\in O_{\mathop{\mathrm{bk},\Lambda_{0}}}
and
R\in(\rho,\sigma)
,

\displaystyle\begin{split}F_{1}^{(\Lambda_{0},\Lambda_{r0})}(R):=\tau(R)\in\left(F_{0}^{(\Lambda_{0},\Lambda_{r0})}(\rho),F_{0}^{(\Lambda_{0},\Lambda_{r0})}(\sigma)\right)_{U}\end{split}

(4.11)

and defines a map
F_{1}^{(\Lambda_{0},\Lambda_{r0})}:(\rho,\sigma)\to\left(F_{0}^{(\Lambda_{0},\Lambda_{r0})}(\rho),F_{0}^{(\Lambda_{0},\Lambda_{r0})}(\sigma)\right)_{U}
.

(iv)

For any
\rho\in O_{\mathop{\mathrm{bk},\Lambda_{0}}}
,
({\Lambda_{1}},{\Lambda_{r1}})\in\mathcal{PC}
and

V_{\rho,{\Lambda_{1}}}\in{\mathcal{V}}_{\rho{\Lambda_{1}}}^{{\mathop{\mathrm{bk}}}}
,

\displaystyle\left.T_{F_{0}^{(\Lambda_{0},\Lambda_{r0})}(\rho)}^{\mathrm{(l)}{\Lambda_{r1}}\tau(V_{\rho,{\Lambda_{1}}})}\right|_{{\mathcal{F}}_{{\mathop{\mathrm{bd}}}}^{U}}=\left.\tau\;T_{\rho}^{(\frac{3\pi}{2},\frac{\pi}{2}),{\Lambda_{1}},V_{\rho,{\Lambda_{1}}}}\;\tau^{-1}\right|_{{\mathcal{F}}_{{\mathop{\mathrm{bd}}}}^{U}}.

(4.12)

For any
\rho\in O_{\mathop{\mathrm{bk},\Lambda_{0}}}
,
the composition (
4.10
) makes sense because

\pi_{\mathop{\mathrm{bk}}}({\mathcal{A}}_{\mathop{\mathrm{H}_{U}}})\subset{\mathcal{F}}_{{\mathop{\mathrm{bk}}}}^{U}
, Lemma
4.1
, Lemma
4.3
.
For any
\rho\in O_{\mathop{\mathrm{bk},\Lambda_{0}}}
,
(\Lambda,\Lambda_{r})\in\mathcal{PC}
and
V_{\rho,\Lambda}\in{\mathcal{V}}_{\rho\Lambda}^{{\mathop{\mathrm{bk}}}}
, by Lemma 2.14 of
[
26
]
and Lemma
4.4
we have
V_{\rho,\Lambda}\in\left(T_{\rho}^{(\frac{3\pi}{2},\frac{\pi}{2}),{\Lambda_{0}},{\mathbb{I}}},T_{\rho}^{(\frac{3\pi}{2},\frac{\pi}{2}),\Lambda,V_{\rho,\Lambda}}\right)\subset{\mathcal{F}}_{{\mathop{\mathrm{bk}}}}^{U}
.
Hence
\tau(V_{\rho,\Lambda})\in{\mathcal{F}}_{{\mathop{\mathrm{bd}}}}^{U}
is well-defined, and
by definition,

\displaystyle\begin{split}&\left.\mathop{\mathrm{Ad}}\nolimits\left(\tau(V_{\rho,\Lambda})\right)F_{0}^{(\Lambda_{0},\Lambda_{r0})}(\rho)\right|_{{\mathcal{A}}_{\Lambda^{c}\cap\mathop{\mathrm{H}_{U}}}}=\left.\mathop{\mathrm{Ad}}\nolimits\left(\tau(V_{\rho,\Lambda})\right)\tau\;T_{\rho}^{(\frac{3\pi}{2},\frac{\pi}{2}),{\Lambda_{0}},{\mathbb{I}}}\;\tau^{-1}\pi_{\mathop{\mathrm{bd}}}\right|_{{\mathcal{A}}_{\Lambda^{c}\cap\mathop{\mathrm{H}_{U}}}}\\
&=\left.\mathop{\mathrm{Ad}}\nolimits\left(\tau(V_{\rho,\Lambda})\right)\tau\;T_{\rho}^{(\frac{3\pi}{2},\frac{\pi}{2}),{\Lambda_{0}},{\mathbb{I}}}\;\pi_{\mathop{\mathrm{bk}}}\right|_{{\mathcal{A}}_{\Lambda^{c}\cap\mathop{\mathrm{H}_{U}}}}=\left.\mathop{\mathrm{Ad}}\nolimits\left(\tau(V_{\rho,\Lambda})\right)\tau\;\rho\right|_{{\mathcal{A}}_{\Lambda^{c}\cap\mathop{\mathrm{H}_{U}}}}=\left.\tau\;\pi_{\mathop{\mathrm{bk}}}\right|_{{\mathcal{A}}_{\Lambda^{c}\cap\mathop{\mathrm{H}_{U}}}}=\left.\pi_{\mathop{\mathrm{bd}}}\right|_{{\mathcal{A}}_{\Lambda^{c}\cap\mathop{\mathrm{H}_{U}}}}.\end{split}

(4.13)

Hence
\tau(V_{\rho,\Lambda})\in{\mathcal{V}}_{F_{0}^{(\Lambda_{0},\Lambda_{r0})}(\rho)\Lambda}^{\mathop{\mathrm{bu}}}

for any
\rho\in O_{\mathop{\mathrm{bk},\Lambda_{0}}}
,
(\Lambda,\Lambda_{r})\in\mathcal{PC}
and
V_{\rho,\Lambda}\in{\mathcal{V}}_{\rho\Lambda}^{{\mathop{\mathrm{bk}}}}
.
In particular, if we take
\Lambda=\Lambda_{0}
and
V_{\rho,\Lambda_{0}}=\mathbb{I}
,
we see that
\left.F_{0}^{(\Lambda_{0},\Lambda_{r0})}(\rho)\right|_{{\mathcal{A}}_{\Lambda_{0}^{c}\cap\mathop{\mathrm{H}_{U}}}}=\left.\pi_{\mathop{\mathrm{bd}}}\right|_{{\mathcal{A}}_{\Lambda_{0}^{c}\cap\mathop{\mathrm{H}_{U}}}}
.
This implies (i) and (ii).
(Note that for any
\Lambda_{1}\in{\mathcal{C}}_{{\mathop{\mathrm{bk}}}}^{U}
, there exists a
(\Lambda,\Lambda_{r})\in\mathcal{PC}
such that

\Lambda\subset\Lambda_{1}\cap\mathop{\mathrm{H}_{U}}
.)
For any
\rho,\sigma\in O_{\mathop{\mathrm{bk},\Lambda_{0}}}
and
R\in(\rho,\sigma)
,

R\in\left(T_{\rho}^{(\frac{3\pi}{2},\frac{\pi}{2}),{\Lambda_{0}},{\mathbb{I}}},T_{\sigma}^{(\frac{3\pi}{2},\frac{\pi}{2}),{\Lambda_{0}},{\mathbb{I}}}\right)\subset{\mathcal{F}}_{{\mathop{\mathrm{bk}}}}^{U}
by Lemma
4.4

and
\tau(R)\in{\mathcal{F}}_{{\mathop{\mathrm{bd}}}}^{U}
is well-defined.
It is then straight forward to check that
\tau(R)\in\left(F_{0}^{(\Lambda_{0},\Lambda_{r0})}(\rho),F_{0}^{(\Lambda_{0},\Lambda_{r0})}(\sigma)\right)_{U}
,
proving (iii).
To show (iv), let
\rho\in O_{\mathop{\mathrm{bk},\Lambda_{0}}}
,
({\Lambda_{1}},{\Lambda_{r1}})\in\mathcal{PC}
and

V_{\rho,{\Lambda_{1}}}\in{\mathcal{V}}_{\rho{\Lambda_{1}}}^{{\mathop{\mathrm{bk}}}}
.
Note that
\left.T_{F_{0}^{(\Lambda_{0},\Lambda_{r0})}(\rho)}^{\mathrm{(l)}{\Lambda_{r1}}\tau(V_{\rho,{\Lambda_{1}}})}\right|_{{\mathcal{F}}_{{\mathop{\mathrm{bd}}}}^{U}}
.
is
\sigma
-weak continuous on
\pi_{\mathop{\mathrm{bd}}}\left({\mathcal{A}}_{\Lambda\cap\mathop{\mathrm{H}_{U}}}\right)^{\prime\prime}
for any
\Lambda\in{\mathcal{C}}_{{\mathop{\mathrm{bk}}}}^{U}
,
because for any
\Lambda\in{\mathcal{C}}_{{\mathop{\mathrm{bk}}}}^{U}
, there is some
\Lambda_{l}\in{\mathcal{C}}_{{\mathop{\mathrm{bd}}}}^{l}
such that
\Lambda\cap\mathop{\mathrm{H}_{U}}\subset\Lambda_{l}
.
(Recall Lemma
2.1
.)
Therefore,
\tau^{-1}\;\left.T_{F_{0}^{(\Lambda_{0},\Lambda_{r0})}(\rho)}^{\mathrm{(l)}{\Lambda_{r1}}\tau(V_{\rho,{\Lambda_{1}}})}\right|_{{\mathcal{F}}_{{\mathop{\mathrm{bd}}}}^{U}}\;\tau
is
\sigma
-weak continuous on

\pi_{\mathop{\mathrm{bk}}}\left({\mathcal{A}}_{\Lambda\cap\mathop{\mathrm{H}_{U}}}\right)^{\prime\prime}
for any
\Lambda\in{\mathcal{C}}_{{\mathop{\mathrm{bk}}}}^{U}
.
On the other hand, for any
A\in{\mathcal{A}}_{\mathop{\mathrm{H}_{U}}}
,

\displaystyle\begin{split}\tau^{-1}\;\left.T_{F_{0}^{(\Lambda_{0},\Lambda_{r0})}(\rho)}^{\mathrm{(l)}{\Lambda_{r1}}\tau(V_{\rho,{\Lambda_{1}}})}\right|_{{\mathcal{F}}_{{\mathop{\mathrm{bd}}}}^{U}}\;\tau\pi_{\mathop{\mathrm{bk}}}(A)=\tau^{-1}\;\left.T_{F_{0}^{(\Lambda_{0},\Lambda_{r0})}(\rho)}^{\mathrm{(l)}{\Lambda_{r1}}\tau(V_{\rho,{\Lambda_{1}}})}\right|_{{\mathcal{F}}_{{\mathop{\mathrm{bd}}}}^{U}}\;\pi_{\mathop{\mathrm{bd}}}(A)=\tau^{-1}\;\mathop{\mathrm{Ad}}\nolimits\left(\tau(V_{\rho,{\Lambda_{1}}})\right)F_{0}^{(\Lambda_{0},\Lambda_{r0})}(\rho)(A)\\
=\tau^{-1}\;\mathop{\mathrm{Ad}}\nolimits\left(\tau(V_{\rho,{\Lambda_{1}}})\right)\tau\;T_{\rho}^{(\frac{3\pi}{2},\frac{\pi}{2}),{\Lambda_{0}},{\mathbb{I}}}\;\tau^{-1}\pi_{\mathop{\mathrm{bd}}}(A)=\;\mathop{\mathrm{Ad}}\nolimits\left(V_{\rho,{\Lambda_{1}}}\right)\;T_{\rho}^{(\frac{3\pi}{2},\frac{\pi}{2}),{\Lambda_{0}},{\mathbb{I}}}\;\pi_{\mathop{\mathrm{bk}}}(A)=T_{\rho}^{(\frac{3\pi}{2},\frac{\pi}{2}),{\Lambda_{1}},V_{\rho,{\Lambda_{1}}}}\;\pi_{\mathop{\mathrm{bk}}}(A).\end{split}

(4.14)

This proves (iv), as in the proof of Lemma 2.14
[
26
]
, via the
\sigma
weak continuity above.

\square

Braidings are translated accordingly.

Lemma 4.6
.

Consider the setting in subsection
1.1
.
Assume Assumption
1.4
, Assumption
1.6
, Assumption
1.7
and Assumption
1.3
.
Let
(\Lambda_{0},\Lambda_{r0})\in\mathcal{PC}
.
Let
\epsilon_{-}^{(\Lambda_{0})}(\rho:\sigma)
be the braiding defined in Definition 4.12
[
26
]
for

\rho,\sigma\in O_{\mathop{\mathrm{bk},\Lambda_{0}}}
.
Then we have

F_{1}^{(\Lambda_{0},\Lambda_{r0})}\left(\epsilon_{-}^{(\Lambda_{0})}(\rho:\sigma)\right)=\iota^{(\Lambda_{0},\Lambda_{r0})}\left(F_{0}^{(\Lambda_{0},\Lambda_{r0})}(\rho):F_{0}^{(\Lambda_{0},\Lambda_{r0})}(\sigma)\right).

Let
{\Lambda_{1}},{\Lambda_{2}}\in{\mathcal{C}}_{{\mathop{\mathrm{bk}}}}^{U}={\mathcal{C}}_{(\frac{3\pi}{2},\frac{\pi}{2})}
with
{\Lambda_{1}},{\Lambda_{2}}\subset\mathop{\mathrm{H}_{U}}

such that
{\Lambda_{1}}\leftarrow_{(\frac{3\pi}{2},\frac{\pi}{2})}{\Lambda_{2}}
,

{\Lambda_{1}}\perp_{(\frac{3\pi}{2},\frac{\pi}{2})}{\Lambda_{2}}
(see Definition 2.6 Definition 4.8 of
[
26
]
).
Let
V_{\rho,{\Lambda_{1}}(t_{1})}\in{\mathcal{V}}_{\rho{\Lambda_{1}}(t_{1})}^{{\mathop{\mathrm{bk}}}}
,
t_{1}\geq 0
,

V_{\sigma,{\Lambda_{2}}(t_{2})}\in{\mathcal{V}}_{\sigma{\Lambda_{2}}(t_{2})}^{{\mathop{\mathrm{bk}}}}
,
t_{2}\geq 0
.
By Lemma
4.4
, they belong to
{\mathcal{F}}_{{\mathop{\mathrm{bk}}}}^{U}
.
By the Definition 4.12
[
26
]
,
we have

\displaystyle\epsilon_{-}^{(\Lambda_{0})}(\rho:\sigma)={\mathop{\mathrm{norm}}}-\lim_{t_{1},t_{2}\to\infty}\left(V_{\sigma,{\Lambda_{2}}(t_{2})}T_{\sigma}^{(\frac{3\pi}{2},\frac{\pi}{2}),{\Lambda_{0}},{\mathbb{I}}}\left(V_{\rho,{\Lambda_{1}}(t_{1})}\right)\right)^{*}V_{\rho,{\Lambda_{1}}(t_{1})}T_{\rho}^{(\frac{3\pi}{2},\frac{\pi}{2}),{\Lambda_{0}},{\mathbb{I}}}\left(V_{\sigma,{\Lambda_{2}}(t_{2})}\right).

(4.15)

We have
\epsilon_{-}^{(\Lambda_{0})}(\rho:\sigma)\in{\mathcal{F}}_{{\mathop{\mathrm{bk}}}}^{U}
because of Lemma
4.3
.
Applying
\tau
to this equation, from Lemma
4.5
, we have

\displaystyle\begin{split}&\tau\left(\epsilon_{-}^{(\Lambda_{0})}(\rho:\sigma)\right)\\
&={\mathop{\mathrm{norm}}}-\lim_{t_{1},t_{2}\to\infty}\left(\tau\left(V_{\sigma,{\Lambda_{2}}(t_{2})}\right)T_{F_{0}^{(\Lambda_{0},\Lambda_{r0})}(\sigma)}^{\mathrm{(l)}\Lambda_{r0}\mathbb{I}}\left(\tau\left(V_{\rho,{\Lambda_{1}}(t_{1})}\right)\right)\right)^{*}\tau\left(V_{\rho,{\Lambda_{1}}(t_{1})}\right)T_{F_{0}^{(\Lambda_{0},\Lambda_{r0})}(\rho)}^{\mathrm{(l)}\Lambda_{r0}\mathbb{I}}\left(\tau\left(V_{\sigma,{\Lambda_{2}}(t_{2})}\right)\right).\end{split}

(4.16)

Let
{\Lambda_{r2}}\in{\mathcal{C}}_{{\mathop{\mathrm{bd}}}}^{r}
with
{\Lambda_{1}}\leftarrow_{r}{\Lambda_{r2}}
,
\arg{\Lambda_{2}}\subset\arg{\Lambda_{r2}}
,
and let
{\Lambda_{r1}}\in{\mathcal{C}}_{{\mathop{\mathrm{bd}}}}^{r}
with
({\Lambda_{1}},{\Lambda_{r1}})\in\mathcal{PC}
.
Then by Lemma A.2 of
[
26
]
, for any
t\geq 0
, there exists
t_{0}(t)\geq 0

such that

{\Lambda_{2}}(t_{2})\subset{\Lambda_{r2}}(t)
for all
t_{2}\geq t_{0}(t)
, with
\lim_{t\to\infty}t_{0}(t)=\infty
.
From Lemma
4.5
, for such
t_{2}\geq t_{0}(t)
,

\tau\left(V_{\sigma,{\Lambda_{2}}(t_{2})}\right)\in{\mathcal{V}}_{F_{0}^{(\Lambda_{0},\Lambda_{r0})}(\sigma){\Lambda_{2}}(t_{2})}^{\mathop{\mathrm{bu}}}\subset{\mathcal{V}}_{F_{0}^{(\Lambda_{0},\Lambda_{r0})}(\sigma){\Lambda_{r2}}(t)}^{{\mathop{\mathrm{bd}}}U}.

Hence we have

\displaystyle\begin{split}&\tau\left(\epsilon_{-}^{(\Lambda_{0})}(\rho:\sigma)\right)\\
&={\mathop{\mathrm{norm}}}-\lim_{t_{1},t\to\infty}\left(\tau\left(V_{\sigma,{\Lambda_{2}}(t_{0}(t))}\right)T_{F_{0}^{(\Lambda_{0},\Lambda_{r0})}(\sigma)}^{\mathrm{(l)}\Lambda_{r0}\mathbb{I}}\left(\tau\left(V_{\rho,{\Lambda_{1}}(t_{1})}\right)\right)\right)^{*}\tau\left(V_{\rho,{\Lambda_{1}}(t_{1})}\right)T_{F_{0}^{(\Lambda_{0},\Lambda_{r0})}(\rho)}^{\mathrm{(l)}\Lambda_{r0}\mathbb{I}}\left(\tau\left(V_{\sigma,{\Lambda_{2}}(t_{0}(t))}\right)\right)\\
&=\iota^{(\Lambda_{0},\Lambda_{r0})}\left(F_{0}^{(\Lambda_{0},\Lambda_{r0})}(\rho):F_{0}^{(\Lambda_{0},\Lambda_{r0})}(\sigma)\right).\end{split}

(4.17)

\square

In
[
26
]
, we showed that
O_{\mathop{\mathrm{bk},\Lambda_{0}}}
with their morphisms
(\rho,\sigma)
,

\rho,\sigma\in O_{\mathop{\mathrm{bk},\Lambda_{0}}}

forms a braided
C^{*}
-tensor category
C_{\mathop{\mathrm{bk},\Lambda_{0}}}
.
It is now easy to see we have a functor from this bulk theory to our boundary theory.

Theorem 4.7
.

Let
(\Lambda_{0},\Lambda_{r0})\in\mathcal{PC}
.
Consider the setting in subsection
1.1
, and
assume Assumption
1.4
, Assumption
1.6
, Assumption
1.7
,
Assumption
1.3
, Assumption
1.9
.
(Note from Lemma
1.12
, Assumption
1.10
automatically holds.)
Then
F^{(\Lambda_{0},\Lambda_{r0})}:C_{\mathop{\mathrm{bk},\Lambda_{0}}}\to C_{\mathop{\mathrm{bd}},\Lambda_{0}}^{\mathop{\mathrm{bu}}}
given by

F^{(\Lambda_{0},\Lambda_{r0})}_{0}
(
4.10
),
F^{(\Lambda_{0},\Lambda_{r0})}_{1}
(
4.11
)
gives a fully faithful unitary braided tensor functor.

Example 4.8
.

Let us consider the map
F^{(\Lambda_{0},\Lambda_{r0})}_{0}

for the Toric code.
Let
\gamma^{\prime}
be an infinite path in
\Lambda_{0}
on the lattice and
consider endomorphism
\rho_{\gamma^{\prime}}^{Z}
in Example
1.2
.
Then we have

\displaystyle\begin{split}F^{(\Lambda_{0},\Lambda_{r0})}_{0}\left(\pi_{\mathop{\mathrm{bk}}}\rho_{\gamma^{\prime}}^{Z}\right)=\tau\;T_{\pi_{\mathop{\mathrm{bk}}}\rho_{\gamma^{\prime}}^{Z}}^{(\frac{3\pi}{2},\frac{\pi}{2}),{\Lambda_{0}},{\mathbb{I}}}\;\tau^{-1}\pi_{\mathop{\mathrm{bd}}}=\tau\;T_{\pi_{\mathop{\mathrm{bk}}}\rho_{\gamma^{\prime}}^{Z}}^{(\frac{3\pi}{2},\frac{\pi}{2}),{\Lambda_{0}},{\mathbb{I}}}\;\pi_{\mathop{\mathrm{bk}}}=\tau\pi_{\mathop{\mathrm{bk}}}\rho_{\gamma^{\prime}}^{Z}=\pi_{\mathop{\mathrm{bd}}}\rho_{\gamma^{\prime}}^{Z},\end{split}

(4.18)

matching with
[
28
]
.

5
A boundary to bulk map

In this section, we construct an inverse of the map constructed in the previous section.

Lemma 5.1
.

Consider the setting in subsection
1.1
, and assume Assumption
1.3
, Assumption
1.4
,
and Assumption
1.14
.
Then for any
\rho\in O^{rU}_{\mathop{\mathrm{bd}}}
, there exists a unique
*
-homomorphism

G(\rho):{\mathcal{A}}_{\mathbb{Z}^{2}}\to{\mathcal{B}}({\mathcal{H}}_{\mathop{\mathrm{bk}}})
such that

\displaystyle G(\rho)(A)=\tau^{-1}\rho(A),\quad A\in{\mathcal{A}}_{\mathop{\mathrm{H}_{U}}},

(5.1)

\displaystyle G(\rho)(B)=\pi_{\mathop{\mathrm{bk}}}(B),\quad B\in{\mathcal{A}}_{\mathop{\mathrm{H}_{D}}}.

(5.2)

If furthermore
\rho\in O_{\mathop{\mathrm{bd}}}^{\mathop{\mathrm{bu}}}
, then

G(\rho)\in O_{\mathop{\mathrm{bk}}}^{U}=O_{\mathop{\mathrm{bk}}}
.
In particular, for
(\Lambda_{0},\Lambda_{r0})\in\mathcal{PC}
,
\rho\in O_{\mathop{\mathrm{bd}},\Lambda_{0}}^{\mathop{\mathrm{bu}}}
, we have

G(\rho)\in O_{\mathop{\mathrm{bk},\Lambda_{0}}}^{U}=O_{\mathop{\mathrm{bk},\Lambda_{0}}}
, and
F_{0}^{{(\Lambda_{0},\Lambda_{r0})}}\left(G(\rho)\right)=\rho
.
For
\hat{\rho}\in O_{\mathop{\mathrm{bk},\Lambda_{0}}}^{U}=O_{\mathop{\mathrm{bk},\Lambda_{0}}}
, we have

G(F_{0}^{(\Lambda_{0},\Lambda_{r0})}(\hat{\rho}))=\hat{\rho}
.
The map preserves tensor i.e.,
G(\rho\otimes\sigma)=G(\rho)\otimes G(\sigma)

for any
\rho,\sigma\in O_{\mathop{\mathrm{bd}},\Lambda_{0}}^{\mathop{\mathrm{bu}}}
.

Let
(\Lambda_{0},\Lambda_{r0})\in\mathcal{PC}
and
\rho\in O^{rU}_{\mathop{\mathrm{bd}}}
,
V_{\rho,\Lambda_{r0}}\in{\mathcal{V}}_{\rho\Lambda_{r0}}^{{\mathop{\mathrm{bd}}}U}
.
Note that for any
A\in{\mathcal{A}}_{\mathop{\mathrm{H}_{U}}}
,

\rho(A)=\mathop{\mathrm{Ad}}\nolimits\left(V_{\rho,\Lambda_{r0}}^{*}\right)T_{\rho}^{\mathrm{(l)}\Lambda_{r0}V_{{\rho},\Lambda_{r0}}}\pi_{\mathop{\mathrm{bd}}}(A)\in{\mathcal{F}}_{{\mathop{\mathrm{bd}}}}^{U}.

Therefore,
\tau^{-1}\rho(A)
is well-defined.
Hence there is a representation

G_{1}(\rho)
( resp.
G_{2}(\rho)
) of
{\mathcal{A}}_{\mathop{\mathrm{H}_{U}}}
(resp
{\mathcal{A}}_{\mathop{\mathrm{H}_{D}}}
)
on
{\mathcal{H}}_{\mathop{\mathrm{bk}}}
, given by the right hand side of
(
5.1
)( resp. (
5.2
)). Because
G_{1}(\rho)({\mathcal{A}}_{\mathop{\mathrm{H}_{U}}})\subset{\mathcal{F}}_{{\mathop{\mathrm{bk}}}}^{U}

and
{\mathcal{F}}_{{\mathop{\mathrm{bk}}}}^{U}
commutes with
\pi_{\mathop{\mathrm{bk}}}({\mathcal{A}}_{\mathop{\mathrm{H}_{D}}})

by definition, the range of
G_{1}(\rho)
and
G_{2}(\rho)
commutes.
Because
{\mathcal{A}}_{\mathop{\mathrm{H}_{U}}}
,
{\mathcal{A}}_{\mathop{\mathrm{H}_{D}}}
are nuclear,
there exists a unique representation
G(\rho):{\mathcal{A}}_{\mathbb{Z}^{2}}\to{\mathcal{B}}({\mathcal{H}}_{\mathop{\mathrm{bk}}})

such that
G(\rho)|_{{\mathcal{A}}_{\mathop{\mathrm{H}_{U}}}}=G_{1}(\rho)
,
G(\rho)|_{{\mathcal{A}}_{\mathop{\mathrm{H}_{D}}}}=G_{2}(\rho)
.
(Proposition 4.7 IV of
[
27
]
.)

Now suppose that
\rho\in O_{\mathop{\mathrm{bd}}}^{\mathop{\mathrm{bu}}}
.
For any
{\Lambda}\in{\mathcal{C}}_{{\mathop{\mathrm{bk}}}}^{U}
, take
V_{\rho,{\Lambda}}\in{\mathcal{V}}_{\rho{\Lambda}}^{\mathop{\mathrm{bu}}}\subset{\mathcal{F}}_{{\mathop{\mathrm{bd}}}}^{U}
.
Then for any
A\in{\mathcal{A}}_{\Lambda^{c}\cap\mathop{\mathrm{H}_{U}}}
,

\displaystyle\begin{split}&\mathop{\mathrm{Ad}}\nolimits\left(\tau^{-1}\left(V_{\rho,{\Lambda}}\right)\right)G(\rho)(A)=\mathop{\mathrm{Ad}}\nolimits\left(\tau^{-1}\left(V_{\rho,{\Lambda}}\right)\right)\left(\tau^{-1}\rho(A)\right)\\
&=\tau^{-1}\left(\mathop{\mathrm{Ad}}\nolimits\left(\left(V_{\rho,{\Lambda}}\right)\left(\rho(A)\right)\right)\right)=\tau^{-1}\left(\pi_{\mathop{\mathrm{bd}}}(A)\right)=\pi_{\mathop{\mathrm{bk}}}(A).\end{split}

(5.3)

For any
B\in{\mathcal{A}}_{\mathop{\mathrm{H}_{D}}}
, we have

\displaystyle\mathop{\mathrm{Ad}}\nolimits\left(\tau^{-1}\left(V_{\rho,{\Lambda}}\right)\right)G(\rho)(B)=\mathop{\mathrm{Ad}}\nolimits\left(\tau^{-1}\left(V_{\rho,{\Lambda}}\right)\right)\left(\pi_{\mathop{\mathrm{bk}}}(B)\right)=\pi_{\mathop{\mathrm{bk}}}(B),

(5.4)

because
\pi_{\mathop{\mathrm{bk}}}(B)\in{{\mathcal{F}}_{{\mathop{\mathrm{bk}}}}^{U}}^{\prime}
.
Combining these, we obtain

\displaystyle\left.\mathop{\mathrm{Ad}}\nolimits\left(\tau^{-1}\left(V_{\rho,{\Lambda}}\right)\right)G(\rho)\right|_{{\mathcal{A}}_{\Lambda^{c}}}=\left.\pi_{\mathop{\mathrm{bk}}}\right|_{{\mathcal{A}}_{\Lambda^{c}}}

(5.5)

for any
\Lambda\in{\mathcal{C}}_{{\mathop{\mathrm{bk}}}}^{U}
, proving
G(\rho)\in O_{\mathop{\mathrm{bk}}}^{U}
.

If
(\Lambda_{0},\Lambda_{r0})\in\mathcal{PC}
,
\rho\in O_{\mathop{\mathrm{bd}},\Lambda_{0}}^{\mathop{\mathrm{bu}}}
, then we may take
V_{\rho,\Lambda_{0}}=\mathbb{I}
above.
Hence
G(\rho)\in O_{\mathop{\mathrm{bk},\Lambda_{0}}}^{U}=O_{\mathop{\mathrm{bk},\Lambda_{0}}}
.
For any
A\in\mathop{\mathrm{H}_{U}}
, we have

\displaystyle\begin{split}F_{0}^{(\Lambda_{0},\Lambda_{r0})}\left(G(\rho)\right)(A)=\tau T_{G(\rho)}^{(\frac{3\pi}{2},\frac{\pi}{2}),{\Lambda_{0}},{\mathbb{I}}}\tau^{-1}\pi_{\mathop{\mathrm{bd}}}(A)=\tau G(\rho)(A)=\rho(A).\end{split}

(5.6)

Hence we have
F_{0}^{(\Lambda_{0},\Lambda_{r0})}\left(G(\rho)\right)=\rho
.

For
\hat{\rho}\in O_{\mathop{\mathrm{bk},\Lambda_{0}}}^{U}=O_{\mathop{\mathrm{bk},\Lambda_{0}}}
, we have

\displaystyle\begin{split}&G(F_{0}^{(\Lambda_{0},\Lambda_{r0})}(\hat{\rho}))\left(A\right)=\tau^{-1}F_{0}^{(\Lambda_{0},\Lambda_{r0})}\left(\hat{\rho}\right)(A)=\tau^{-1}\tau T_{\hat{\rho}}^{(\frac{3\pi}{2},\frac{\pi}{2}),{\Lambda_{0}},{\mathbb{I}}}\tau^{-1}\pi_{\mathop{\mathrm{bd}}}(A)\\
&=T_{\hat{\rho}}^{(\frac{3\pi}{2},\frac{\pi}{2}),{\Lambda_{0}},{\mathbb{I}}}\pi_{\mathop{\mathrm{bk}}}(A)=\hat{\rho}(A),\quad A\in{\mathcal{A}}_{\mathop{\mathrm{H}_{U}}},\\
&G(F_{0}^{(\Lambda_{0},\Lambda_{r0})}(\hat{\rho}))(B)=\pi_{\mathop{\mathrm{bk}}}(B)=\hat{\rho}(B),\quad B\in{\mathcal{A}}_{\mathop{\mathrm{H}_{D}}}.\end{split}

(5.7)

Hence
we have

G(F_{0}^{(\Lambda_{0},\Lambda_{r0})}(\hat{\rho}))=\hat{\rho}
.

For any
\rho,\sigma\in O_{\mathop{\mathrm{bd}},\Lambda_{0}}^{\mathop{\mathrm{bu}}}
,
from above and Theorem
4.7
, we have

\displaystyle\begin{split}F_{0}^{(\Lambda_{0},\Lambda_{r0})}G(\rho\otimes\sigma)=\rho\otimes\sigma=F_{0}^{(\Lambda_{0},\Lambda_{r0})}G(\rho)\otimes F_{0}^{(\Lambda_{0},\Lambda_{r0})}G(\sigma)=F_{0}^{(\Lambda_{0},\Lambda_{r0})}\left(G(\rho)\otimes G(\sigma)\right).\end{split}

(5.8)

Applying
G
to this, we have

\displaystyle\begin{split}G(\rho\otimes\sigma)=GF_{0}^{(\Lambda_{0},\Lambda_{r0})}\left(G(\rho)\otimes G(\sigma)\right)=G(\rho)\otimes G(\sigma).\end{split}

(5.9)

This proves the last statement.

\square

Hence we obtain the following.

Corollary 5.2
.

Consider the setting in subsection
1.1
, and
assume Assumption
1.4
, Assumption
1.6
, Assumption
1.7
,
Assumption
1.3
, Assumption
1.9
and Assumption
1.14
.
The functor
F^{(\Lambda_{0},\Lambda_{r0})}
in Theorem
4.7
is a braided monoidal equivalence
between braided
C^{*}
-tensor categories.

The functor
F^{(\Lambda_{0},\Lambda_{r0})}
is also essentially surjective from Lemma
5.1
.
Hence it is an equivalence.

\square

In a word, there is a copy of the bulk theory at the boundary.

Remark 5.3
.

From this Corollary and the fact that the vacuum and the
m
-particle are not equivalent
in the bulk, we conclude that the
\pi_{\mathop{\mathrm{bd}}}
and
\pi_{\gamma}^{X}
in Example

1.2
are not equivalent with respect to

\simeq_{{\mathcal{U}}}
.

6
Bulk-boundary correspondence

In this section, we construct a tensor category
{\mathcal{M}}

by adding to
C_{\mathop{\mathrm{bd}},\Lambda_{0}}^{\mathop{\mathrm{bu}}}
morphisms from
{\mathcal{B}}_{l}
.
By taking the idempotent completion of
{\mathcal{M}}
,
we obtain a
C^{*}
-tensor category
\tilde{\mathcal{M}}
.
We introduce
the Drinfeld center with an asymptotic constraint

{\mathcal{Z}}_{a}(\tilde{\mathcal{M}})
of
\tilde{\mathcal{M}}
.
This is the same as the usual Drinfeld center
[
11
]
, except that the half-braidings
are required to satisfy the asymptotic property we saw in Lemma
3.20
.

Note that in Corollary
5.2
, we did not assume
the gap condition on the boundary.
In this section we introduce a condition that we expect to be related to the gap condition:
the
Assumption
6.11
.
It can be interpreted as a kind of non-triviality of the braiding.
Under this condition,

{\mathcal{Z}}_{a}(\tilde{\mathcal{M}})
gets a braided
C^{*}
-tensor category that is
equivalent to the bulk category
C_{\mathop{\mathrm{bk}}}
.

Definition 6.1
.

Consider the setting in subsection
1.1
.
Let
