(\Lambda_{0},\Lambda_{r0})\in\mathcal{PC}
.
Let
{\mathcal{M}}
be a category with object
{\mathop{\mathrm{Obj}}}{\mathcal{M}}:=O_{\mathop{\mathrm{bd}},\Lambda_{0}}^{\mathop{\mathrm{bu}}}
,
and morphisms between objects
\rho,\sigma\in{\mathop{\mathrm{Obj}}}{\mathcal{M}}:=O_{\mathop{\mathrm{bd}},\Lambda_{0}}^{\mathop{\mathrm{bu}}}

defined by

{\mathop{\mathrm{Mor}}}_{{\mathcal{M}}}(\rho,\sigma):=(\rho,\sigma)_{l}
.
Then
{\mathcal{M}}
is a strict tensor category with respect to the tensor
\otimes

of
C_{\mathop{\mathrm{bd}},\Lambda_{0}}^{\mathop{\mathrm{bu}}}
.

That
{\mathcal{M}}
is a strict tensor category
follows from the fact that
C_{\mathop{\mathrm{bd}},\Lambda_{0}}^{\mathop{\mathrm{bu}}}
is closed under tensor
and
C_{\mathop{\mathrm{bd}},\Lambda_{0}}^{\mathop{\mathrm{bu}}}
is a subcategory of
C^{r}_{\mathop{\mathrm{bd},\Lambda_{r0}}}
.
The tensor category
{\mathcal{M}}
is not necessarily a
C^{*}
-tensor category in the sense that there may not be subobjects.
By taking the idempotent completion, we obtain a
C^{*}
-tensor category

\tilde{\mathcal{M}}
.
Idempotent completion is a well-known procedure. See, for example the Appendix of

[
20
]
.
We include the proof for the reader’s convenience.

Proposition 6.2
.

Let
(\Lambda_{0},\Lambda_{r0})\in\mathcal{PC}
.
Consider the setting in subsection
1.1
, and
assume
Assumption
1.10
.
Let
\tilde{\mathcal{M}}
be a category with objects

\displaystyle\begin{split}{\mathop{\mathrm{Obj}}}\tilde{\mathcal{M}}:=\left\{(\rho,p)\mid\rho\in{\mathop{\mathrm{Obj}}}{\mathcal{M}}=O_{\mathop{\mathrm{bd}},\Lambda_{0}}^{\mathop{\mathrm{bu}}},\quad p\in(\rho,\rho)_{l}\;\;\text{projection}\right\},\end{split}

(6.1)

and
morphisms

\displaystyle\begin{split}{\mathop{\mathrm{Mor}}}_{\tilde{\mathcal{M}}}\left((\rho,p),(\sigma,q)\right):=\left\{R\in(\rho,\sigma)_{l}\mid qR=R=Rp\right\}\quad\text{for}\quad(\rho,p),(\sigma,q)\in{\mathop{\mathrm{Obj}}}\tilde{\mathcal{M}}.\end{split}

(6.2)

The category
\tilde{\mathcal{M}}
is a
C^{*}
-category with
identity

\displaystyle\begin{split}\mathop{\mathrm{id}}\nolimits_{(\rho,p)}:=p,\quad\text{for each}\quad(\rho,p)\in{\mathop{\mathrm{Obj}}}\tilde{\mathcal{M}}\end{split}

(6.3)

composition of morphisms

\displaystyle\begin{split}{\mathop{\mathrm{Mor}}}_{\tilde{\mathcal{M}}}\left((\sigma,q),(\gamma,r)\right)\times{\mathop{\mathrm{Mor}}}_{\tilde{\mathcal{M}}}\left((\rho,p),(\sigma,q)\right)\ni(S,R)\mapsto SR\in{\mathop{\mathrm{Mor}}}_{\tilde{\mathcal{M}}}\left((\rho,p),(\gamma,r)\right),\end{split}

(6.4)

and an antilinear contravariant functor
*:\tilde{\mathcal{M}}\to\tilde{\mathcal{M}}

given by the adjoint
*
of
{\mathcal{B}}({\mathcal{H}}_{\mathop{\mathrm{bd}}})

for morphisms

\displaystyle\begin{split}{\mathop{\mathrm{Mor}}}_{\tilde{\mathcal{M}}}\left((\rho,p),(\sigma,q)\right)\ni T\to T^{*}\in{\mathop{\mathrm{Mor}}}_{\tilde{\mathcal{M}}}\left((\sigma,q),(\rho,p)\right).\end{split}

(6.5)

Furthermore,
\tilde{\mathcal{M}}
is a
C^{*}
-tensor category
with the tensor

\displaystyle\begin{split}(\rho,p)\otimes_{\tilde{M}}(\sigma,q):=(\rho\otimes\sigma,p\otimes q),\quad\text{for}\quad(\rho,p),(\sigma,q)\in{\mathop{\mathrm{Obj}}}\tilde{\mathcal{M}}\end{split}

(6.6)

and

\displaystyle\begin{split}&R\otimes_{\tilde{M}}S:=R\otimes S\in{\mathop{\mathrm{Mor}}}_{\tilde{\mathcal{M}}}\left((\rho,p)\otimes_{\tilde{M}}(\sigma,q),(\rho^{\prime},p^{\prime})\otimes_{\tilde{M}}(\sigma^{\prime},q^{\prime})\right)\\
&\text{for}\quad R\in{\mathop{\mathrm{Mor}}}_{\tilde{\mathcal{M}}}\left((\rho,p),(\rho^{\prime},p^{\prime})\right),\quad S\in{\mathop{\mathrm{Mor}}}_{\tilde{\mathcal{M}}}\left((\sigma,q),(\sigma^{\prime},q^{\prime})\right).\end{split}

(6.7)

The tensor unit is
(\pi_{\mathop{\mathrm{bd}}},\mathbb{I})
.

Clearly, each
{\mathop{\mathrm{Mor}}}_{\tilde{\mathcal{M}}}\left((\rho,p),(\sigma,q)\right)
is a Banach space
with the norm inherited from
{\mathcal{B}}({\mathcal{H}}_{\mathop{\mathrm{bd}}})
.
In (
6.4
), we have
SR\in{\mathop{\mathrm{Mor}}}_{\tilde{\mathcal{M}}}\left((\rho,p),(\gamma,r)\right)

because
SR\in(\rho,\gamma)_{l}
and
rSR=SR=SRp
.
The map (
6.4
) is bilinear and we have
\left\|SR\right\|\leq\left\|S\right\|\left\|R\right\|
.
The associativity of the composition follows from that of
{\mathcal{B}}({\mathcal{H}}_{\mathop{\mathrm{bd}}})
.
In (
6.5
), we have
T^{*}\in{\mathop{\mathrm{Mor}}}_{\tilde{\mathcal{M}}}\left((\sigma,q),(\rho,p)\right)

because
T^{*}\in(\sigma,\rho)_{l}
and
pT^{*}=T^{*}=T^{*}q
.
That
T^{**}=T
,
\left\|T^{*}T\right\|=\left\|T\right\|^{2}
,

T^{*}T\geq 0
follows from the corresponding properties of
{\mathcal{B}}({\mathcal{H}}_{\mathop{\mathrm{bd}}})
.
The formula (
6.3
) gives the identity morphism :
clearly, for each object
(\rho,p)\in{\mathop{\mathrm{Obj}}}\tilde{\mathcal{M}}
, we have

\mathop{\mathrm{id}}\nolimits_{(\rho,p)}:=p\in{\mathop{\mathrm{Mor}}}_{\tilde{\mathcal{M}}}\left((\rho,p),(\rho,p)\right)

because
p\in(\rho,\rho)_{l}
and
pp=p=pp
.
Furthermore, for any

R\in{\mathop{\mathrm{Mor}}}_{\tilde{\mathcal{M}}}\left((\rho,p),(\sigma,q)\right)

we have
R\circ\mathop{\mathrm{id}}\nolimits_{(\rho,p)}=Rp=R=qR=\mathop{\mathrm{id}}\nolimits_{(\sigma,q)}\circ R
.
Hence,
\tilde{\mathcal{M}}
is a
C^{*}
-category.

The formula in (
6.6
)
gives an object of
\tilde{\mathcal{M}}
because

\rho\otimes\sigma\in{\mathop{\mathrm{Obj}}}{\mathcal{M}}=O_{\mathop{\mathrm{bd}},\Lambda_{0}}^{\mathop{\mathrm{bu}}}

and
p\otimes q\in(\rho\otimes\sigma,\rho\otimes\sigma)_{l}

with

\displaystyle\begin{split}(p\otimes q)^{*}=p^{*}\otimes q^{*}=p\otimes q,\quad(p\otimes q)^{2}=p^{2}\otimes q^{2}=p\otimes q,\end{split}

(6.8)

by Lemma
2.5
.
The formula in (
6.7
) is well-defined because

R\otimes_{\tilde{M}}S:=R\otimes S\in(\rho\otimes\sigma,\rho^{\prime}\otimes\sigma^{\prime})_{l}

and

\displaystyle\begin{split}(p^{\prime}\otimes q^{\prime})(R\otimes S)=p^{\prime}R\otimes q^{\prime}S=R\otimes S=Rp\otimes Sq=(R\otimes S)(p\otimes q),\end{split}

(6.9)

by Lemma
2.5
.
If we further have

R^{\prime}\in{\mathop{\mathrm{Mor}}}_{\tilde{\mathcal{M}}}\left((\rho^{\prime},p^{\prime}),(\rho^{\prime\prime},p^{\prime\prime})\right)
,

S^{\prime}\in{\mathop{\mathrm{Mor}}}_{\tilde{\mathcal{M}}}\left((\sigma^{\prime},q^{\prime}),(\sigma^{\prime\prime},q^{\prime\prime})\right)
,
then we have

\displaystyle\begin{split}&\left(R^{\prime}\otimes_{\tilde{M}}S^{\prime}\right)\circ\left(R\otimes_{\tilde{M}}S\right)=\left(R^{\prime}\otimes S^{\prime}\right)\left(R\otimes S\right)=R^{\prime}R\otimes S^{\prime}S=R^{\prime}R\otimes_{\tilde{M}}S^{\prime}S,\\
&\left(R\otimes_{\tilde{\mathcal{M}}}S\right)^{*}=\left(R\otimes S\right)^{*}=R^{*}\otimes S^{*}=R^{*}\otimes_{\tilde{\mathcal{M}}}S^{*},\end{split}

(6.10)

by Lemma
2.5
.
We also have

\displaystyle\begin{split}\mathop{\mathrm{id}}\nolimits_{(\rho,p)\otimes_{\tilde{M}}(\sigma,q)}=\mathop{\mathrm{id}}\nolimits_{(\rho\otimes\sigma,p\otimes q)}=p\otimes q=p\otimes_{\tilde{\mathcal{M}}}q=\mathop{\mathrm{id}}\nolimits_{(\rho,p)}\otimes_{\tilde{\mathcal{M}}}\mathop{\mathrm{id}}\nolimits_{(\sigma,\rho)}.\end{split}

(6.11)

As the associativity morphism, we take

\displaystyle\begin{split}&\alpha_{(\rho,p),(\sigma,q),(\gamma,r)}:=(p\otimes q)\otimes r\\
&\in{\mathop{\mathrm{Mor}}}_{\tilde{\mathcal{M}}}\left(\left((\rho,p)\otimes_{\tilde{\mathcal{M}}}(\sigma,q)\right)\otimes_{\tilde{\mathcal{M}}}(\gamma,r),(\rho,p)\otimes_{\tilde{\mathcal{M}}}\left((\sigma,q)\otimes_{\tilde{\mathcal{M}}}(\gamma,r)\right)\right)\\
&\quad={\mathop{\mathrm{Mor}}}_{\tilde{\mathcal{M}}}\left(\left(\rho\otimes\sigma\otimes\gamma,p\otimes q\otimes r\right),\left(\rho\otimes\sigma\otimes\gamma,p\otimes q\otimes r\right)\right).\end{split}

(6.12)

In fact, we have

\displaystyle\begin{split}\alpha_{(\rho,p),(\sigma,q),(\gamma,r)}:=p\otimes q\otimes r\in(\rho\otimes\sigma\otimes\gamma,\rho\otimes\sigma\otimes\gamma)_{l}\end{split}

(6.13)

and

\displaystyle\begin{split}&\left(p\otimes q\otimes r\right)\alpha_{(\rho,p),(\sigma,q),(\gamma,r)}=\left(p\otimes q\otimes r\right)^{2}=\left(p\otimes q\otimes r\right)=\alpha_{(\rho,p),(\sigma,q),(\gamma,r)},\\
&\alpha_{(\rho,p),(\sigma,q),(\gamma,r)}\left(p\otimes q\otimes r\right)=\left(p\otimes q\otimes r\right)^{2}=\left(p\otimes q\otimes r\right)=\alpha_{(\rho,p),(\sigma,q),(\gamma,r)}.\end{split}

(6.14)

In fact it is an isomorphism because
\mathop{\mathrm{id}}\nolimits_{\left(\rho\otimes\sigma\otimes\gamma,p\otimes q\otimes r\right)}=p\otimes q\otimes r
.
The naturality of
\alpha
and the Pentagon axiom follows from
the definition and Lemma
2.5
.
The object
(\pi_{\mathop{\mathrm{bd}}},\mathbb{I})
is the tensor unit with
left/right unit constraints

\displaystyle\begin{split}&l_{(\rho,p)}:=p\in{\mathop{\mathrm{Mor}}}_{\tilde{\mathcal{M}}}\left((\pi_{\mathop{\mathrm{bd}}},\mathbb{I})\otimes_{\tilde{\mathcal{M}}}(\rho,p),(\rho,p)\right)={\mathop{\mathrm{Mor}}}_{\tilde{\mathcal{M}}}\left((\rho,p),(\rho,p)\right),\\
&r_{(\rho,p)}:=p\in{\mathop{\mathrm{Mor}}}_{\tilde{\mathcal{M}}}\left((\rho,p)\otimes_{\tilde{\mathcal{M}}}(\pi_{\mathop{\mathrm{bd}}},\mathbb{I}),(\rho,p)\right)={\mathop{\mathrm{Mor}}}_{\tilde{\mathcal{M}}}\left((\rho,p),(\rho,p)\right).\end{split}

(6.15)

Their naturalities follow from the definition of morphisms and identity morphisms.The triangle axiom
follows by

\displaystyle\begin{split}\left(\mathop{\mathrm{id}}\nolimits_{(\rho,p)}\otimes_{\tilde{\mathcal{M}}}l_{(\sigma,q)}\right)\alpha_{(p,\rho),(\pi_{\mathop{\mathrm{bd}}},\mathbb{I}),(\sigma,q)}=\left(p\otimes q\right)\left(p\otimes\mathbb{I}\otimes q\right)=\left(p\otimes q\right)=r_{(\rho,p)}\otimes_{\tilde{\mathcal{M}}}\mathop{\mathrm{id}}\nolimits_{(\sigma,q)}.\end{split}

(6.16)

Now we construct a direct sum for

(\rho,p),(\sigma,q)\in{\mathop{\mathrm{Obj}}}\tilde{\mathcal{M}}
.
By Lemma
2.6
, there exist
\gamma\in{\mathop{\mathrm{Obj}}}{\mathcal{M}}=O_{\mathop{\mathrm{bd}},\Lambda_{0}}^{\mathop{\mathrm{bu}}}

and isometries
u\in(\rho,\gamma)_{U}
,
v\in(\sigma,\gamma)_{U}

such that
uu^{*}+vv^{*}=\mathbb{I}
.
We set
r:=upu^{*}+vqv^{*}
, which is a projection in
(\gamma,\gamma)_{l}
.
Hence we have
(\gamma,r)\in{\mathop{\mathrm{Obj}}}\tilde{\mathcal{M}}
.
We have
up\in{\mathop{\mathrm{Mor}}}_{\tilde{\mathcal{M}}}\left((\rho,p),(\gamma,r)\right)

because
up\in(\rho,\gamma)_{l}
and
rup=up=upp
.
It is an isometry because
pu^{*}up=p=\mathop{\mathrm{id}}\nolimits_{(\rho,p)}
.
Similarly, we have
vq\in{\mathop{\mathrm{Mor}}}_{\tilde{\mathcal{M}}}\left((\sigma,q),(\gamma,r)\right)

and it is an isometry.
We also have
upu^{*}+vqv^{*}=r=\mathop{\mathrm{id}}\nolimits_{(\gamma,r)}
.
Hence we obtain the direct sum.

Next we introduce a subobject of
(\rho,p)\in{\mathop{\mathrm{Obj}}}\tilde{\mathcal{M}}

with respect to a projection
q\in{\mathop{\mathrm{Mor}}}_{\tilde{\mathcal{M}}}\left((\rho,p),(\rho,p)\right)
.
We have
(\rho,q)\in{\mathop{\mathrm{Obj}}}\left({\tilde{\mathcal{M}}}\right)
because
\rho\in{\mathop{\mathrm{Obj}}}({\mathcal{M}})
,

q\in(\rho,\rho)_{l}
a projection.
Because
q\in{\mathop{\mathrm{Mor}}}_{\tilde{\mathcal{M}}}\left((\rho,p),(\rho,p)\right)
, we have

pq=q=qp
.
Note also that
q\in{\mathop{\mathrm{Mor}}}_{\tilde{\mathcal{M}}}\left((\rho,q),(\rho,p)\right)

because
pq=q=qq
.
It is an isometry because
q^{*}q=q=\mathop{\mathrm{id}}\nolimits_{(\rho,q)}
.
We also have
qq^{*}=q
. Hence
(\rho,q)\in{\mathop{\mathrm{Obj}}}\left({\tilde{\mathcal{M}}}\right)
and

q\in{\mathop{\mathrm{Mor}}}_{\tilde{\mathcal{M}}}\left((\rho,q),(\rho,p)\right)

gives the subobject.
Finally, we have
{\mathop{\mathrm{Mor}}}_{\tilde{\mathcal{M}}}\left((\pi_{\mathop{\mathrm{bd}}},\mathbb{I}),(\pi_{\mathop{\mathrm{bd}}},\mathbb{I})\right)=(\pi_{\mathop{\mathrm{bd}}},\pi_{\mathop{\mathrm{bd}}})_{l}={\mathbb{C}}\mathbb{I}

and complete the proof.

\square

We regard this
\tilde{\mathcal{M}}
our boundary theory.

Remark 6.3
.

Note that
C_{\mathop{\mathrm{bd}},\Lambda_{0}}^{\mathop{\mathrm{bu}}}
acts on
\tilde{\mathcal{M}}
by

(\rho,\mathbb{I})\otimes_{\tilde{\mathcal{M}}}
.
By Corollary
5.2
, we may identify
C_{\mathop{\mathrm{bd}},\Lambda_{0}}^{\mathop{\mathrm{bu}}}

and the bulk theory
C_{\mathop{\mathrm{bk},\Lambda_{0}}}
.
Hence the boundary theory
\tilde{\mathcal{M}}
is a left module category
over the bulk theory
C_{\mathop{\mathrm{bk},\Lambda_{0}}}
.

Let
(\rho,p)\in{\mathop{\mathrm{Obj}}}\left({\tilde{\mathcal{M}}}\right)
with a non-trivial projection
p\in{\mathcal{B}}_{l}
.
Suppose that no
\gamma\in O_{\mathop{\mathrm{bd}},\Lambda_{0}}^{\mathop{\mathrm{bu}}}
can “detect” the projection
p\in{\mathcal{B}}_{l}
, i.e.,

\displaystyle T_{\gamma}^{\mathrm{(l)}\Lambda_{r0}\mathbb{I}}(p)=p,\quad\text{for all}\quad\gamma\in O_{\mathop{\mathrm{bd}},\Lambda_{0}}^{\mathop{\mathrm{bu}}}.

(6.17)

Then for any
\gamma\in O_{\mathop{\mathrm{bd}},\Lambda_{0}}^{\mathop{\mathrm{bu}}}
, we have

\displaystyle\begin{split}(\gamma,\mathbb{I})\otimes_{\tilde{\mathcal{M}}}(\rho,p)=(\gamma\otimes\rho,\mathbb{I}_{\gamma}\otimes p)=(\gamma\otimes\rho,p).\end{split}

(6.18)

Hence, the space of
{\mathop{\mathrm{Obj}}}\left({\tilde{\mathcal{M}}}\right)
given by elements of the form

(-,p)\in{\mathop{\mathrm{Obj}}}\left({\tilde{\mathcal{M}}}\right)
is invariant under the action of
C_{\mathop{\mathrm{bk},\Lambda_{0}}}
.
If stable gapped boundary is given by an irreducible left module
[
14
]
, this should not happen.
From this point of view, we expect that for a stable gapped boundary,
(
6.17
) should not happen. Namely,
any non-trivial projection
p\in{\mathcal{B}}_{l}
should be detected by some anyons in
O_{\mathop{\mathrm{bd}},\Lambda_{0}}^{\mathop{\mathrm{bu}}}
.

Remark 6.4
.

Physically,
considering the idempotent completion means
including all the condensation descendants of
{\mathcal{M}}
.
See section 3.3 of
[
15
]
.
More operator algebraically,
(\rho,p)\in{\mathop{\mathrm{Obj}}}\left({\tilde{\mathcal{M}}}\right)
is a descendant of

\rho\in{\mathcal{M}}
in the sense that

\tilde{\rho}(A):=\rho(A)p,\quad A\in{\mathcal{A}}_{\mathop{\mathrm{H}_{U}}}

is a sub-representation of
\rho
.

Next we introduce the half-braiding with an asymptotic constraint on
\tilde{\mathcal{M}}
.
Note that the only difference between the following and the usual definition
of half-braiding is the asymptotic constraint (iv).
Recall that this asymptotic property (iv)
is observed in Lemma
3.20
.

Definition 6.5
.

Let
(\Lambda_{0},\Lambda_{r0})\in\mathcal{PC}
.
Consider the setting in subsection
1.1
, and
assume
Assumption
1.10
.
For each
(\rho,p)\in{\mathop{\mathrm{Obj}}}\left({\tilde{\mathcal{M}}}\right)
, we say

C_{(\rho,p)}
is a half-braiding for
(\rho,p)

with asymptotic constraint if it
is a map
C_{(\rho,p)}:{\mathop{\mathrm{Obj}}}\left({\tilde{\mathcal{M}}}\right)\to{\mathop{\mathrm{Mor}}}(\tilde{\mathcal{M}})

satisfying the following.

(i)

For any
(\sigma,q)\in{\mathop{\mathrm{Obj}}}\left({\tilde{\mathcal{M}}}\right)
,

C_{(\rho,p)}\left((\sigma,q)\right)\in{\mathop{\mathrm{Mor}}}_{\tilde{\mathcal{M}}}\left((\sigma,q)\otimes_{\tilde{\mathcal{M}}}(\rho,p),(\rho,p)\otimes_{\tilde{\mathcal{M}}}(\sigma,q)\right)
.

(ii)

For any
(\sigma,q),(\sigma^{\prime},q^{\prime})\in{\mathop{\mathrm{Obj}}}\left({\tilde{\mathcal{M}}}\right)
and
R\in{\mathop{\mathrm{Mor}}}_{\tilde{\mathcal{M}}}\left((\sigma,q),(\sigma^{\prime},q^{\prime})\right)
, we have

\displaystyle\begin{split}C_{(\rho,p)}\left((\sigma^{\prime},q^{\prime})\right)\left(R\otimes_{\tilde{\mathcal{M}}}\mathbb{I}_{(\rho,p)}\right)=\left(\mathbb{I}_{(\rho,p)}\otimes_{\tilde{\mathcal{M}}}R\right)C_{(\rho,p)}\left((\sigma,q)\right).\end{split}

(6.19)

(iii)

For any
(\sigma,q),(\gamma,r)\in{\mathop{\mathrm{Obj}}}\left({\tilde{\mathcal{M}}}\right)
, we have

\displaystyle\begin{split}C_{(\rho,p)}\left((\sigma,q)\otimes_{\tilde{\mathcal{M}}}(\gamma,r)\right)=\left(C_{(\rho,p)}\left((\sigma,q)\right)\otimes_{\tilde{\mathcal{M}}}\mathop{\mathrm{id}}\nolimits_{(\gamma,r)}\right)\left(\mathop{\mathrm{id}}\nolimits_{(\sigma,q)}\otimes_{\tilde{\mathcal{M}}}C_{(\rho,p)}\left((\gamma,r)\right)\right).\end{split}

(6.20)

(iv)

For any

{\Lambda_{1}},{\Lambda_{3}}\in{\mathcal{C}}_{{\mathop{\mathrm{bk}}}}^{U}
with
{\Lambda_{1}}\leftarrow_{r}{\Lambda_{3}}
,
{\Lambda_{1}},{\Lambda_{3}}\subset\Lambda_{0}

and
V_{\rho,{\Lambda_{3}}}\in{\mathcal{V}}_{\rho{\Lambda_{3}}}^{\mathop{\mathrm{bu}}}

we have

\displaystyle\begin{split}\lim_{t\to\infty}\sup_{\sigma\in O_{\mathop{\mathrm{bd}{\Lambda_{1}}(t)}}^{\mathop{\mathrm{bu}}}(\sigma,q)\in{\mathop{\mathrm{Obj}}}\left({\tilde{\mathcal{M}}}\right)}\left\|C_{(\rho,p)}\left((\sigma,q)\right)-pT_{\rho}^{\mathrm{(l)}\Lambda_{r0}\mathbb{I}}(q)\cdot V_{\rho,{\Lambda_{3}}}^{*}T_{\sigma}^{\mathrm{(l)}\Lambda_{r0}\mathbb{I}}\left(V_{\rho,{\Lambda_{3}}}\right)qT_{\sigma}^{\mathrm{(l)}\Lambda_{r0}\mathbb{I}}(p)\right\|=0.\end{split}

(6.21)

We call this condition the asymptotic constraint.

(v)

For each
(\sigma,q)\in{\mathop{\mathrm{Obj}}}\left({\tilde{\mathcal{M}}}\right)
, the morphism

C_{(\rho,p)}\left((\sigma,q)\right)

in (i) is an isomorphism.

Remark 6.6
.

Let us consider the physical meaning of the asymptotic constraint (iv) for the case
p=\mathbb{I}
.
Let
{\Lambda_{1}},{\Lambda_{3}}\in{\mathcal{C}}_{{\mathop{\mathrm{bk}}}}^{U}
with
{\Lambda_{1}}\leftarrow_{r}{\Lambda_{3}}
,
{\Lambda_{1}},{\Lambda_{3}}\subset\Lambda_{0}

(see Figure
12
)
and
V_{\rho,{\Lambda_{3}}}\in{\mathcal{V}}_{\rho{\Lambda_{3}}}^{\mathop{\mathrm{bu}}}
, and set

\displaystyle\begin{split}\tilde{\rho}(A):=\mathop{\mathrm{Ad}}\nolimits V_{\rho,{\Lambda_{3}}}\rho(A),\quad A\in{\mathcal{A}}_{\mathop{\mathrm{H}_{U}}}.\end{split}

(6.22)

By this definition, we have

\displaystyle\begin{split}\tilde{\rho}\in O_{\mathop{\mathrm{bd}{\Lambda_{3}}}}^{\mathop{\mathrm{bu}}}\subset O_{\mathop{\mathrm{bd}},\Lambda_{0}}^{\mathop{\mathrm{bu}}},\end{split}

(6.23)

and
(\tilde{\rho},\mathbb{I})\in{\mathop{\mathrm{Obj}}}\left({\tilde{\mathcal{M}}}\right)
.
For this object
(\tilde{\rho},\mathbb{I})\in{\mathop{\mathrm{Obj}}}\left({\tilde{\mathcal{M}}}\right)
,
because we may set
V_{\tilde{\rho},{\Lambda_{3}}}=\mathbb{I}
,
the asymptotic constraint (iv) reduces to

\displaystyle\begin{split}\lim_{t\to\infty}\sup_{\sigma\in O_{\mathop{\mathrm{bd}{\Lambda_{1}}(t)}}^{\mathop{\mathrm{bu}}}(\sigma,q)\in{\mathop{\mathrm{Obj}}}\left({\tilde{\mathcal{M}}}\right)}\left\|C_{(\tilde{\rho},\mathbb{I})}\left((\sigma,q)\right)-(\mathbb{I}_{\tilde{\rho}}\otimes q)(q\otimes\mathbb{I}_{\tilde{\rho}})\right\|=0.\end{split}

(6.24)

Let us regard half-braiding
C_{(\tilde{\rho},\mathbb{I})}(\sigma,q)

as the crossing between
\tilde{\rho}
and
\sigma
,
when we move the particle
\tilde{\rho}
to right
[
16
]
.
Then, because
\tilde{\rho}\in O_{\mathop{\mathrm{bd}{\Lambda_{3}}}}^{\mathop{\mathrm{bu}}}
is already at the right of
\sigma\in O_{\mathop{\mathrm{bd}{\Lambda_{1}}(t)}}^{\mathop{\mathrm{bu}}}

for
t
large enough
(Figure
12
), there is no crossing, when we move
\tilde{\rho}
to the right.
This is what (
6.24
) tells us.
(Note that
\mathbb{I}_{\tilde{\rho}}\otimes q
,
q\otimes\mathbb{I}_{\tilde{\rho}}

are identity morphisms of
(\tilde{\rho},\mathbb{I})\otimes_{\tilde{\mathcal{M}}}(\sigma,q)
,

(\sigma,q)\otimes_{\tilde{\mathcal{M}}}(\tilde{\rho},\mathbb{I})
respectively.
In particular, if
q=\mathbb{I}
, then
(\mathbb{I}_{\tilde{\rho}}\otimes q)(q\otimes\mathbb{I}_{\tilde{\rho}})=\mathbb{I}_{{\mathcal{H}}}
.)
If we suppose that
C_{(\rho,\mathbb{I})}
is natural with respect to

\rho
for morphisms in
{\mathcal{F}}_{{\mathop{\mathrm{bk}}}}^{U}
, then we have

\displaystyle V_{\rho,{\Lambda_{3}}}C_{(\rho,\mathbb{I})}\left((\sigma,q)\right)=C_{(\tilde{\rho},\mathbb{I})}\left((\sigma,q)\right)T_{\sigma}^{\mathrm{(l)}\Lambda_{r0}\mathbb{I}}\left(V_{\rho,{\Lambda_{3}}}\right).

(6.25)

Substituting this to (
6.24
), we get (
6.21
) for
p=\mathbb{I}
.
The general (iv) is a truncation of this.

Lemma 6.7
.

Let
(\Lambda_{0},\Lambda_{r0})\in\mathcal{PC}
.
Consider the setting in subsection
1.1
, and
assume Assumption
1.6
, Assumption
1.7
,
Assumption
1.10
.
For each
(\rho,p),(\sigma,q)\in{\mathop{\mathrm{Obj}}}\left({\tilde{\mathcal{M}}}\right)
, set

\displaystyle\begin{split}\tilde{\iota}\left((\sigma,q),(\rho,p)\right):=\left(p\otimes q\right)\cdot\iota^{(\Lambda_{0},\Lambda_{r0})}\left(\sigma:\rho\right)\cdot\left(q\otimes p\right).\end{split}

(6.26)

Then for each
(\rho,p)\in{\mathop{\mathrm{Obj}}}\left({\tilde{\mathcal{M}}}\right)
,
\tilde{\iota}\left((-,-),(\rho,p)\right)
satisfies
properties (i), (ii), (iv) of Definition
6.5
.
Conversely, if
C_{(\rho,p)}:{\mathop{\mathrm{Obj}}}\left({\tilde{\mathcal{M}}}\right)\to{\mathcal{B}}({\mathcal{H}}_{\mathop{\mathrm{bd}}})

satisfies (i), (ii), (iv) of Definition
6.5
for
(\rho,p)\in{\mathop{\mathrm{Obj}}}\left({\tilde{\mathcal{M}}}\right)
,
then we have
C_{(\rho,p)}=\tilde{\iota}\left((-,-),(\rho,p)\right)
.

(i) By the definition, we have
\tilde{\iota}\left((\sigma,q),(\rho,p)\right)\in\left(\sigma\otimes\rho,\rho\otimes\sigma\right)_{l}
,
and

\displaystyle\begin{split}\left(p\otimes q\right)\cdot\tilde{\iota}\left((\sigma,q),(\rho,p)\right)=\tilde{\iota}\left((\sigma,q),(\rho,p)\right)=\tilde{\iota}\left((\sigma,q),(\rho,p)\right)\cdot\left(q\otimes p\right).\end{split}

(6.27)

(ii)
For any
(\sigma,q),(\sigma^{\prime},q^{\prime})\in{\mathop{\mathrm{Obj}}}\left({\tilde{\mathcal{M}}}\right)
and
R\in{\mathop{\mathrm{Mor}}}_{\tilde{\mathcal{M}}}\left((\sigma,q),(\sigma^{\prime},q^{\prime})\right)
, we have

\displaystyle\begin{split}&\tilde{\iota}\left((\sigma^{\prime},q^{\prime}),(\rho,p)\right)\left(R\otimes_{\tilde{\mathcal{M}}}\mathbb{I}_{(\rho,p)}\right)=(p\otimes q^{\prime})\iota^{(\Lambda_{0},\Lambda_{r0})}\left(\sigma^{\prime}:\rho\right)(q^{\prime}\otimes p)(R\otimes p)\\
&=(p\otimes q^{\prime})\iota^{(\Lambda_{0},\Lambda_{r0})}\left(\sigma^{\prime}:\rho\right)(R\otimes\mathbb{I}_{\rho})(q\otimes p)=(p\otimes q^{\prime})\left(\mathbb{I}_{\rho}\otimes R\right)\iota^{(\Lambda_{0},\Lambda_{r0})}\left(\sigma:\rho\right)(q\otimes p)\\
&=\left(\mathbb{I}_{(\rho,p)}\otimes_{\tilde{\mathcal{M}}}R\right)\tilde{\iota}\left((\sigma,q),(\rho,p)\right),\end{split}

(6.28)

by Lemma
3.16
.

(iv) For any

{\Lambda_{1}},{\Lambda_{3}}\in{\mathcal{C}}_{{\mathop{\mathrm{bk}}}}^{U}
with
{\Lambda_{1}}\leftarrow_{r}{\Lambda_{3}}
,
{\Lambda_{1}},{\Lambda_{3}}\subset\Lambda_{0}

and
V_{\rho,{\Lambda_{3}}}\in{\mathcal{V}}_{\rho{\Lambda_{3}}}^{\mathop{\mathrm{bu}}}

we have

\displaystyle\begin{split}&\lim_{t\to\infty}\sup_{\sigma\in O_{\mathop{\mathrm{bd}{\Lambda_{1}}(t)}}^{\mathop{\mathrm{bu}}}(\sigma,q)\in{\mathop{\mathrm{Obj}}}\left({\tilde{\mathcal{M}}}\right)}\left\|\tilde{\iota}\left((\sigma,q),(\rho,p)\right)-pT_{\rho}^{\mathrm{(l)}\Lambda_{r0}\mathbb{I}}(q)\cdot V_{\rho,{\Lambda_{3}}}^{*}T_{\sigma}^{\mathrm{(l)}\Lambda_{r0}\mathbb{I}}\left(V_{\rho,{\Lambda_{3}}}\right)qT_{\sigma}^{\mathrm{(l)}\Lambda_{r0}\mathbb{I}}(p)\right\|\\
&=\lim_{t\to\infty}\sup_{\sigma\in O_{\mathop{\mathrm{bd}{\Lambda_{1}}(t)}}^{\mathop{\mathrm{bu}}}(\sigma,q)\in{\mathop{\mathrm{Obj}}}\left({\tilde{\mathcal{M}}}\right)}\left\|(p\otimes q)\iota^{(\Lambda_{0},\Lambda_{r0})}\left(\sigma:\rho\right)(q\otimes p)-(p\otimes q)\cdot V_{\rho,{\Lambda_{3}}}^{*}T_{\sigma}^{\mathrm{(l)}\Lambda_{r0}\mathbb{I}}\left(V_{\rho,{\Lambda_{3}}}\right)(q\otimes p)\right\|\\
&=0,\end{split}

(6.29)

by Lemma
3.20
.

Suppose now that
C_{(\rho,p)}:{\mathop{\mathrm{Obj}}}\left({\tilde{\mathcal{M}}}\right)\to{\mathcal{B}}({\mathcal{H}}_{\mathop{\mathrm{bd}}})

satisfies (i), (ii), (iv) of Definition
6.5
for
(\rho,p)\in{\mathop{\mathrm{Obj}}}\left({\tilde{\mathcal{M}}}\right)
.
Consider
{\Lambda_{1}},{\Lambda_{3}}
in (iv).
For each
t\geq 0
and
(\sigma,q)\in{\mathop{\mathrm{Obj}}}\left({\tilde{\mathcal{M}}}\right)
,
we fix
V_{\sigma,{\Lambda_{1}}(t)}\in{\mathcal{V}}_{\sigma{\Lambda_{1}}(t)}^{\mathop{\mathrm{bu}}}
and set

\displaystyle\begin{split}\sigma(t):=\mathop{\mathrm{Ad}}\nolimits V_{\sigma,{\Lambda_{1}}(t)}\circ\sigma\in O_{\mathop{\mathrm{bd}{\Lambda_{1}}(t)}}^{\mathop{\mathrm{bu}}}\subset O_{\mathop{\mathrm{bd}},\Lambda_{0}}^{\mathop{\mathrm{bu}}}={\mathop{\mathrm{Obj}}}{\mathcal{M}},\quad q(t):=\mathop{\mathrm{Ad}}\nolimits V_{\sigma,{\Lambda_{1}}(t)}(q).\end{split}

(6.30)

Then we have
(\sigma(t),q(t))\in{\mathop{\mathrm{Obj}}}\left({\tilde{\mathcal{M}}}\right)
, and

q(t)V_{\sigma,{\Lambda_{1}}(t)}q\in{\mathop{\mathrm{Mor}}}_{\tilde{\mathcal{M}}}\left((\sigma,q),(\sigma(t),q(t))\right)
.
Because
C_{(\rho,p)}
satisfies (ii) of Definition
6.5
,
we have

\displaystyle\begin{split}C_{(\rho,p)}\left((\sigma(t),q(t))\right)\left(q(t)V_{\sigma,{\Lambda_{1}}(t)}q\otimes_{\tilde{\mathcal{M}}}\mathop{\mathrm{id}}\nolimits_{(\rho,p)}\right)=\left(\mathop{\mathrm{id}}\nolimits_{(\rho,p)}\otimes_{\tilde{\mathcal{M}}}q(t)V_{\sigma,{\Lambda_{1}}(t)}q\right)C_{(\rho,p)}\left((\sigma,q)\right).\end{split}

(6.31)

Multiplying
\left(\mathop{\mathrm{id}}\nolimits_{(\rho,p)}\otimes_{\tilde{\mathcal{M}}}q{V_{\sigma,{\Lambda_{1}}(t)}}^{*}q(t)\right)

from the left, we have

\displaystyle\begin{split}\left(\mathop{\mathrm{id}}\nolimits_{(\rho,p)}\otimes_{\tilde{\mathcal{M}}}q{V_{\sigma,{\Lambda_{1}}(t)}}^{*}q(t)\right)C_{(\rho,p)}\left((\sigma(t),q(t))\right)\left(q(t)V_{\sigma,{\Lambda_{1}}(t)}q\otimes_{\tilde{\mathcal{M}}}\mathop{\mathrm{id}}\nolimits_{(\rho,p)}\right)=\left(p\otimes_{\tilde{\mathcal{M}}}q\right)C_{(\rho,p)}\left((\sigma,q)\right)=C_{(\rho,p)}\left((\sigma,q)\right).\end{split}

(6.32)

In the last equation we used (i) of Definition
6.5
.
Taking
t\to\infty
limit and using (iv) of Definition
6.5
,
we get

\displaystyle\begin{split}&C_{(\rho,p)}\left((\sigma,q)\right)\\
&=\lim_{t\to\infty}\left(\mathop{\mathrm{id}}\nolimits_{(\rho,p)}\otimes_{\tilde{\mathcal{M}}}q{V_{\sigma,{\Lambda_{1}}(t)}}^{*}q(t)\right)pT_{\rho}^{\mathrm{(l)}\Lambda_{r0}\mathbb{I}}(q(t))\cdot V_{\rho,{\Lambda_{3}}}^{*}T_{\sigma(t)}^{\mathrm{(l)}\Lambda_{r0}\mathbb{I}}\left(V_{\rho,{\Lambda_{3}}}\right)q(t)T_{\sigma(t)}^{\mathrm{(l)}\Lambda_{r0}\mathbb{I}}(p)\left(q(t)V_{\sigma,{\Lambda_{1}}(t)}q\otimes_{\tilde{\mathcal{M}}}\mathop{\mathrm{id}}\nolimits_{(\rho,p)}\right).\end{split}

(6.33)

In particular, because
\tilde{\iota}\left((-,-),(\rho,p)\right)
satisfies
properties (i), (ii), (iv) of Definition
6.5
,
it is given by the right hand side of (
6.33
).
Therefore, we get
C_{(\rho,p)}=\tilde{\iota}\left((-,-),(\rho,p)\right)
.

\square

We checked that

\tilde{\iota}\left((\sigma,q),(\rho,p)\right)
in (
6.26
) satisfies (i), (ii), (iv) of Definition
6.5
.
For
\tilde{\iota}\left((\sigma,q),(\rho,p)\right)
to satisfy (v) of Definition
6.5
,

p
need to satisfy some condition.

Lemma 6.8
.

Let
(\Lambda_{0},\Lambda_{r0})\in\mathcal{PC}
.
Consider the setting in subsection
1.1
, and
assume Assumption
1.6
, Assumption
1.7
,
Assumption
1.10
.
Then we have the following

1.

If
(\rho,p)\in{\mathop{\mathrm{Obj}}}\left({\tilde{\mathcal{M}}}\right)
satisfies
p\in{\mathcal{F}}_{{\mathop{\mathrm{bd}}}}^{U}
, then

\tilde{\iota}\left((-,-),(\rho,p)\right)
satisfies (iii) and (v) of Definition
6.5

and is a half-braiding for
(\rho,p)

with asymptotic constraint.

2.

Let
(\rho,p)\in{\mathop{\mathrm{Obj}}}\left({\tilde{\mathcal{M}}}\right)
.
Suppose that

\tilde{\iota}\left((-,-),(\rho,p)\right)
satisfies (v) of Definition
6.5
.
Let
{\Lambda_{1}},{\Lambda_{3}}\in{\mathcal{C}}_{{\mathop{\mathrm{bk}}}}^{U}
with
{\Lambda_{1}}\leftarrow_{r}{\Lambda_{3}}
,
{\Lambda_{1}},{\Lambda_{3}}\subset\Lambda_{0}

and
V_{\rho,{\Lambda_{3}}}\in{\mathcal{V}}_{\rho{\Lambda_{3}}}^{\mathop{\mathrm{bu}}}
.
Set
\tilde{p}:=V_{\rho,{\Lambda_{3}}}p{V_{\rho,{\Lambda_{3}}}}^{*}
.
Then we have

\displaystyle\begin{split}\lim_{t\to\infty}\sup_{\sigma\in O_{\mathop{\mathrm{bd}{\Lambda_{1}}(t)}}^{\mathop{\mathrm{bu}}}}\left\|\tilde{p}-\left(T_{\sigma}^{\mathrm{(l)}\Lambda_{r0}\mathbb{I}}(\tilde{p})\right)\right\|=0.\end{split}

(6.34)

1.

Let
(\rho,p)\in{\mathop{\mathrm{Obj}}}\left({\tilde{\mathcal{M}}}\right)
.
Then for any
(\sigma,q),(\gamma,r)\in{\mathop{\mathrm{Obj}}}\left({\tilde{\mathcal{M}}}\right)
, we have

\displaystyle\begin{split}&\left(\tilde{\iota}\left((\sigma,q),(\rho,p)\right)\otimes_{\tilde{\mathcal{M}}}\mathop{\mathrm{id}}\nolimits_{(\gamma,r)}\right)\left(\mathop{\mathrm{id}}\nolimits_{(\sigma,q)}\otimes_{\tilde{\mathcal{M}}}\tilde{\iota}\left((\gamma,r),(\rho,p)\right)\right)\\
&=\left(\left((p\otimes q)\iota^{(\Lambda_{0},\Lambda_{r0})}\left(\sigma:\rho\right)(q\otimes p)\right)\otimes r\right)\left(q\otimes\left((p\otimes r)\iota^{(\Lambda_{0},\Lambda_{r0})}\left(\gamma:\rho\right)(r\otimes p)\right)\right)\\
&=\left(p\otimes q\otimes r\right)\left(\left(\iota^{(\Lambda_{0},\Lambda_{r0})}\left(\sigma:\rho\right)\otimes\mathbb{I}_{\gamma}\right)\left(\mathbb{I}_{\sigma}\otimes p\otimes\mathbb{I}_{\gamma}\right)\left(\mathbb{I}_{\sigma}\otimes\iota^{(\Lambda_{0},\Lambda_{r0})}\left(\gamma:\rho\right)\right)\right)\left(q\otimes r\otimes p\right)\end{split}

(6.35)

If
p\in{\mathcal{F}}_{{\mathop{\mathrm{bd}}}}^{U}
,
using Lemma
3.14
and Lemma
3.18
,
we have

\displaystyle\begin{split}&\left(\tilde{\iota}\left((\sigma,q),(\rho,p)\right)\otimes_{\tilde{\mathcal{M}}}\mathop{\mathrm{id}}\nolimits_{(\gamma,r)}\right)\left(\mathop{\mathrm{id}}\nolimits_{(\sigma,q)}\otimes_{\tilde{\mathcal{M}}}\tilde{\iota}\left((\gamma,r),(\rho,p)\right)\right)=(\ref {midori})\\
&=\left(p\otimes q\otimes r\right)\left(\left(\iota^{(\Lambda_{0},\Lambda_{r0})}\left(\sigma:\rho\right)\otimes\mathbb{I}_{\gamma}\right)\left(\mathbb{I}_{\sigma}\otimes\iota^{(\Lambda_{0},\Lambda_{r0})}\left(\gamma:\rho\right)\right)\right)\left(q\otimes r\otimes p\right)\\
&=\left(p\otimes q\otimes r\right)\iota^{(\Lambda_{0},\Lambda_{r0})}\left(\sigma\otimes\gamma:\rho\right)\left(q\otimes r\otimes p\right)=\tilde{\iota}\left((\sigma,q)\otimes_{\tilde{\mathcal{M}}}(\gamma,r),(\rho,p)\right).\end{split}

(6.36)

Hence (iii) holds. Similarly, if
p\in{\mathcal{F}}_{{\mathop{\mathrm{bd}}}}^{U}
,
(v) can be checked by

\displaystyle\begin{split}\tilde{\iota}\left((\sigma,q),(\rho,p)\right)=\left(p\otimes q\right)\iota^{(\Lambda_{0},\Lambda_{r0})}\left(\sigma:\rho\right)\left(q\otimes p\right)=\left(p\otimes q\right)\iota^{(\Lambda_{0},\Lambda_{r0})}\left(\sigma:\rho\right)=\iota^{(\Lambda_{0},\Lambda_{r0})}\left(\sigma:\rho\right)\left(q\otimes p\right).\end{split}

(6.37)

Here, we used
Lemma
3.14
and Lemma
3.16
.

2.

Consider the setting of
2
.
If (v) holds for
\tilde{\iota}\left((-,-),(\rho,p)\right)
,
then

\left(\tilde{\iota}\left((\sigma,\mathbb{I}),(\rho,p)\right)\right)^{*}\tilde{\iota}\left((\sigma,\mathbb{I}),(\rho,p)\right)

should be the identity of
(\sigma,\mathbb{I})\otimes_{\tilde{\mathcal{M}}}(\rho,p)
, which is

T_{\sigma}^{\mathrm{(l)}\Lambda_{r0}\mathbb{I}}(p)
, for any
\sigma\in{\mathop{\mathrm{Obj}}}{\mathcal{M}}
.
Similarly,

\tilde{\iota}\left((\sigma,\mathbb{I}),(\rho,p)\right)\left(\tilde{\iota}\left((\sigma,\mathbb{I}),(\rho,p)\right)\right)^{*}

should be the identity of
(\rho,p)\otimes_{\tilde{\mathcal{M}}}(\sigma,\mathbb{I})
, which is

p
, for any
\sigma\in{\mathop{\mathrm{Obj}}}{\mathcal{M}}
.
Therefore,
for any
\sigma\in{\mathop{\mathrm{Obj}}}{\mathcal{M}}
, we have

\displaystyle\begin{split}&T_{\sigma}^{\mathrm{(l)}\Lambda_{r0}\mathbb{I}}(p)=\left(\tilde{\iota}\left((\sigma,\mathbb{I}),(\rho,p)\right)\right)^{*}\tilde{\iota}\left((\sigma,\mathbb{I}),(\rho,p)\right)\\
&=T_{\sigma}^{\mathrm{(l)}\Lambda_{r0}\mathbb{I}}(p)\cdot\left(\iota^{(\Lambda_{0},\Lambda_{r0})}\left(\sigma:\rho\right)\right)^{*}\cdot p\cdot\iota^{(\Lambda_{0},\Lambda_{r0})}\left(\sigma:\rho\right)\cdot T_{\sigma}^{\mathrm{(l)}\Lambda_{r0}\mathbb{I}}(p),\\
&p=\tilde{\iota}\left((\sigma,\mathbb{I}),(\rho,p)\right)\cdot\left(\tilde{\iota}\left((\sigma,\mathbb{I}),(\rho,p)\right)\right)^{*}=p\cdot\iota^{(\Lambda_{0},\Lambda_{r0})}\left(\sigma:\rho\right)\cdot T_{\sigma}^{\mathrm{(l)}\Lambda_{r0}\mathbb{I}}(p)\cdot\left(\iota^{(\Lambda_{0},\Lambda_{r0})}\left(\sigma:\rho\right)\right)^{*}p.\end{split}

(6.38)

Then from Lemma
3.20
, we have

\displaystyle\begin{split}&\lim_{t\to\infty}\sup_{\sigma\in O_{\mathop{\mathrm{bd}{\Lambda_{1}}(t)}}^{\mathop{\mathrm{bu}}}}\left\|T_{\sigma}^{\mathrm{(l)}\Lambda_{r0}\mathbb{I}}(\tilde{p})\tilde{p}T_{\sigma}^{\mathrm{(l)}\Lambda_{r0}\mathbb{I}}(\tilde{p})-T_{\sigma}^{\mathrm{(l)}\Lambda_{r0}\mathbb{I}}(\tilde{p})\right\|\\
&=\lim_{t\to\infty}\sup_{\sigma\in O_{\mathop{\mathrm{bd}{\Lambda_{1}}(t)}}^{\mathop{\mathrm{bu}}}}\left\|T_{\sigma}^{\mathrm{(l)}\Lambda_{r0}\mathbb{I}}(p)T_{\sigma}^{\mathrm{(l)}\Lambda_{r0}\mathbb{I}}(V_{\rho,{\Lambda_{3}}}^{*})V_{\rho,{\Lambda_{3}}}pV_{\rho,{\Lambda_{3}}}^{*}T_{\sigma}^{\mathrm{(l)}\Lambda_{r0}\mathbb{I}}(V_{\rho,{\Lambda_{3}}})T_{\sigma}^{\mathrm{(l)}\Lambda_{r0}\mathbb{I}}(p)-T_{\sigma}^{\mathrm{(l)}\Lambda_{r0}\mathbb{I}}(p)\right\|=0,\end{split}

(6.39)

and

\displaystyle\begin{split}&\lim_{t\to\infty}\sup_{\sigma\in O_{\mathop{\mathrm{bd}{\Lambda_{1}}(t)}}^{\mathop{\mathrm{bu}}}}\left\|\tilde{p}T_{\sigma}^{\mathrm{(l)}\Lambda_{r0}\mathbb{I}}(\tilde{p})\tilde{p}-\tilde{p}\right\|\\
&=\lim_{t\to\infty}\sup_{\sigma\in O_{\mathop{\mathrm{bd}{\Lambda_{1}}(t)}}^{\mathop{\mathrm{bu}}}}\left\|pV_{\rho,{\Lambda_{3}}}^{*}T_{\sigma}^{\mathrm{(l)}\Lambda_{r0}\mathbb{I}}(V_{\rho,{\Lambda_{3}}})T_{\sigma}^{\mathrm{(l)}\Lambda_{r0}\mathbb{I}}(p)T_{\sigma}^{\mathrm{(l)}\Lambda_{r0}\mathbb{I}}(V_{\rho,{\Lambda_{3}}}^{*})V_{\rho,{\Lambda_{3}}}p-p\right\|=0.\end{split}

(6.40)

Therefore, we have

\displaystyle\begin{split}&\lim_{t\to\infty}\sup_{\sigma\in O_{\mathop{\mathrm{bd}{\Lambda_{1}}(t)}}^{\mathop{\mathrm{bu}}}}\left\|\tilde{p}T_{\sigma}^{\mathrm{(l)}\Lambda_{r0}\mathbb{I}}(\tilde{p})-T_{\sigma}^{\mathrm{(l)}\Lambda_{r0}\mathbb{I}}(\tilde{p})\right\|=\lim_{t\to\infty}\sup_{\sigma\in O_{\mathop{\mathrm{bd}{\Lambda_{1}}(t)}}^{\mathop{\mathrm{bu}}}}\left\|T_{\sigma}^{\mathrm{(l)}\Lambda_{r0}\mathbb{I}}(\tilde{p})\left(\tilde{p}-\mathbb{I}\right)T_{\sigma}^{\mathrm{(l)}\Lambda_{r0}\mathbb{I}}(\tilde{p})\right\|^{\frac{1}{2}}=0,\\
&\lim_{t\to\infty}\sup_{\sigma\in O_{\mathop{\mathrm{bd}{\Lambda_{1}}(t)}}^{\mathop{\mathrm{bu}}}}\left\|\tilde{p}T_{\sigma}^{\mathrm{(l)}\Lambda_{r0}\mathbb{I}}(\tilde{p})-\tilde{p}\right\|=\lim_{t\to\infty}\sup_{\sigma\in O_{\mathop{\mathrm{bd}{\Lambda_{1}}(t)}}^{\mathop{\mathrm{bu}}}}\left\|T_{\sigma}^{\mathrm{(l)}\Lambda_{r0}\mathbb{I}}(\tilde{p})\tilde{p}-\tilde{p}\right\|\\
&=\lim_{t\to\infty}\sup_{\sigma\in O_{\mathop{\mathrm{bd}{\Lambda_{1}}(t)}}^{\mathop{\mathrm{bu}}}}\left\|\tilde{p}\left(T_{\sigma}^{\mathrm{(l)}\Lambda_{r0}\mathbb{I}}(\tilde{p})-\mathbb{I}\right)\tilde{p}\right\|^{\frac{1}{2}}=0.\end{split}

(6.41)

Hence we obtain

\displaystyle\begin{split}\lim_{t\to\infty}\sup_{\sigma\in O_{\mathop{\mathrm{bd}{\Lambda_{1}}(t)}}^{\mathop{\mathrm{bu}}}}\left\|\tilde{p}-T_{\sigma}^{\mathrm{(l)}\Lambda_{r0}\mathbb{I}}(\tilde{p})\right\|=0.\end{split}

(6.42)

\square

Next we introduce the Drinfeld center
{\mathop{\mathrm{Obj}}}\left({\mathcal{Z}}_{a}\left(\tilde{\mathcal{M}}\right)\right)
with the asymptotic constraints.
First we prepare sets corresponding to the objects and morphisms.

Definition 6.9
.

Let
(\Lambda_{0},\Lambda_{r0})\in\mathcal{PC}
.
Consider the setting in subsection
1.1
, and
assume
Assumption
1.10
.
We denote by
{\mathop{\mathrm{Obj}}}\left({\mathcal{Z}}_{a}\left(\tilde{\mathcal{M}}\right)\right)
, the set of all pairs
\left((\rho,p),C_{(\rho,p)}\right)

where

(\rho,p)
is an object of
\tilde{\mathcal{M}}
and

C_{(\rho,p)}
is a half-braiding for
(\rho,p)
with the asymptotic constraint.
For
\left((\rho,p),C_{(\rho,p)}\right),\left((\sigma,q),C_{(\sigma,q)}\right)\in{\mathop{\mathrm{Obj}}}\left({\mathcal{Z}}_{a}\left(\tilde{\mathcal{M}}\right)\right)
, we denote by

{\mathop{\mathrm{Mor}}}_{{\mathcal{Z}}_{a}\left(\tilde{\mathcal{M}}\right)}\left(\left((\rho,p),C_{(\rho,p)}\right),\left((\sigma,q),C_{(\sigma,q)}\right)\right)

the set of all
R\in{\mathop{\mathrm{Mor}}}_{\tilde{\mathcal{M}}}\left((\rho,p),(\sigma,q)\right)

satisfying

\displaystyle\begin{split}\left(R\otimes_{\tilde{\mathcal{M}}}\mathbb{I}_{(\gamma,r)}\right)C_{(\rho,p)}\left((\gamma,r)\right)=C_{(\sigma,q)}\left((\gamma,r)\right)\left(\mathbb{I}_{(\gamma,r)}\otimes_{\tilde{\mathcal{M}}}R\right)\end{split}

(6.43)

for all
(\gamma,r)\in{\mathop{\mathrm{Obj}}}\left({\tilde{\mathcal{M}}}\right)
.

Note that this definition is identical to the standard one
[
11
]
except for the asymptotic constraints.
From Lemma
3.14
, Lemma
6.7
,
we have the following

Lemma 6.10
.

Let
(\Lambda_{0},\Lambda_{r0})\in\mathcal{PC}
.
Consider the setting in subsection
1.1
, and
assume Assumption
1.6
, Assumption
1.7
,
Assumption
1.10
.
Then for any

\left((\rho,p),C_{(\rho,p)}\right),\left((\sigma,q),C_{(\sigma,q)}\right)\in{\mathop{\mathrm{Obj}}}\left({\mathcal{Z}}_{a}\left(\tilde{\mathcal{M}}\right)\right)
,
we have

\displaystyle\begin{split}{\mathop{\mathrm{Mor}}}_{\tilde{\mathcal{M}}}\left((\rho,p),(\sigma,q)\right)\cap{\mathcal{F}}_{{\mathop{\mathrm{bd}}}}^{U}\subset{\mathop{\mathrm{Mor}}}_{{\mathcal{Z}}_{a}\left(\tilde{\mathcal{M}}\right)}\left(\left((\rho,p),C_{(\rho,p)}\right),\left((\sigma,q),C_{(\sigma,q)}\right)\right)\end{split}

(6.44)

Now, we introduce an additional assumption.

Assumption 6.11
.

Let
(\Lambda_{0},\Lambda_{r0})\in\mathcal{PC}
.
Consider the setting in subsection
1.1
.
For any

{\Lambda_{1}},{\Lambda_{3}}\in{\mathcal{C}}_{{\mathop{\mathrm{bk}}}}^{U}
with
{\Lambda_{1}}\leftarrow_{r}{\Lambda_{3}}
,
{\Lambda_{1}},{\Lambda_{3}}\subset\Lambda_{0}
,
we assume the following to hold:
If
R\in(\rho,\sigma)_{l}
for some
\rho,\sigma\in O_{\mathop{\mathrm{bd}{\Lambda_{3}}}}^{\mathop{\mathrm{bu}}}

and

\displaystyle\begin{split}\lim_{t\to\infty}\sup_{\gamma\in O_{\mathop{\mathrm{bd}{\Lambda_{1}}(t)}}^{\mathop{\mathrm{bu}}}}\left\|R-T_{\gamma}^{\mathrm{(l)}\Lambda_{r0}\mathbb{I}}(R)\right\|=0,\end{split}

(6.45)

then

R\in{\mathcal{F}}_{{\mathop{\mathrm{bd}}}}^{U}
.

Remark 6.12
.

For
\rho,\sigma,R
and

\gamma\in O_{\mathop{\mathrm{bd}{\Lambda_{1}}(t)}}^{\mathop{\mathrm{bu}}}
in Assumption
6.11
,
from Lemma
3.16
,
we have

\displaystyle\begin{split}\iota^{(\Lambda_{0},\Lambda_{r0})}\left(\sigma:\gamma\right)R=\iota^{(\Lambda_{0},\Lambda_{r0})}\left(\sigma:\gamma\right)\left(R\otimes\mathbb{I}_{\gamma}\right)=\left(\mathbb{I}_{\gamma}\otimes R\right)\iota^{(\Lambda_{0},\Lambda_{r0})}\left(\rho:\gamma\right)=T_{\gamma}^{\mathrm{(l)}\Lambda_{r0}\mathbb{I}}(R)\iota^{(\Lambda_{0},\Lambda_{r0})}\left(\rho:\gamma\right).\end{split}

(6.46)

From this, we have

\displaystyle\begin{split}\left\|\iota^{(\Lambda_{0},\Lambda_{r0})}\left(\sigma:\gamma\right)R-R\iota^{(\Lambda_{0},\Lambda_{r0})}\left(\rho:\gamma\right)\right\|=\left\|R-T_{\gamma}^{\mathrm{(l)}\Lambda_{r0}\mathbb{I}}(R)\right\|.\end{split}

(6.47)

In particular, if
R
is a unitary,
(
6.45
) holds if and only if
\iota^{(\Lambda_{0},\Lambda_{r0})}\left(\sigma:-\right)

and
\iota^{(\Lambda_{0},\Lambda_{r0})}\left(\rho:-\right)
are asymptotically unitarily equivalent.
From this point of view, Assumption
6.11
says that
quasi-particles which have the asymptotically unitarily equivalent
half-braiding should be equivalent with respect to
\simeq_{U}
.
Under the assumptions of Corollary
5.2
, using the equivalence from Corollary
5.2
,
it means two quasi-particles in the bulk which have the asymptotically unitarily equivalent braiding
are equivalent.
This is a kind of a non-degeneracy condition of the braiding.

Remark 6.13
.

Note that in general,
R\in(\rho,\sigma)_{l}
above belongs to

\pi_{\mathop{\mathrm{bd}}}({\mathcal{A}}_{(\Lambda_{3})^{c}})^{\prime}
but not necessarily to
\pi_{\mathop{\mathrm{bd}}}({\mathcal{A}}_{\Lambda_{3}})^{\prime\prime}
.
As was seen for Toric code (Example
1.2
), there can be an element

R\in\pi_{\mathop{\mathrm{bd}}}({\mathcal{A}}_{(\Lambda_{3})^{c}})^{\prime}\setminus\pi_{\mathop{\mathrm{bd}}}({\mathcal{A}}_{\Lambda_{3}})^{\prime\prime}
given by a weak limit of string operators
on the paths
\zeta_{n}
depicted in Figure
13
,
where
a_{n}\to-\infty
as
n\to\infty
.
Recall in Toric code (Example
1.2
),
\pi_{\gamma}^{X}
can be created from the vacuum by
a unitary constructed in this manner.
Assumption
6.11
tells us we can detect the fact that

R
does not belong to
\pi_{\mathop{\mathrm{bd}}}({\mathcal{A}}_{\Lambda_{3}})^{\prime\prime}
,
(i.e., corresponding to a
deconfined
particle) by a quasi-particle
\gamma

sitting at the cone
\Lambda_{1}(t)
.

Let us consider the case that
\rho=\sigma
and
R
is a projection.
Recall Remark
6.3
. There, we physically concluded that at a stable gapped boundary,
any nontrivial projection in
{\mathcal{B}}_{l}
should be detected by some anyons in
O_{\mathop{\mathrm{bd}},\Lambda_{0}}^{\mathop{\mathrm{bu}}}
.
Suppose our projection
R
is in
\pi_{\mathop{\mathrm{bd}}}({\mathcal{A}}_{(\Lambda_{3})^{c}})^{\prime}\setminus\pi_{\mathop{\mathrm{bd}}}({\mathcal{A}}_{\Lambda_{3}})^{\prime\prime}
and constructed
as above as a weak limit of operators defined on the string
\zeta_{n}
.
Suppose, nevertheless, (
6.45
) holds.
This (roughly) means that our projection

R
cannot be detected by anyons, because crossing at
\Lambda_{1}(t)
does not change
p
.
From Remark
6.3
, the existence of such projection physically means that
the boundary is not a stable gapped boundary.

For this reason, we would like to regard Assumption
6.11

as a stable gapped boundary condition.

Remark 6.14
.

In Toric code (Example
1.2
), Proposition 12.7 of
[
28
]

and its proof proves Assumption
6.11
.

Figure 13:

Under this Assumption
6.11
, we note that
p
need to be in
{\mathcal{F}}_{{\mathop{\mathrm{bd}}}}^{U}

for
(\rho,p)
to have half-braiding with the asymptotic constraint:

Lemma 6.15
.

p

and

2.

for any

,

\displaystyle\begin{split}{\mathop{\mathrm{Mor}}}_{\tilde{\mathcal{M}}}\left((\rho,p),(\sigma,q)\right)\cap{\mathcal{F}}_{{\mathop{\mathrm{bd}}}}^{U}={\mathop{\mathrm{Mor}}}_{{\mathcal{Z}}_{a}\left(\tilde{\mathcal{M}}\right)}\left(\left((\rho,p),C_{(\rho,p)}\right),\left((\sigma,q),C_{(\sigma,q)}\right)\right).\end{split}

(6.48)

1.

We already know the inclusion

from Lemma

Then by the definition of the morphisms, and Lemma

(6.49)

for all

In particular, considering

case, we have

\displaystyle\begin{split}\left(R\otimes\mathbb{I}_{\gamma}\right)\iota^{(\Lambda_{0},\Lambda_{r0})}\left(\gamma:\rho\right)\left(\mathbb{I}_{\gamma}\otimes p\right)-(q\otimes\mathbb{I}_{\gamma})\iota^{(\Lambda_{0},\Lambda_{r0})}\left(\gamma:\sigma\right)(\mathbb{I}_{\gamma}\otimes R)=0\end{split}

(6.50)

, and

,

\tilde{p}:=\mathop{\mathrm{Ad}}\nolimits\left(V_{\rho,{\Lambda_{3}}}\right)(p)\in\pi_{\mathop{\mathrm{bd}}}({\mathcal{A}}_{{\Lambda_{3}}^{c}})^{\prime}
,

\tilde{q}:=\mathop{\mathrm{Ad}}\nolimits\left(V_{\sigma,{\Lambda_{3}}}\right)(q)\in\pi_{\mathop{\mathrm{bd}}}({\mathcal{A}}_{{\Lambda_{3}}^{c}})^{\prime}
.
Then we obtain

\displaystyle\begin{split}&\lim_{t\to\infty}\sup_{{\gamma\in O_{\mathop{\mathrm{bd}{\Lambda_{1}}(t)}}^{\mathop{\mathrm{bu}}}}}\left\|\tilde{R}T_{\gamma}^{\mathrm{(l)}\Lambda_{r0}\mathbb{I}}(\tilde{p})-\tilde{q}T_{\gamma}^{\mathrm{(l)}\Lambda_{r0}\mathbb{I}}(\tilde{R})\right\|\\
&=\lim_{t\to\infty}\sup_{{\gamma\in O_{\mathop{\mathrm{bd}{\Lambda_{1}}(t)}}^{\mathop{\mathrm{bu}}}}}\left\|RV_{\rho,{\Lambda_{3}}}^{*}T_{\gamma}^{\mathrm{(l)}\Lambda_{r0}\mathbb{I}}\left(V_{\rho,{\Lambda_{3}}}\right)T_{\gamma}^{\mathrm{(l)}\Lambda_{r0}\mathbb{I}}(p)-qV_{\sigma,{\Lambda_{3}}}^{*}T_{\gamma}^{\mathrm{(l)}\Lambda_{r0}\mathbb{I}}\left(V_{\sigma,{\Lambda_{3}}}\right)T_{\gamma}^{\mathrm{(l)}\Lambda_{r0}\mathbb{I}}(R)\right\|=0.\end{split}

(6.51)

from Lemma
3.20
, using (
6.50
).
Because of
1
, we already know that
p,q\in{\mathcal{F}}_{{\mathop{\mathrm{bd}}}}^{U}
.
Therefore, by the same proof as
Lemma
3.6
we have

\displaystyle\begin{split}\lim_{t\to\infty}\sup_{{\gamma\in O_{\mathop{\mathrm{bd}{\Lambda_{1}}(t)}}^{\mathop{\mathrm{bu}}}}}\left\|T_{\gamma}^{\mathrm{(l)}\Lambda_{r0}\mathbb{I}}(\tilde{p})-\tilde{p}\right\|,\quad\lim_{t\to\infty}\sup_{{\gamma\in O_{\mathop{\mathrm{bd}{\Lambda_{1}}(t)}}^{\mathop{\mathrm{bu}}}}}\left\|T_{\gamma}^{\mathrm{(l)}\Lambda_{r0}\mathbb{I}}(\tilde{q})-\tilde{q}\right\|\to 0,\quad t\to\infty.\end{split}

(6.52)

Substituting this to (
6.51
), we obtain

\displaystyle\begin{split}\lim_{t\to\infty}\sup_{{\gamma\in O_{\mathop{\mathrm{bd}{\Lambda_{1}}(t)}}^{\mathop{\mathrm{bu}}}}}\left\|\tilde{R}-T_{\gamma}^{\mathrm{(l)}\Lambda_{r0}\mathbb{I}}(\tilde{R})\right\|\to 0.\end{split}

(6.53)

Because
\tilde{R}\in\left(\mathop{\mathrm{Ad}}\nolimits V_{\rho,{\Lambda_{3}}}\circ\rho,\mathop{\mathrm{Ad}}\nolimits V_{\sigma,{\Lambda_{3}}}\circ\sigma\right)_{l}

and
\mathop{\mathrm{Ad}}\nolimits V_{\rho,{\Lambda_{3}}}\circ\rho,\mathop{\mathrm{Ad}}\nolimits V_{\sigma,{\Lambda_{3}}}\circ\sigma\in O_{\mathop{\mathrm{bd}{\Lambda_{3}}}}^{\mathop{\mathrm{bu}}}
,
from the assumption
6.11
, we get
\tilde{R}\in{\mathcal{F}}_{{\mathop{\mathrm{bd}}}}^{U}
.
This means

R\in{\mathcal{F}}_{{\mathop{\mathrm{bd}}}}^{U}
.

\square

Under the assumptions we consider, our
{\mathcal{Z}}_{a}\left(\tilde{\mathcal{M}}\right)

becomes a braided
C^{*}
-tensor category.
The construction is pararell to the standard one
[
11
]
.

Proposition 6.16
.

Let
(\Lambda_{0},\Lambda_{r0})\in\mathcal{PC}
.
Consider the setting in subsection
1.1
, and
assume Assumption
1.6
, Assumption
1.7
,
Assumption
1.10
, Assumption
6.11
.
Then
{\mathcal{Z}}_{a}\left(\tilde{\mathcal{M}}\right)
is a braided
C^{*}
-tensor category
with objects
{\mathop{\mathrm{Obj}}}\left({\mathcal{Z}}_{a}\left(\tilde{\mathcal{M}}\right)\right)
, and morphisms
{\mathop{\mathrm{Mor}}}\left({\mathcal{Z}}_{a}\left(\tilde{\mathcal{M}}\right)\right)

and

(i)

identity morphism

\mathop{\mathrm{id}}\nolimits_{\left((\rho,p),C_{(\rho,p)}\right)}:=p
, for
\left((\rho,p),C_{(\rho,p)}\right)\in{\mathop{\mathrm{Obj}}}\left({\mathcal{Z}}_{a}\left(\tilde{\mathcal{M}}\right)\right)
,

(ii)

composition of morphisms and an anti-linear contravariant functor
*:{\mathcal{Z}}_{a}\left(\tilde{\mathcal{M}}\right)\to{\mathcal{Z}}_{a}\left(\tilde{\mathcal{M}}\right)

inherited from that of
{\mathop{\mathrm{Mor}}}(\tilde{\mathcal{M}})
(
6.4
), (
6.5
)

(iii)

the tensor product of objects

\left((\rho,p),C_{(\rho,p)}\right),\left((\sigma,q),C_{(\sigma,q)}\right)\in{\mathop{\mathrm{Obj}}}\left({\mathcal{Z}}_{a}\left(\tilde{\mathcal{M}}\right)\right)

\displaystyle\begin{split}\left((\rho,p),C_{(\rho,p)}\right)\otimes_{{\mathcal{Z}}_{a}\left(\tilde{\mathcal{M}}\right)}\left((\sigma,q),C_{(\sigma,q)}\right):=\left((\rho,p)\otimes_{\tilde{\mathcal{M}}}(\sigma,q),C_{(\rho,p)}\hat{\otimes}C_{(\sigma,q)}\right)\end{split}

(6.54)

where

\displaystyle\begin{split}\left(C_{(\rho,p)}\hat{\otimes}C_{(\sigma,q)}\right)\left((\gamma,r)\right):=\left(\mathop{\mathrm{id}}\nolimits_{(\rho,p)}\otimes_{\tilde{\mathcal{M}}}C_{(\sigma,q)}\left((\gamma,r)\right)\right)\cdot\left(C_{(\rho,p)}\left((\gamma,r)\right)\otimes_{\tilde{\mathcal{M}}}\mathop{\mathrm{id}}\nolimits_{(\sigma,q)}\right),\end{split}

(6.55)

for
(\gamma,r)\in{\mathop{\mathrm{Obj}}}\left({\tilde{\mathcal{M}}}\right)

with the
tensor unit

\left((\pi_{\mathop{\mathrm{bd}}},\mathbb{I}),\tilde{\iota}\left((-,-),(\pi_{\mathop{\mathrm{bd}}},\mathbb{I})\right)\right)
,
and the tensor of morphisms inherited from that
of
\tilde{\mathcal{M}}
, and

(iv)

the braiding

\displaystyle\begin{split}\epsilon_{{\mathcal{Z}}_{a}\left(\tilde{\mathcal{M}}\right)}\left(\left((\rho,p),C_{(\rho,p)}\right),\left((\sigma,q),C_{(\sigma,q)}\right)\right):=C_{(\sigma,q)}\left((\rho,p)\right)\end{split}

(6.56)

for
\left((\rho,p),C_{(\rho,p)}\right),\left((\sigma,q),C_{(\sigma,q)}\right)\in{\mathop{\mathrm{Obj}}}\left({\mathcal{Z}}_{a}\left(\tilde{\mathcal{M}}\right)\right)
.

Clearly, each
{\mathop{\mathrm{Mor}}}_{{\mathcal{Z}}_{a}\left(\tilde{\mathcal{M}}\right)}\left(\left((\rho,p),C_{(\rho,p)}\right),\left((\sigma,q),C_{(\sigma,q)}\right)\right)

is a Banach space by the definition.
Because of Lemma
6.15

1
,
2
, the element in (i) gives the identity morphism and the composition
and
*
, inherited from

\tilde{\mathcal{M}}
are well-defined.
For example, for
T\in{\mathop{\mathrm{Mor}}}_{{\mathcal{Z}}_{a}\left(\tilde{\mathcal{M}}\right)}\left(\left((\rho,p),C_{(\rho,p)}\right),\left((\sigma,q),C_{(\sigma,q)}\right)\right)
,
we have

\displaystyle\begin{split}T^{*}\in{\mathop{\mathrm{Mor}}}_{\tilde{\mathcal{M}}}\left((\sigma,q),(\rho,p)\right)\cap{\mathcal{F}}_{{\mathop{\mathrm{bd}}}}^{U}={\mathop{\mathrm{Mor}}}_{{\mathcal{Z}}_{a}\left(\tilde{\mathcal{M}}\right)}\left(\left((\sigma,q),C_{(\sigma,q)}\right),\left((\rho,p),C_{(\rho,p)}\right)\right).\end{split}

(6.57)

With these operations,
{\mathcal{Z}}_{a}\left(\tilde{\mathcal{M}}\right)
becomes a
C^{*}
-category.

Next we consider the
tensor product of objects

\left((\rho,p),C_{(\rho,p)}\right),\left((\sigma,q),C_{(\sigma,q)}\right)\in{\mathcal{Z}}_{a}\left(\tilde{\mathcal{M}}\right)
.
By Lemma
6.7
, we have

C_{(\rho,p)}=\tilde{\iota}\left((-,-),(\rho,p)\right)
,
C_{(\sigma,q)}=\tilde{\iota}\left((-,-),(\sigma,q)\right)
.
Therefore, any
\left(\gamma,r\right)\in{\mathop{\mathrm{Obj}}}\left({\tilde{\mathcal{M}}}\right)
, we have

\displaystyle\begin{split}&\left(C_{(\rho,p)}\hat{\otimes}C_{(\sigma,q)}\right)\left((\gamma,r)\right):=\left(\mathop{\mathrm{id}}\nolimits_{(\rho,p)}\otimes_{\tilde{\mathcal{M}}}\tilde{\iota}\left((\gamma,r),(\sigma,q)\right)\right)\cdot\left(\tilde{\iota}\left((\gamma,r),(\rho,p)\right)\otimes_{\tilde{\mathcal{M}}}\mathop{\mathrm{id}}\nolimits_{(\sigma,q)}\right)\\
&=\left(p\otimes q\otimes r\right)\left(\mathbb{I}_{\rho}\otimes\iota^{(\Lambda_{0},\Lambda_{r0})}\left(\gamma:\sigma\right)\right)\left(\mathbb{I}_{\rho}\otimes r\otimes\mathbb{I}_{\sigma}\right)\left(\iota^{(\Lambda_{0},\Lambda_{r0})}\left(\gamma:\rho\right)\otimes\mathbb{I}_{\sigma}\right)\left(r\otimes p\otimes q\right)\\
&=\left(p\otimes q\otimes r\right)\left(\mathbb{I}_{\rho}\otimes\iota^{(\Lambda_{0},\Lambda_{r0})}\left(\gamma:\sigma\right)\right)\left(\iota^{(\Lambda_{0},\Lambda_{r0})}\left(\gamma:\rho\right)\otimes\mathbb{I}_{\sigma}\right)\left(r\otimes p\otimes q\right)\\
&=\left(p\otimes q\otimes r\right)\iota^{(\Lambda_{0},\Lambda_{r0})}\left(\gamma:\rho\otimes\sigma\right)\left(r\otimes p\otimes q\right)\\
&=\tilde{\iota}\left((\gamma,r),(\rho,p)\otimes_{\tilde{\mathcal{M}}}(\sigma,q)\right)\end{split}

(6.58)

using Lemma
3.16
and Lemma
3.17
.
Hence we have
C_{(\rho,p)}\hat{\otimes}C_{(\sigma,q)}=\tilde{\iota}\left((-,-),(\rho,p)\otimes_{\tilde{\mathcal{M}}}(\sigma,q)\right)
.
By Lemma
6.15

1.
,

p,q
belong to
{\mathcal{F}}_{{\mathop{\mathrm{bd}}}}^{U}
and so is
p\otimes q
.
Therefore,
C_{(\rho,p)}\hat{\otimes}C_{(\sigma,q)}=\tilde{\iota}\left((-,-),(\rho,p)\otimes_{\tilde{\mathcal{M}}}(\sigma,q)\right)
is
a half-braiding for
(\rho,p)\otimes_{\tilde{\mathcal{M}}}(\sigma,q)
with asymptotic constraint by
Lemma
6.8
.
Hence (
6.54
) defines an element in
{\mathop{\mathrm{Obj}}}\left({\mathcal{Z}}_{a}\left(\tilde{\mathcal{M}}\right)\right)
.
The tensor of morphisms inherited from that
of
\tilde{\mathcal{M}}
gives a well-defined tensor product on
{\mathop{\mathrm{Mor}}}_{{\mathcal{Z}}_{a}\left(\tilde{\mathcal{M}}\right)}
:
For any
R\in{\mathop{\mathrm{Mor}}}_{{\mathcal{Z}}_{a}\left(\tilde{\mathcal{M}}\right)}\left(\left((\rho,p),C_{(\rho,p)}\right),\left((\rho^{\prime},p^{\prime}),C_{(\rho^{\prime},p^{\prime})}\right)\right)
and

S\in{\mathop{\mathrm{Mor}}}_{{\mathcal{Z}}_{a}\left(\tilde{\mathcal{M}}\right)}\left(\left((\sigma,q),C_{(\sigma,q)}\right)\left((\sigma^{\prime},q^{\prime}),C_{(\sigma^{\prime},q^{\prime})}\right)\right)
belong to
{\mathcal{F}}_{{\mathop{\mathrm{bd}}}}^{U}
,
we have

\displaystyle\begin{split}R\otimes_{{\mathcal{Z}}_{a}\left(\tilde{\mathcal{M}}\right)}S:=R\otimes_{\tilde{\mathcal{M}}}S\in{\mathop{\mathrm{Mor}}}_{\tilde{\mathcal{M}}}\left((\rho,p)\otimes_{\tilde{\mathcal{M}}}(\sigma,q),(\rho^{\prime},p^{\prime})\otimes_{\tilde{\mathcal{M}}}(\sigma^{\prime},q^{\prime})\right)\cap{\mathcal{F}}_{{\mathop{\mathrm{bd}}}}^{U}.\end{split}

(6.59)

From Lemma
6.15
we have

\displaystyle\begin{split}&R\otimes_{{\mathcal{Z}}_{a}\left(\tilde{\mathcal{M}}\right)}S\\
&\in{\mathop{\mathrm{Mor}}}_{{\mathcal{Z}}_{a}\left(\tilde{\mathcal{M}}\right)}\left(\left((\rho,p),C_{(\rho,p)}\right)\otimes_{{\mathcal{Z}}_{a}\left(\tilde{\mathcal{M}}\right)}\left((\sigma,q),C_{(\sigma,q)}\right),\left((\rho^{\prime},p^{\prime}),C_{(\rho^{\prime},p^{\prime})}\right)\otimes_{{\mathcal{Z}}_{a}\left(\tilde{\mathcal{M}}\right)}\left((\sigma^{\prime},q^{\prime}),C_{(\sigma^{\prime},q^{\prime})}\right)\right).\end{split}

(6.60)

Because the morphisms and identity morphisms and tensors of morphisms
in
{\mathcal{Z}}_{a}\left(\tilde{\mathcal{M}}\right)

are just the ones inherited from
\tilde{\mathcal{M}}
,
properties corresponding to (
6.10
), (
