) automatically holds.

The associators are also inherited from
\tilde{\mathcal{M}}
:

\displaystyle\begin{split}a_{\left((\rho,p),C_{(\rho,p)}\right),\left((\sigma,q),C_{(\sigma,q)}\right),\left((\gamma,r),C_{(\gamma,r)}\right)}:=\alpha_{(\rho,p),(\sigma,q),(\gamma,r)}=p\otimes q\otimes r.\end{split}

(6.61)

From Lemma
6.15
, this belongs to
the morphism in
{\mathcal{Z}}_{a}\left(\tilde{\mathcal{M}}\right)
between

\left(\left((\rho,p),C_{(\rho,p)}\right)\otimes_{{\mathcal{Z}}_{a}\left(\tilde{\mathcal{M}}\right)}\left((\sigma,q),C_{(\sigma,q)}\right)\right)\otimes_{{\mathcal{Z}}_{a}\left(\tilde{\mathcal{M}}\right)}\left((\gamma,r),C_{(\gamma,r)}\right)

and

\left((\rho,p),C_{(\rho,p)}\right)\otimes_{{\mathcal{Z}}_{a}\left(\tilde{\mathcal{M}}\right)}\left(\left((\sigma,q),C_{(\sigma,q)}\right)\otimes_{{\mathcal{Z}}_{a}\left(\tilde{\mathcal{M}}\right)}\left((\gamma,r),C_{(\gamma,r)}\right)\right).

This is an isomorphism, and its naturality and the
pentagon equation immediately follows from those in
\tilde{\mathcal{M}}
case.

Note that

\displaystyle\begin{split}&\left((\pi_{\mathop{\mathrm{bd}}},\mathbb{I}),\tilde{\iota}\left((-,-),(\pi_{\mathop{\mathrm{bd}}},\mathbb{I})\right)\right)\otimes_{{\mathcal{Z}}_{a}\left(\tilde{\mathcal{M}}\right)}\left((\rho,p),C_{(\rho,p)}\right)=\left((\rho,p),C_{(\rho,p)}\right)\\
&=\left((\rho,p),C_{(\rho,p)}\right)\otimes_{{\mathcal{Z}}_{a}\left(\tilde{\mathcal{M}}\right)}\left((\pi_{\mathop{\mathrm{bd}}},\mathbb{I}),\tilde{\iota}\left((-,-),(\pi_{\mathop{\mathrm{bd}}},\mathbb{I})\right)\right),\end{split}

(6.62)

because of (
6.58
) and Lemma
6.7
.
Hence
\hat{l}_{\left((\rho,p),C_{(\rho,p)}\right)}:=l_{(\rho,p)}=p
,

\hat{r}_{\left((\rho,p),C_{(\rho,p)}\right)}:=r_{(\rho,p)}=p

defines left/right constraint
using Lemma
6.15
again.
Note that their naturality, as well as the triangle equality follow from
that in
\tilde{\mathcal{M}}
.
The tensor unit is simple because
\pi_{\mathop{\mathrm{bd}}}
is irreducible.

Next we construct a direct sum for

\left((\rho,p),C_{(\rho,p)}\right),\left((\sigma,q),C_{(\sigma,q)}\right)\in{\mathop{\mathrm{Obj}}}\left({\mathcal{Z}}_{a}\left(\tilde{\mathcal{M}}\right)\right)
.
Let
(\gamma,r)\in{\mathop{\mathrm{Obj}}}\left({\tilde{\mathcal{M}}}\right)
be the direct sum of

(\rho,p),(\sigma,q)\in{\mathop{\mathrm{Obj}}}\left({\tilde{\mathcal{M}}}\right)
,

up\in{\mathop{\mathrm{Mor}}}_{\tilde{\mathcal{M}}}\left((\rho,p),(\gamma,r)\right)
,

vq\in{\mathop{\mathrm{Mor}}}_{\tilde{\mathcal{M}}}\left((\sigma,q),(\gamma,r)\right)

in the proof of Proposition
6.2
.
By Lemma
6.15
, we have
p,q\in{\mathcal{F}}_{{\mathop{\mathrm{bd}}}}^{U}
hence
we have
r=upu^{*}+vqv^{*}\in{\mathcal{F}}_{{\mathop{\mathrm{bd}}}}^{U}
.
Therefore, by Lemma
6.8
,

\left((\gamma,r),\tilde{\iota}\left((-,-),(\gamma,r)\right)\right)

is an object of
{\mathcal{Z}}_{a}\left(\tilde{\mathcal{M}}\right)
.
Because
up,vq\in{\mathcal{F}}_{{\mathop{\mathrm{bd}}}}^{U}
as well,
they are morphisms in
{\mathcal{Z}}_{a}\left(\tilde{\mathcal{M}}\right)

from
\left((\rho,p),C_{(\rho,p)}\right)
,
\left((\sigma,q),C_{(\sigma,q)}\right)
to

\left((\gamma,r),\tilde{\iota}\left((-,-),(\gamma,r)\right)\right)
respectively,
from Lemma
6.15
.
They are isometries such that

upu^{*}+vqv^{*}=r=\mathop{\mathrm{id}}\nolimits_{(\gamma,r)}
.

To obtain a subobject of

\left((\rho,p),C_{(\rho,p)}\right)\in{\mathop{\mathrm{Obj}}}\left({\mathcal{Z}}_{a}\left(\tilde{\mathcal{M}}\right)\right)

and a projection

\displaystyle\begin{split}q\in{\mathop{\mathrm{Mor}}}_{{\mathcal{Z}}_{a}\left(\tilde{\mathcal{M}}\right)}\left(\left((\rho,p),C_{(\rho,p)}\right),\left((\rho,p),C_{(\rho,p)}\right)\right)={\mathop{\mathrm{Mor}}}_{\tilde{\mathcal{M}}}\left((\rho,p),(\rho,p)\right)\cap{\mathcal{F}}_{{\mathop{\mathrm{bd}}}}^{U},\end{split}

(6.63)

recall from the proof of Proposition
6.2

that
(\rho,q)\in{\mathop{\mathrm{Obj}}}\left({\tilde{\mathcal{M}}}\right)
and

q\in{\mathop{\mathrm{Mor}}}_{\tilde{\mathcal{M}}}\left((\rho,q),(\rho,p)\right)
.
Because
q\in{\mathcal{F}}_{{\mathop{\mathrm{bd}}}}^{U}
,
we have

\displaystyle\begin{split}&\left((\rho,q),\tilde{\iota}\left((-,-),(\rho,q)\right)\right)\in{\mathop{\mathrm{Obj}}}\left({\mathcal{Z}}_{a}\left(\tilde{\mathcal{M}}\right)\right),\\
&q\in{\mathop{\mathrm{Mor}}}_{\tilde{\mathcal{M}}}\left((\rho,q),(\rho,p)\right)\cap{\mathcal{F}}_{{\mathop{\mathrm{bd}}}}^{U}={\mathop{\mathrm{Mor}}}_{{\mathcal{Z}}_{a}\left(\tilde{\mathcal{M}}\right)}\left(\left((\rho,q),\tilde{\iota}\left((-,-),(\rho,q)\right)\right),\left((\rho,p),C_{(\rho,p)}\right)\right),\end{split}

(6.64)

by Lemma
6.8
, Lemma
6.15
.
Because
q^{*}q=q
and
qq^{*}=q
, this gives a subobject.
Hence
{\mathcal{Z}}_{a}\left(\tilde{\mathcal{M}}\right)
is a
C^{*}
-tensor category.

Now we show that (
6.56
) defines
a braiding on
{\mathcal{Z}}_{a}\left(\tilde{\mathcal{M}}\right)
.
For any
\left((\rho,p),C_{(\rho,p)}\right),\left((\sigma,q),C_{(\sigma,q)}\right)\in{\mathop{\mathrm{Obj}}}\left({\mathcal{Z}}_{a}\left(\tilde{\mathcal{M}}\right)\right)
,
Lemma
6.15
implies
p,q\in{\mathcal{F}}_{{\mathop{\mathrm{bd}}}}^{U}
.
By Lemma
6.7
the definition (
6.56
)
means

\displaystyle\begin{split}&\epsilon_{{\mathcal{Z}}_{a}\left(\tilde{\mathcal{M}}\right)}\left(\left((\rho,p),C_{(\rho,p)}\right),\left((\sigma,q),C_{(\sigma,q)}\right)\right):=C_{(\sigma,q)}\left((\rho,p)\right)=\tilde{\iota}\left((\rho,p),(\sigma,q)\right)\\
&\in{\mathop{\mathrm{Mor}}}_{\tilde{\mathcal{M}}}\left((\rho,p)\otimes_{\tilde{\mathcal{M}}}(\sigma,q),(\sigma,q)\otimes_{\tilde{\mathcal{M}}}(\rho,p)\right)\cap{\mathcal{F}}_{{\mathop{\mathrm{bd}}}}^{U}\\
&={\mathop{\mathrm{Mor}}}_{{\mathcal{Z}}_{a}\left(\tilde{\mathcal{M}}\right)}\left(\left((\rho,p),C_{(\rho,p)}\right)\otimes_{{\mathcal{Z}}_{a}\left(\tilde{\mathcal{M}}\right)}\left((\sigma,q),C_{(\sigma,q)}\right),\left((\sigma,q),C_{(\sigma,q)}\right)\otimes_{{\mathcal{Z}}_{a}\left(\tilde{\mathcal{M}}\right)}\left((\rho,p),C_{(\rho,p)}\right)\right).\end{split}

(6.65)

Here we used
p,q\in{\mathcal{F}}_{{\mathop{\mathrm{bd}}}}^{U}
and Lemma
6.15
.
From this and the definition of the identity morphism
and (v) of Definition
6.5
for
C_{(\sigma,q)}
, it is straightforward
to see that this gives an isomorphism.
From the definition of objects and morphisms of
{\mathcal{Z}}_{a}\left(\tilde{\mathcal{M}}\right)
,
the naturality for the braiding hold.
The definition of the tensor product (
6.58
)
and definition of the half-braiding Definition
6.5
(iii)
guarantee the hexagon axiom.

Hence
{\mathcal{Z}}_{a}\left(\tilde{\mathcal{M}}\right)
is a braided
C^{*}
-tensor category.

\square

Finally, we get the following bulk-edge correspondence.

Theorem 6.17
.

Let
(\Lambda_{0},\Lambda_{r0})\in\mathcal{PC}
.
Consider the setting in subsection
1.1
, and
assume Assumption
1.4
, Assumption
1.6
, Assumption
1.7
,
Assumption
1.3
, Assumption
1.9
,
Assumption
1.14
and
Assumption
6.11
.
(Note that Assumption
1.10
automatically holds.)
Then the functor
\Psi:C_{\mathop{\mathrm{bk},\Lambda_{0}}}\to{\mathcal{Z}}_{a}\left(\tilde{\mathcal{M}}\right)
given by

\displaystyle\begin{split}&\Psi(\rho):=\left(\left(F_{0}^{(\Lambda_{0},\Lambda_{r0})}(\rho),\mathbb{I}\right),\tilde{\iota}\left((-,-),(F_{0}^{(\Lambda_{0},\Lambda_{r0})}(\rho),\mathbb{I})\right)\right),\quad\rho\in O_{\mathop{\mathrm{bk},\Lambda_{0}}}^{U},\\
&\Psi(R):=F_{1}^{(\Lambda_{0},\Lambda_{r0})}(R),\quad R\in(\rho,\sigma),\quad\rho,\sigma\in O_{\mathop{\mathrm{bk},\Lambda_{0}}}^{U}=O_{\mathop{\mathrm{bk},\Lambda_{0}}}\end{split}

(6.66)

is an equivalence of braided
C^{*}
-tensor categories.
Here
F^{(\Lambda_{0},\Lambda_{r0})}
is the functor from Theorem
4.7

F^{(\Lambda_{0},\Lambda_{r0})}:C_{\mathop{\mathrm{bk},\Lambda_{0}}}\to C_{\mathop{\mathrm{bd}},\Lambda_{0}}^{\mathop{\mathrm{bu}}}
.

First we check
\Psi
is indeed a functor.
Clearly we have
\left(F_{0}^{(\Lambda_{0},\Lambda_{r0})}(\rho),\mathbb{I}\right)\in{\mathop{\mathrm{Obj}}}\left({\tilde{\mathcal{M}}}\right)
by Lemma
4.5

and
Lemma
6.8
then implies
\Psi(\rho)\in{\mathop{\mathrm{Obj}}}\left({\mathcal{Z}}_{a}\left(\tilde{\mathcal{M}}\right)\right)
.
For any
\rho,\sigma\in O_{\mathop{\mathrm{bk},\Lambda_{0}}}^{U}
and
R\in(\rho,\sigma)
,
we have
\Psi(R)\in\left(F_{0}^{(\Lambda_{0},\Lambda_{r0})}(\rho),F_{0}^{(\Lambda_{0},\Lambda_{r0})}(\sigma)\right)_{U}

by Lemma
4.5
, and by Lemma
6.15
,

\left(F_{0}^{(\Lambda_{0},\Lambda_{r0})}(\rho),F_{0}^{(\Lambda_{0},\Lambda_{r0})}(\sigma)\right)_{U}

is equal to
{\mathop{\mathrm{Mor}}}_{{\mathcal{Z}}_{a}\left(\tilde{\mathcal{M}}\right)}\left(\Psi(\rho),\Psi(\sigma)\right)
.
Hence
\Psi(R)\in{\mathop{\mathrm{Mor}}}_{{\mathcal{Z}}_{a}\left(\tilde{\mathcal{M}}\right)}\left(\Psi(\rho),\Psi(\sigma)\right)
.
For any object
\rho\in O_{\mathop{\mathrm{bk},\Lambda_{0}}}^{U}
,
we have
\Psi\left(\mathbb{I}_{\rho}\right)=\Psi(\mathbb{I})=\mathbb{I}=\mathop{\mathrm{id}}\nolimits_{\Psi(\rho)}
.
For any
R\in(\rho,\sigma)
,
S\in(\sigma,\gamma)
for

\rho,\sigma,\gamma\in O_{\mathop{\mathrm{bk},\Lambda_{0}}}^{U}
,
we have

\Psi(SR)=\Psi(S)\Psi(R)
.
Hence
\Psi
is a functor. Clearly it is linear on the morphisms.

Next we see that
\Psi
is a tensor functor.
First,

\displaystyle\begin{split}\varphi_{0}:=\mathbb{I}:\left(\left({\pi_{\mathop{\mathrm{bd}}}},\mathbb{I}\right),\tilde{\iota}\left((-,-),(\pi_{\mathop{\mathrm{bd}}},\mathbb{I})\right)\right)\to\Psi(\pi_{\mathop{\mathrm{bk}}})=\left(\left({\pi_{\mathop{\mathrm{bd}}}},\mathbb{I}\right),\tilde{\iota}\left((-,-),(\pi_{\mathop{\mathrm{bd}}},\mathbb{I})\right)\right)\end{split}

(6.67)

is a morphism by Lemma
6.15

2.
, hence an isomorphism.
Note that

\displaystyle\begin{split}&\Psi(\rho)\otimes_{{\mathcal{Z}}_{a}\left(\tilde{\mathcal{M}}\right)}\Psi(\sigma)\\
&=\left(\left(F_{0}^{(\Lambda_{0},\Lambda_{r0})}(\rho),\mathbb{I}\right)\otimes_{\tilde{\mathcal{M}}}\left(F_{0}^{(\Lambda_{0},\Lambda_{r0})}(\sigma),\mathbb{I}\right),\tilde{\iota}\left((-,-),\left(F_{0}^{(\Lambda_{0},\Lambda_{r0})}(\rho),\mathbb{I}\right)\otimes_{\tilde{\mathcal{M}}}\left(F_{0}^{(\Lambda_{0},\Lambda_{r0})}(\sigma),\mathbb{I}\right)\right)\right)\\
&=\left(\left(\left(F_{0}^{(\Lambda_{0},\Lambda_{r0})}(\rho)\otimes F_{0}^{(\Lambda_{0},\Lambda_{r0})}(\sigma),\mathbb{I}\right),\tilde{\iota}\left((-,-),\left(F_{0}^{(\Lambda_{0},\Lambda_{r0})}(\rho)\otimes F_{0}^{(\Lambda_{0},\Lambda_{r0})}(\sigma),\mathbb{I}\right)\right)\right)\right)\\
&=\left(\left(F_{0}^{(\Lambda_{0},\Lambda_{r0})}(\rho\otimes\sigma),\mathbb{I}\right),\tilde{\iota}\left((--),\left(F_{0}^{(\Lambda_{0},\Lambda_{r0})}(\rho\otimes\sigma),\mathbb{I}\right)\right)\right)=\Psi(\rho\otimes\sigma),\end{split}

(6.68)

by Lemma
4.5
and the calculation (
6.58
).
Hence

\varphi_{2}(\rho,\sigma):=\mathbb{I}:\Psi(\rho)\otimes_{{\mathcal{Z}}_{a}\left(\tilde{\mathcal{M}}\right)}\Psi(\sigma)\to\Psi(\rho\otimes\sigma)

is an isomorphism and it is natural
because the functor
F^{(\Lambda_{0},\Lambda_{r0})}
is a tensor functor:

\displaystyle\begin{split}\Psi(R\otimes S)=F_{1}^{(\Lambda_{0},\Lambda_{r0})}(R\otimes S)=F_{1}^{(\Lambda_{0},\Lambda_{r0})}(R)\otimes F_{1}^{(\Lambda_{0},\Lambda_{r0})}(S)=\Psi(R)\otimes_{{\mathcal{Z}}_{a}\left(\tilde{\mathcal{M}}\right)}\Psi(S),\end{split}

(6.69)

for
R\in(\rho,\rho^{\prime})
,
S\in(\sigma,\sigma^{\prime})
,
\rho,\rho^{\prime},\sigma,\sigma^{\prime}\in O_{\mathop{\mathrm{bk},\Lambda_{0}}}
.
For any
\rho,\sigma,\gamma\in O_{\mathop{\mathrm{bk},\Lambda_{0}}}
,
we have

\displaystyle\begin{split}\Psi\left(\mathbb{I}\right)&\varphi_{2}\left(\rho\otimes\sigma,\gamma\right)\left(\varphi_{2}\left(\rho,\sigma\right)\otimes_{{\mathcal{Z}}_{a}\left(\tilde{\mathcal{M}}\right)}\mathop{\mathrm{id}}\nolimits_{\Psi(\gamma)}\right)=\mathbb{I}=\varphi_{2}\left(\rho,\sigma\otimes\gamma\right)\left(\mathop{\mathrm{id}}\nolimits_{\Psi(\rho)}\otimes_{{\mathcal{Z}}_{a}\left(\tilde{\mathcal{M}}\right)}\varphi_{2}\left(\sigma,\gamma\right)\right)a_{\Psi(\rho),\Psi(\sigma),\Psi(\gamma)},\\
&\Psi(\mathbb{I})\varphi_{2}\left(\pi_{\mathop{\mathrm{bk}}},\rho\right)\left(\varphi_{0}\otimes_{{\mathcal{Z}}_{a}\left(\tilde{\mathcal{M}}\right)}\mathop{\mathrm{id}}\nolimits_{\Psi(\rho)}\right)=\mathbb{I}=\hat{l}_{\Psi(\rho)}\\
&\Psi(\mathbb{I})\varphi_{2}\left(\rho,\pi_{\mathop{\mathrm{bk}}}\right)\left(\mathop{\mathrm{id}}\nolimits_{\Psi(\rho)}\otimes_{{\mathcal{Z}}_{a}\left(\tilde{\mathcal{M}}\right)}\varphi_{0}\right)=\mathbb{I}=\hat{r}_{\Psi(\rho)}\\
\end{split}

(6.70)

Hence
\Psi
is a tensor functor.

The tensor functor
\Psi
is braided
because

\displaystyle\begin{split}&\Psi\left(\epsilon_{-}^{(\Lambda_{0})}(\rho:\sigma)\right)=F_{1}^{(\Lambda_{0},\Lambda_{r0})}\left(\epsilon_{-}^{(\Lambda_{0})}(\rho:\sigma)\right)=\iota^{(\Lambda_{0},\Lambda_{r0})}\left(F_{0}^{(\Lambda_{0},\Lambda_{r0})}(\rho):F_{0}^{(\Lambda_{0},\Lambda_{r0})}(\sigma)\right)\\
&=\tilde{\iota}\left((F_{0}^{(\Lambda_{0},\Lambda_{r0})}(\rho),\mathbb{I}),(F_{0}^{(\Lambda_{0},\Lambda_{r0})}(\sigma),\mathbb{I})\right)=\epsilon_{{\mathcal{Z}}_{a}\left(\tilde{\mathcal{M}}\right)}\left(\Psi(\rho),\Psi(\sigma)\right),\end{split}

(6.71)

by
Lemma
4.6
.

By Lemma
4.5
, Lemma
6.15
and the definition of
\Psi
,

\Psi
is fully faithful.
It is also essentially surjective.
To see this, let
\left((\rho,p),C_{(\rho,p)}\right)\in{\mathop{\mathrm{Obj}}}\left({\mathcal{Z}}_{a}\left(\tilde{\mathcal{M}}\right)\right)
.
By Lemma
6.15
, we have
p\in(\rho,\rho)_{U}
.
Therefore, by Lemma
2.12
,
there exists a
\gamma\in O_{\mathop{\mathrm{bd}},\Lambda_{0}}^{\mathop{\mathrm{bu}}}
and an isometry

v\in(\gamma,\rho)_{U}
such that

vv^{*}=p
.
For this
\gamma\in O_{\mathop{\mathrm{bd}},\Lambda_{0}}^{\mathop{\mathrm{bu}}}
, we get
G(\gamma)\in O_{\mathop{\mathrm{bk},\Lambda_{0}}}
by
Lemma
5.1
.
Then we have

\displaystyle\begin{split}\Psi\left(G(\gamma)\right)=\left((\gamma,\mathbb{I}),\tilde{\iota}\left((-,-),(\gamma,\mathbb{I})\right)\right).\end{split}

(6.72)

Note from Lemma
6.15
that

\displaystyle\begin{split}v\in{\mathop{\mathrm{Mor}}}_{\tilde{\mathcal{M}}}\left((\gamma,\mathbb{I}),(\rho,p)\right)\cap{\mathcal{F}}_{{\mathop{\mathrm{bd}}}}^{U}={\mathop{\mathrm{Mor}}}_{{\mathcal{Z}}_{a}\left(\tilde{\mathcal{M}}\right)}\left(\Psi\left(G(\gamma)\right),\left((\rho,p),C_{(\rho,p)}\right)\right).\end{split}

(6.73)

Because

\displaystyle\begin{split}vv^{*}=p=\mathop{\mathrm{id}}\nolimits_{\left((\rho,p),C_{(\rho,p)}\right)},\quad v^{*}v=\mathbb{I},\end{split}

(6.74)

it is an isomorphism.
Hence
\Psi
is essentially surjective.
Hence
\Psi
is an equivalence of braided
C^{*}
-tensor category.

\square

7
Assumption
1.3

In this section, we give some sufficient condition
for Assumption
1.3
to hold.
We introduce a cone version of TQO (see
[
2
]
for original TQO):

Definition 7.1
.

Let
\Phi
be a positive uniformly bounded finite range interaction on
{\mathcal{A}}_{\mathbb{Z}^{2}}

with a unique frustration-free ground state
\omega
.
Let
r\in{\mathbb{N}}
be the interaction length of
\Phi
.
For each finite subset
S
of
{\mathbb{Z}}^{2}
, we denote by

S_{r}
the set of all points
\bm{x}\in{\mathbb{Z}}^{2}

whose Euclidean distance from
S
are less than or equal to
r
.
We denote by
P_{S_{r}}
the spectral projection of

H_{\Phi}(S_{r}):=\sum_{X\subset S_{r}}\Phi(X)
corresponding to eigenvalue
0
.
We say that
\Phi
satisfies
strict cone TQO

if for any
\theta_{1},\theta_{2}
with
0<\theta_{1}<\frac{\pi}{2}<\theta_{2}<\pi

and
-\infty<a\leq b<\infty
,
there exists a number
m_{\theta_{1},\theta_{2},a,b}\in{\mathbb{N}}

satisfying the following condition :
for any
c\geq r
and
l\geq m_{\theta_{1},\theta_{2},a,b}
,
setting

\displaystyle\begin{split}&S:=\left(\Lambda_{(a,0),\frac{\theta_{2}}{2},\frac{\theta_{2}}{2}}\setminus\Lambda_{(b,0),\frac{\theta_{1}}{2},\frac{\theta_{1}}{2}}\right)\cap\left({\mathbb{Z}}\times[c,c+l]\right),\end{split}

(7.1)

we have

\displaystyle\begin{split}P_{S_{r}}AP_{S_{r}}=\omega(A)P_{S_{r}},\quad A\in{\mathcal{A}}_{S}.\end{split}

(7.2)

Here is some sufficient condition for the strict cone TQO.

Lemma 7.2
.

Let
\Phi
be a positive uniformly bounded finite range commuting interaction
on
{\mathcal{A}}_{\mathbb{Z}^{2}}

with a unique frustration-free ground state
\omega
.
Let
r
be the interaction length of
\Phi
.
Suppose that this
\omega
satisfies the following property:
for any
\theta_{1},\theta_{2}\in{\mathbb{R}}
with

0<\theta_{1}<\frac{\pi}{2}<\theta_{2}<\pi

and
-\infty<a<b<\infty
,
there exists an
m_{\theta_{1},\theta_{2},a,b}\in{\mathbb{N}}

such that

\displaystyle\begin{split}s\left(\left.\omega\right|_{{\mathcal{A}}_{S_{r}}}\right)=P_{S_{r}},\end{split}

(7.3)

holds
for any
l\geq m_{\theta_{1},\theta_{2},a,b}
and
c\geq r
.
Here we set
S:={\left(\Lambda_{(a,0),\frac{\theta_{2}}{2},\frac{\theta_{2}}{2}}\setminus\Lambda_{(b,0),\frac{\theta_{1}}{2},\frac{\theta_{1}}{2}}\right)\cap\left({\mathbb{Z}}\times[c,c+l]\right)}
, and

S_{r}
,
P_{S_{r}}
are defined by the formulas in Definition
7.1

with respect to our
l
and
c
.
The notation

s\left(\left.\omega\right|_{{\mathcal{A}}_{S_{r}}}\right)
indicates the support projection of
the state
\left.\omega\right|_{{\mathcal{A}}_{S_{r}}}
.
Then
\Phi
satisfies the cone strict TQO.

Let
({\mathcal{H}},\pi,\Omega)
be a GNS triple of
\omega
.
For any
\Gamma\subset{\mathbb{Z}}^{2}
,
let
Q_{\Gamma}
be an orthogonal projection onto

\cap_{X\subset\Gamma}\ker\pi\left(\Phi(X)\right)
.
Note that
Q_{S}=\pi\left(\mathop{\mathrm{Proj}}\nolimits[H_{\Phi}(S)=0]\right)\in\pi\left({\mathcal{A}}_{S}\right)
,
for finite
S\subset{\mathbb{Z}}^{2}
.
Here
\mathop{\mathrm{Proj}}\nolimits[H_{\Phi}(S)=0]
denotes the spectral
projection of
H_{\Phi}(S)
corresponding to eigenvalue
0
.

For any
\Gamma\subset{\mathbb{Z}}^{2}

and finite subsets
\Gamma_{n}\Subset\Gamma
with
\Gamma_{n}\uparrow\Gamma
,

Q_{\Gamma_{n}}
is decreasing, hence there is a orthogonal projection

\tilde{Q}
such that

Q_{\Gamma_{n}}\to\tilde{Q}
,
\sigma
-strongly.
In particular, we have
\tilde{Q}\in\pi({\mathcal{A}}_{\Gamma})^{\prime\prime}
.
Because
Q_{\Gamma}\leq Q_{\Gamma_{n}}
for any
n
,
we have
Q_{\Gamma}\leq\tilde{Q}
.
On the other hand, for any finite
X\subset\Gamma
, we have

\displaystyle\pi(\Phi(X))\tilde{Q}=\pi(\Phi(X))Q_{\Gamma_{n}}\tilde{Q}=0

(7.4)

eventually.
Therefore, we have
\tilde{Q}\leq Q_{\Gamma}
.
Hence we conclude
\tilde{Q}=Q_{\Gamma}
.
In particular, we have
Q_{\Gamma}\in\pi({\mathcal{A}}_{\Gamma})^{\prime\prime}
.

Because our interaction is commuting,
for any finite
S_{1},S_{2}\subset{\mathbb{Z}}^{2}
,
H_{\Phi}(S_{1})
and
H_{\Phi}(S_{2})
commute.
Therefore, we have

[Q_{S_{1}},Q_{S_{2}}]=0
.
Therefore, for any
\Gamma_{1},\Gamma_{2}\subset{\mathbb{Z}}^{2}
,
we have
[Q_{\Gamma_{1}},Q_{\Gamma_{2}}]=0
.
In particular,
Q_{\Gamma_{1}}Q_{\Gamma_{2}}
is an orthogonal projection.

For any finite
S\subset{\mathbb{Z}}^{2}
,
we have
Q_{S_{r}}Q_{S^{c}}=Q_{{\mathbb{Z}}^{2}}
.
To see this,
multiply
Q_{S_{r}}\geq Q_{{\mathbb{Z}}^{2}}
by

Q_{S^{c}}
from left and right.
Then we obtain

\displaystyle\begin{split}Q_{S_{r}}Q_{S^{c}}=Q_{S^{c}}Q_{S_{r}}Q_{S^{c}}\geq Q_{S^{c}}Q_{{\mathbb{Z}}^{2}}Q_{S^{c}}=Q_{{\mathbb{Z}}^{2}}.\end{split}

(7.5)

On the other hand, because
r
is the interaction length of

\Phi
, for any finite subset
X
of
{\mathbb{Z}}^{2}
with
\Phi(X)\neq 0
,
we have
X\subset S_{r}
or
X\subset S^{c}
.
Therefore,
for any finite subset
X
of
{\mathbb{Z}}^{2}
, we have

\Phi(X)Q_{S_{r}}Q_{S^{c}}=0
.
It means
Q_{S_{r}}Q_{S^{c}}\leq Q_{{\mathbb{Z}}^{2}}
, showing the claim.

Because
\omega
is the unique frustration-free state, it is pure and

\pi
is irreducible. Therefore,

Q_{{\mathbb{Z}}^{2}}
is an orthogonal projection onto
{\mathbb{C}}\Omega
.
Therefore, for any finite
S\subset{\mathbb{Z}}^{2}
and
for any
A\in{\mathcal{A}}_{S}
, we have

\displaystyle\begin{split}\pi\left(P_{S_{r}}AP_{S_{r}}\right)\Omega=Q_{S_{r}}\pi(A)\Omega=Q_{S_{r}}\pi(A)Q_{S^{c}}\Omega=Q_{S_{r}}Q_{S^{c}}\pi(A)\Omega=Q_{{\mathbb{Z}}^{2}}\pi(A)\Omega=\omega(A)\Omega.\end{split}

(7.6)

In the third equality, we recalled
Q_{S^{c}}\in\pi({\mathcal{A}}_{S^{c}})^{\prime\prime}
.

Now we would like to remove
\Omega
from the equality above
for
S
described in the Lemma.
Let
\theta_{1},\theta_{2}\in{\mathbb{R}}
with

0<\theta_{1}<\frac{\pi}{2}<\theta_{2}<\pi

and
-\infty<a<b<\infty
,
and let
m_{\theta_{1},\theta_{2},a,b}\in{\mathbb{N}}
be the number
given by the assumption of the Lemma.
Let
l\geq m_{\theta_{1},\theta_{2},a,b}
and
c\geq r

and set

S:={\left(\Lambda_{(a,0),\frac{\theta_{2}}{2},\frac{\theta_{2}}{2}}\setminus\Lambda_{(b,0),\frac{\theta_{1}}{2},\frac{\theta_{1}}{2}}\right)\cap\left({\mathbb{Z}}\times[c,c+l]\right)}
.
Let
{\mathcal{K}}
be a Hilbert space
and
U:{\mathcal{H}}\to{\mathcal{H}}_{S_{r}}\otimes{\mathcal{K}}
a unitary
such that

\displaystyle\begin{split}&\mathop{\mathrm{Ad}}\nolimits U\left(\pi(A)\right)=A\otimes\mathbb{I},\quad A\in{\mathcal{A}}_{S_{r}},\\
&\mathop{\mathrm{Ad}}\nolimits U\left(\pi\left({\mathcal{A}}_{S_{r}^{c}}\right)^{\prime\prime}\right)=\mathbb{I}\otimes{\mathcal{B}}({\mathcal{K}}).\end{split}

(7.7)

Here we set
{\mathcal{H}}_{S_{r}}:=\bigotimes_{z\in S_{r}}{\mathbb{C}}^{d}
.
Let

\displaystyle\begin{split}U\Omega=\sum c_{i}\xi_{i}\otimes\eta_{i}\in{\mathcal{H}}_{S_{r}}\otimes{\mathcal{K}},\quad c_{i}\neq 0\end{split}

(7.8)

be a Schmidt decomposition.
By the assumption,
we have

s\left(\left.\omega\right|_{S_{r}}\right)=P_{S_{r}}
.
Note that
with the Schmidt decomposition above,
the density matrix of
\omega|_{S_{r}}

is

\displaystyle\sum_{i}|c_{i}|^{2}\left|\xi_{i}\right\rangle\left\langle\xi_{i}\right|.

(7.9)

Therefore,
\{\xi_{i}\}
is the CONS of
P_{S_{r}}{\mathcal{H}}_{S_{r}}
.

Now using the Schmidt decomposition and (
7.7
), we have

\displaystyle\begin{split}&\sum c_{i}P_{S_{r}}AP_{S_{r}}\xi_{i}\otimes\eta_{i}=\left(P_{S_{r}}AP_{S_{r}}\otimes\mathbb{I}\right)U\Omega=\mathop{\mathrm{Ad}}\nolimits U\left(\pi(P_{S_{r}}AP_{S_{r}})\right)\cdot U\Omega\\
&=U\pi(P_{S_{r}}AP_{S_{r}})\Omega=\omega(A)U\Omega=\omega(A)\sum c_{i}\xi_{i}\otimes\eta_{i},\end{split}

(7.10)

for any
A\in{\mathcal{A}}_{S}
.
Applying
\left\langle\eta_{i}\right|
on this, and using
c_{i}\neq 0
, we obtain

\displaystyle\begin{split}P_{S_{r}}AP_{S_{r}}\xi_{i}=\omega(A)\xi_{i},\end{split}

(7.11)

for any
A\in{\mathcal{A}}_{S}
.
Because
\{\xi_{i}\}
is a CONS of
P_{S_{r}}{\mathcal{H}}_{S_{r}}
,
this means that

P_{S_{r}}AP_{S_{r}}=\omega(A)P_{S_{r}}
,
for any
A\in{\mathcal{A}}_{S}
.
This completes the proof.

\square

Proposition 7.3
.

Let
\Phi
be a positive uniformly bounded finite range interaction on
{\mathcal{A}}_{\mathbb{Z}^{2}}

with a unique frustration-free state
\omega_{\mathop{\mathrm{bk}}}
, satisfying the strict cone TQO.
Let
r\in{\mathbb{N}}
be the interaction length of
\Phi
.
Let
\Phi_{\mathrm{bd}}
be
a positive uniformly bounded finite range interaction on
{\mathcal{A}}_{\mathop{\mathrm{H}_{U}}}

with a unique frustration-free state
\omega_{\mathrm{bd}}
.
Suppose there exists
r\leq R\in{\mathbb{N}}
such that

\displaystyle\begin{split}\Phi(X)=\Phi_{\mathrm{bd}}(X),\quad\text{for all}\quad X\Subset{\mathbb{Z}}\times[R,\infty).\end{split}

(7.12)

Let
({\mathcal{H}}_{\mathop{\mathrm{bk}}},\pi_{\mathop{\mathrm{bk}}},\Omega_{\mathrm{bk}})
,
({\mathcal{H}}_{\mathop{\mathrm{bd}}},\pi_{\mathop{\mathrm{bd}}},\Omega_{\mathrm{bd}})

be GNS triples of
\omega_{\mathop{\mathrm{bk}}}
,
\omega_{\mathop{\mathrm{bd}}}
respectively.
Then for any
\Lambda\in{\mathcal{C}}_{{\mathop{\mathrm{bk}}}}^{U}
,

\pi_{\mathop{\mathrm{bk}}}|_{\Lambda\cap\mathop{\mathrm{H}_{U}}}
and

\pi_{\mathop{\mathrm{bd}}}|_{\Lambda\cap\mathop{\mathrm{H}_{U}}}
are quasi-equivalent.

Fix any
\Lambda\in{\mathcal{C}}_{{\mathop{\mathrm{bk}}}}^{U}
.
Then there exist
\theta_{1},\theta_{2}
with
0<\theta_{1}<\frac{\pi}{2}<\theta_{2}<\pi

and
-\infty<a\leq b<\infty

such that
\Lambda\cap\mathop{\mathrm{H}_{U}}\subset\left(\Lambda_{(a,0),\frac{\theta_{2}}{2},\frac{\theta_{2}}{2}}\setminus\Lambda_{(b,0),\frac{\theta_{1}}{2},\frac{\theta_{1}}{2}}\right)
.
We have

\displaystyle\begin{split}\left.\omega_{\mathop{\mathrm{bk}}}\right|_{{\mathcal{A}}_{\left(\Lambda_{(a,0),\frac{\theta_{2}}{2},\frac{\theta_{2}}{2}}\setminus\Lambda_{(b,0),\frac{\theta_{1}}{2},\frac{\theta_{1}}{2}}\right)\cap\left({\mathbb{Z}}\times[2R,\infty)\right)}}=\left.\omega_{\mathop{\mathrm{bd}}}\right|_{{\mathcal{A}}_{\left(\Lambda_{(a,0),\frac{\theta_{2}}{2},\frac{\theta_{2}}{2}}\setminus\Lambda_{(b,0),\frac{\theta_{1}}{2},\frac{\theta_{1}}{2}}\right)\cap\left({\mathbb{Z}}\times[2R,\infty)\right)}}.\end{split}

(7.13)

In fact, for any local
A\in{\mathcal{A}}_{\left(\Lambda_{(a,0),\frac{\theta_{2}}{2},\frac{\theta_{2}}{2}}\setminus\Lambda_{(b,0),\frac{\theta_{1}}{2},\frac{\theta_{1}}{2}}\right)\cap\left({\mathbb{Z}}\times[2R,\infty)\right)}
,
there exists
l\geq m_{\theta_{1}\theta_{1}ab}
(given by Definition
7.1
),
such that

\displaystyle A\in{\mathcal{A}}_{\left(\Lambda_{(a,0),\frac{\theta_{2}}{2},\frac{\theta_{2}}{2}}\setminus\Lambda_{(b,0),\frac{\theta_{1}}{2},\frac{\theta_{1}}{2}}\right)\cap\left({\mathbb{Z}}\times[2R,2R+l]\right)}.

(7.14)

Setting
S:={\left(\Lambda_{(a,0),\frac{\theta_{2}}{2},\frac{\theta_{2}}{2}}\setminus\Lambda_{(b,0),\frac{\theta_{1}}{2},\frac{\theta_{1}}{2}}\right)\cap\left({\mathbb{Z}}\times[2R,2R+l]\right)}
,
by strict cone TQO, we have

P_{S_{r}}AP_{S_{r}}=\omega_{\mathop{\mathrm{bk}}}(A)P_{S_{r}}
.
Because

\displaystyle\begin{split}P_{S_{r}}=\mathop{\mathrm{Proj}}\nolimits\left[H_{\Phi}(S_{r})=0\right]=\mathop{\mathrm{Proj}}\nolimits\left[H_{\Phi_{\mathrm{bd}}}(S_{r})=0\right],\end{split}

(7.15)

we have

\displaystyle\begin{split}\omega_{\mathop{\mathrm{bd}}}(A)=\omega_{\mathop{\mathrm{bd}}}\left(P_{S_{r}}AP_{S_{r}}\right)=\omega_{\mathop{\mathrm{bk}}}(A)\omega_{\mathop{\mathrm{bd}}}\left(P_{S_{r}}\right)=\omega_{\mathop{\mathrm{bk}}}(A).\end{split}

(7.16)

As this holds for any local
A\in{\mathcal{A}}_{\left(\Lambda_{(a,0),\frac{\theta_{2}}{2},\frac{\theta_{2}}{2}}\setminus\Lambda_{(b,0),\frac{\theta_{1}}{2},\frac{\theta_{1}}{2}}\right)\cap\left({\mathbb{Z}}\times[2R,\infty)\right)}
,
we obtain (
7.13
).

Set
\Gamma:={\left(\Lambda_{(a,0),\frac{\theta_{2}}{2},\frac{\theta_{2}}{2}}\setminus\Lambda_{(b,0),\frac{\theta_{1}}{2},\frac{\theta_{1}}{2}}\right)\cap\left({\mathbb{Z}}\times[2R,\infty)\right)}
.
Then

\displaystyle\begin{split}{\mathcal{A}}_{\left(\Lambda_{(a,0),\frac{\theta_{2}}{2},\frac{\theta_{2}}{2}}\setminus\Lambda_{(b,0),\frac{\theta_{1}}{2},\frac{\theta_{1}}{2}}\right)}={\mathcal{A}}_{\left(\Lambda_{(a,0),\frac{\theta_{2}}{2},\frac{\theta_{2}}{2}}\setminus\Lambda_{(b,0),\frac{\theta_{1}}{2},\frac{\theta_{1}}{2}}\right)\setminus\Gamma}\otimes{\mathcal{A}}_{\Gamma},\end{split}

(7.17)

where
\left(\Lambda_{(a,0),\frac{\theta_{2}}{2},\frac{\theta_{2}}{2}}\setminus\Lambda_{(b,0),\frac{\theta_{1}}{2},\frac{\theta_{1}}{2}}\right)\setminus\Gamma

is finite.
Because (
7.13
) holds,
by
[
1
]
Lemma 6.2.55, states

\omega_{\mathop{\mathrm{bd}}}|_{\left(\Lambda_{(a,0),\frac{\theta_{2}}{2},\frac{\theta_{2}}{2}}\setminus\Lambda_{(b,0),\frac{\theta_{1}}{2},\frac{\theta_{1}}{2}}\right)}

and
\omega_{\mathop{\mathrm{bk}}}|_{\left(\Lambda_{(a,0),\frac{\theta_{2}}{2},\frac{\theta_{2}}{2}}\setminus\Lambda_{(b,0),\frac{\theta_{1}}{2},\frac{\theta_{1}}{2}}\right)}

are quasi-equivalent.

Because
\omega_{\mathop{\mathrm{bk}}}
,
\omega_{\mathop{\mathrm{bd}}}
are pure,

\pi_{\mathop{\mathrm{bk}}}\left({\mathcal{A}}_{\left(\Lambda_{(a,0),\frac{\theta_{2}}{2},\frac{\theta_{2}}{2}}\setminus\Lambda_{(b,0),\frac{\theta_{1}}{2},\frac{\theta_{1}}{2}}\right)}\right)^{\prime\prime}
and

\pi_{\mathop{\mathrm{bd}}}\left({\mathcal{A}}_{\left(\Lambda_{(a,0),\frac{\theta_{2}}{2},\frac{\theta_{2}}{2}}\setminus\Lambda_{(b,0),\frac{\theta_{1}}{2},\frac{\theta_{1}}{2}}\right)}\right)^{\prime\prime}

are factors.
Hence, with GNS representations

\pi_{1}
,
\pi_{2}

of states
\left.\omega_{\mathop{\mathrm{bk}}}\right|_{{\mathcal{A}}_{\left(\Lambda_{(a,0),\frac{\theta_{2}}{2},\frac{\theta_{2}}{2}}\setminus\Lambda_{(b,0),\frac{\theta_{1}}{2},\frac{\theta_{1}}{2}}\right)}}
and

\left.\omega_{\mathop{\mathrm{bd}}}\right|_{{\mathcal{A}}_{\left(\Lambda_{(a,0),\frac{\theta_{2}}{2},\frac{\theta_{2}}{2}}\setminus\Lambda_{(b,0),\frac{\theta_{1}}{2},\frac{\theta_{1}}{2}}\right)}}

respectively
on
{\mathcal{A}}_{\left(\Lambda_{(a,0),\frac{\theta_{2}}{2},\frac{\theta_{2}}{2}}\setminus\Lambda_{(b,0),\frac{\theta_{1}}{2},\frac{\theta_{1}}{2}}\right)}
,
there are
*
-isomorphisms

\displaystyle\begin{split}\tau_{1}:\pi_{\mathop{\mathrm{bk}}}\left({\mathcal{A}}_{\left(\Lambda_{(a,0),\frac{\theta_{2}}{2},\frac{\theta_{2}}{2}}\setminus\Lambda_{(b,0),\frac{\theta_{1}}{2},\frac{\theta_{1}}{2}}\right)}\right)^{\prime\prime}\to\pi_{1}\left({\mathcal{A}}_{\left(\Lambda_{(a,0),\frac{\theta_{2}}{2},\frac{\theta_{2}}{2}}\setminus\Lambda_{(b,0),\frac{\theta_{1}}{2},\frac{\theta_{1}}{2}}\right)}\right)^{\prime\prime},\\
\tau_{2}:\pi_{\mathop{\mathrm{bd}}}\left({\mathcal{A}}_{\left(\Lambda_{(a,0),\frac{\theta_{2}}{2},\frac{\theta_{2}}{2}}\setminus\Lambda_{(b,0),\frac{\theta_{1}}{2},\frac{\theta_{1}}{2}}\right)}\right)^{\prime\prime}\to\pi_{2}\left({\mathcal{A}}_{\left(\Lambda_{(a,0),\frac{\theta_{2}}{2},\frac{\theta_{2}}{2}}\setminus\Lambda_{(b,0),\frac{\theta_{1}}{2},\frac{\theta_{1}}{2}}\right)}\right)^{\prime\prime},\end{split}

(7.18)

such that

\displaystyle\begin{split}\tau_{1}\pi_{\mathop{\mathrm{bk}}}|_{{{\mathcal{A}}_{\left(\Lambda_{(a,0),\frac{\theta_{2}}{2},\frac{\theta_{2}}{2}}\setminus\Lambda_{(b,0),\frac{\theta_{1}}{2},\frac{\theta_{1}}{2}}\right)}}}=\pi_{1},\quad\tau_{2}\pi_{\mathop{\mathrm{bd}}}|_{{{\mathcal{A}}_{\left(\Lambda_{(a,0),\frac{\theta_{2}}{2},\frac{\theta_{2}}{2}}\setminus\Lambda_{(b,0),\frac{\theta_{1}}{2},\frac{\theta_{1}}{2}}\right)}}}=\pi_{2}.\end{split}

(7.19)

By the quasi-equivalence of

\omega_{\mathop{\mathrm{bd}}}|_{{\mathcal{A}}_{\left(\Lambda_{(a,0),\frac{\theta_{2}}{2},\frac{\theta_{2}}{2}}\setminus\Lambda_{(b,0),\frac{\theta_{1}}{2},\frac{\theta_{1}}{2}}\right)}}

and
\omega_{\mathop{\mathrm{bk}}}|_{{\mathcal{A}}_{\left(\Lambda_{(a,0),\frac{\theta_{2}}{2},\frac{\theta_{2}}{2}}\setminus\Lambda_{(b,0),\frac{\theta_{1}}{2},\frac{\theta_{1}}{2}}\right)}}
,
there exists a
*
-isomorphism

\displaystyle\begin{split}\tau_{0}:\pi_{1}\left({\mathcal{A}}_{\left(\Lambda_{(a,0),\frac{\theta_{2}}{2},\frac{\theta_{2}}{2}}\setminus\Lambda_{(b,0),\frac{\theta_{1}}{2},\frac{\theta_{1}}{2}}\right)}\right)^{\prime\prime}\to\pi_{2}\left({\mathcal{A}}_{\left(\Lambda_{(a,0),\frac{\theta_{2}}{2},\frac{\theta_{2}}{2}}\setminus\Lambda_{(b,0),\frac{\theta_{1}}{2},\frac{\theta_{1}}{2}}\right)}\right)^{\prime\prime}\end{split}

(7.20)

such that
\tau_{0}\pi_{1}=\pi_{2}
.

Hence we obtain a
*
-isomorphism

\displaystyle\begin{split}\tau_{2}^{-1}\tau_{0}\tau_{1}:\pi_{\mathop{\mathrm{bk}}}\left({\mathcal{A}}_{\left(\Lambda_{(a,0),\frac{\theta_{2}}{2},\frac{\theta_{2}}{2}}\setminus\Lambda_{(b,0),\frac{\theta_{1}}{2},\frac{\theta_{1}}{2}}\right)}\right)^{\prime\prime}\to\pi_{\mathop{\mathrm{bd}}}\left({\mathcal{A}}_{\left(\Lambda_{(a,0),\frac{\theta_{2}}{2},\frac{\theta_{2}}{2}}\setminus\Lambda_{(b,0),\frac{\theta_{1}}{2},\frac{\theta_{1}}{2}}\right)}\right)^{\prime\prime}\end{split}

(7.21)

such that

\displaystyle\begin{split}\left.\tau_{2}^{-1}\tau_{0}\tau_{1}\pi_{\mathop{\mathrm{bk}}}\right|_{{\mathcal{A}}_{\left(\Lambda_{(a,0),\frac{\theta_{2}}{2},\frac{\theta_{2}}{2}}\setminus\Lambda_{(b,0),\frac{\theta_{1}}{2},\frac{\theta_{1}}{2}}\right)}}=\left.\pi_{\mathop{\mathrm{bd}}}\right|_{{\mathcal{A}}_{\left(\Lambda_{(a,0),\frac{\theta_{2}}{2},\frac{\theta_{2}}{2}}\setminus\Lambda_{(b,0),\frac{\theta_{1}}{2},\frac{\theta_{1}}{2}}\right)}}.\end{split}

(7.22)

Restricting this to
{\mathcal{A}}_{\Lambda\cap\mathop{\mathrm{H}_{U}}}
,

\left.\pi_{\mathop{\mathrm{bd}}}\right|_{{\mathcal{A}}_{\Lambda\cap\mathop{\mathrm{H}_{U}}}}

is quasi-equivalent to

\left.\pi_{\mathop{\mathrm{bk}}}\right|_{{\mathcal{A}}_{\Lambda\cap\mathop{\mathrm{H}_{U}}}}
.

\square

Acknowledgment.

The author is grateful to Dr. Daniel Ranard for asking her this question,
and to Professor Zhenghan Wang, Professor Liang Kong and Professor Yasuyuki Kawahigashi
for helpful discussions.

Appendix A
Basic Lemmas

For
C^{*}
-algebras
{\mathcal{A}}
,
{\mathcal{B}}
and
\varepsilon>0
,
we write
{\mathcal{A}}\subset_{\varepsilon}{\mathcal{B}}
if
for any
a\in{\mathcal{A}}
, there exists a
b\in{\mathcal{B}}
such that
\left\|b\right\|\leq\left\|a\right\|

and
\left\|a-b\right\|\leq\varepsilon\left\|a\right\|
.
For a
C^{*}
-algebra
{\mathcal{B}}
,
{\mathcal{U}}({\mathcal{B}})
denotes unitaries in
{\mathcal{B}}
.

Lemma A.1
.

For any
0<\varepsilon<1
, there exists
0<\delta_{1}(\varepsilon)<1
satisfying the following:
For any unital
C^{*}
-algebra
{\mathcal{A}}
, projections
p,q
in
{\mathcal{A}}

such that
\left\|q-qp\right\|<\delta_{1}(\varepsilon)
,
there exists a partial isometry
w\in{\mathcal{A}}

such that
ww^{*}=q
,
w^{*}w\leq p
and
\left\|w-q\right\|<\varepsilon
.

Lemma 2.5.2 of
[
18
]
and its proof.

\square

Lemma A.2
.

For any
0<\varepsilon<1
, there exists
0<\delta_{2}(\varepsilon)<1
satisfying the following:
For any unital
C^{*}
-algebra
{\mathcal{A}}
, projections
p,q
in
{\mathcal{A}}

such that
\left\|q-p\right\|<\delta_{2}(\varepsilon)
,
there exists a unitary
u\in{\mathcal{A}}
such that

p=uqu^{*}
and
\left\|u-\mathbb{I}\right\|<\varepsilon
.

Lemma 2.5.1 of
[
18
]
.

\square

Lemma A.3
.

For any
0<\varepsilon<1
, there exists
0<\delta_{3}(\varepsilon)<1
satisfying the following:
For any Hilbert space
{\mathcal{H}}
, a unital
C^{*}
-algebra
{\mathcal{A}}
on
{\mathcal{H}}
, a projection

p\in{\mathcal{B}}({\mathcal{H}})
, and a self-adjoint
x\in{\mathcal{A}}

such that
\left\|p-x\right\|<\delta_{3}(\varepsilon)
,
there exists a projection
q
in
{\mathcal{A}}
such that

\left\|p-q\right\|<\varepsilon
.

Lemma 2.5.4 of
[
18
]
.

\square

Appendix B
Conditional expectations and approximations

We frequently use the following Lemma.

Lemma B.1
.

Let
\Gamma\subset{\mathbb{Z}}^{2}
and
S
a finite subset of
\Gamma
.
Let
({\mathcal{H}},\pi)
be a representation of
{\mathcal{A}}_{\Gamma}
.
Let
\{e_{IJ}:I,J\in{\mathcal{I}}\}
be a system of matrix units of
{\mathcal{A}}_{S}
with indices
{\mathcal{I}}
, and fix one

I_{0}\in{\mathcal{I}}
.
Define
{\mathcal{E}}_{S}:{\mathcal{B}}({\mathcal{H}})\to{\mathcal{B}}({\mathcal{H}})
by

\displaystyle{\mathcal{E}}_{S}(x):=\sum_{I\in{\mathcal{I}}}\pi(e_{II_{0}})x\pi(e_{I_{0}I}),\quad x\in{\mathcal{B}}({\mathcal{H}}).

(B.1)

Then
{\mathcal{E}}_{S}\left({\mathcal{B}}({\mathcal{H}})\right)=\pi({\mathcal{A}}_{S})^{\prime}
and

{\mathcal{E}}_{S}:{\mathcal{B}}({\mathcal{H}})\to\pi({\mathcal{A}}_{S})^{\prime}
is a projection of norm
1
.
Furthermore,
{\mathcal{E}}^{\Gamma}_{S}:={\mathcal{E}}_{S}|_{\pi({\mathcal{A}}_{\Gamma})^{\prime\prime}}:\pi({\mathcal{A}}_{\Gamma})^{\prime\prime}\to\pi({\mathcal{A}}_{\Gamma\setminus S})^{\prime\prime}
is a surjection.

That
{\mathcal{E}}_{S}:{\mathcal{B}}({\mathcal{H}})\to\pi({\mathcal{A}}_{S})^{\prime}
is a projection or norm
1
is easy to check.
Because
\pi({\mathcal{A}}_{\Gamma\setminus S})^{\prime\prime}\subset\pi({\mathcal{A}}_{S})^{\prime}
,
we have
\pi({\mathcal{A}}_{\Gamma\setminus S})^{\prime\prime}={\mathcal{E}}_{S}\left(\pi({\mathcal{A}}_{\Gamma\setminus S})^{\prime\prime}\right)\subset{\mathcal{E}}_{S}\left(\pi({\mathcal{A}}_{\Gamma})^{\prime\prime}\right)
.
Any
A\in{\mathcal{A}}_{\Gamma,\mathop{\mathrm{loc}}}
can be written as

A=\sum_{IJ}e_{IJ}a_{IJ}
with some
a_{IJ}\in{\mathcal{A}}_{\Gamma\setminus S}
.
We then have
{\mathcal{E}}_{S}\left(\pi(A)\right)=\sum_{IJ}{\mathcal{E}}_{S}(\pi(e_{IJ}))\pi(a_{IJ})=\pi(a_{I_{0}I_{0}})\in\pi\left({\mathcal{A}}_{\Gamma\setminus S}\right)^{\prime\prime}
.
Because
{\mathcal{E}}_{S}
is
\sigma
-weak-continuous, this implies

{\mathcal{E}}_{S}\left(\pi({\mathcal{A}}_{\Gamma})^{\prime\prime}\right)\subset\pi({\mathcal{A}}_{\Gamma\setminus S})^{\prime\prime}
.

\square

Lemma B.2
.

Let
\Lambda_{1},\Lambda_{2},\Gamma
be non-empty subsets of
{\mathbb{Z}}^{2}

satisfying
\Lambda_{1},\Lambda_{2}\subset\Gamma
.
Let
({\mathcal{H}},\pi)
be a representation of
{\mathcal{A}}_{\Gamma}
.
Suppose that
x\in\pi\left({\mathcal{A}}_{(\Lambda_{1})^{c}\cap\Gamma}\right)^{\prime}
,

y\in\pi\left({\mathcal{A}}_{\Lambda_{2}}\right)^{\prime\prime}
,

\varepsilon>0
satisfy
\left\|x-y\right\|\leq\varepsilon
.
Then there exists
z\in\pi\left({\mathcal{A}}_{(\Lambda_{1})^{c}\cap\Gamma}\right)^{\prime}\cap\pi\left({\mathcal{A}}_{\Lambda_{2}}\right)^{\prime\prime}

satisfying
\left\|x-z\right\|\leq\varepsilon
.
If
y
is self-adjoint, then
z
can be taken to be self-adjoint.

If
{\Lambda_{1}}^{c}\cap\Gamma\cap{\Lambda_{2}}=\emptyset
,
then
z=y
satisfies the condition.
Suppose
{\Lambda_{1}}^{c}\cap\Gamma\cap{\Lambda_{2}}\neq\emptyset
.

Fix an increasing sequence
\{S_{n}\}_{n=1}^{\infty}
of finite subsets of
\Lambda_{1}^{c}\cap\Gamma\cap{\Lambda_{2}}

such that
S_{n}\uparrow\Lambda_{1}^{c}\cap\Gamma\cap{\Lambda_{2}}
.
For each
S_{n}
, there exists a projection of norm
1
,

{\mathcal{E}}_{S_{n}}:{\mathcal{B}}({\mathcal{H}})\to\pi\left({\mathcal{A}}_{S_{n}}\right)^{\prime}
, by Lemma
B.1
.

By the definition of
{\mathcal{E}}_{S_{n}}
, we have

\displaystyle z_{n}:={\mathcal{E}}_{S_{n}}(y)=\sum_{I\in{\mathcal{I}}}\pi(e_{II_{0}})y\pi(e_{I_{0}I})\in\pi\left({\mathcal{A}}_{S_{n}}\right)^{\prime},

(B.2)

with a system of matrix units
\{e_{IJ}\}
of
{\mathcal{A}}_{S_{n}}
.
Because
y\in\pi\left({\mathcal{A}}_{\Lambda_{2}}\right)^{\prime\prime}
and

\displaystyle\pi(e_{II_{0}})\in\pi\left({\mathcal{A}}_{S_{n}}\right)\subset\pi\left({\mathcal{A}}_{\Lambda_{1}^{c}\cap\Gamma\cap{\Lambda_{2}}}\right)\subset\pi\left({\mathcal{A}}_{\Lambda_{2}}\right),

(B.3)

we get
z_{n}\in\pi\left({\mathcal{A}}_{\Lambda_{2}}\right)^{\prime\prime}\cap\pi\left({\mathcal{A}}_{S_{n}}\right)^{\prime}
.
Because

x\in\pi\left({\mathcal{A}}_{(\Lambda_{1})^{c}\cap\Gamma}\right)^{\prime}\subset\pi\left({\mathcal{A}}_{S_{n}}\right)^{\prime}
,
we have
{\mathcal{E}}_{S_{n}}(x)=x
.
Because
{\mathcal{E}}_{S_{n}}
is of norm
1
, we have

\displaystyle\left\|x-z_{n}\right\|=\left\|{\mathcal{E}}_{S_{n}}\left(x-y\right)\right\|\leq\left\|x-y\right\|\leq\varepsilon,\quad\left\|z_{n}\right\|\leq\left\|y\right\|.

(B.4)

Because
\{z_{n}\}
is a bounded sequence in
\pi\left({\mathcal{A}}_{\Lambda_{2}}\right)^{\prime\prime}
,
there exists a subnet
\{z_{n_{\alpha}}\}

converging to some
z\in\pi\left({\mathcal{A}}_{\Lambda_{2}}\right)^{\prime\prime}

with respect to the
\sigma
-weak topology.
Because each
z_{n}
belongs to
\pi\left({\mathcal{A}}_{S_{n}}\right)^{\prime}
and

S_{n}\uparrow\Lambda_{1}^{c}\cap\Gamma\cap{\Lambda_{2}}
, we have
z\in\pi\left({\mathcal{A}}_{\Lambda_{1}^{c}\cap\Gamma\cap{\Lambda_{2}}}\right)^{\prime}
.
Hence we get

\displaystyle z\in\pi\left({\mathcal{A}}_{\Lambda_{2}}\right)^{\prime\prime}\cap\pi\left({\mathcal{A}}_{\Lambda_{1}^{c}\cap\Gamma\cap{\Lambda_{2}}}\right)^{\prime}=\pi\left({\mathcal{A}}_{\Lambda_{2}}\right)^{\prime\prime}\cap\pi\left({\mathcal{A}}_{\Lambda_{1}^{c}\cap\Gamma\cap{\Lambda_{2}}}\right)^{\prime}\cap\pi\left({\mathcal{A}}_{{\Lambda_{2}}^{c}}\right)^{\prime}\subset\pi\left({\mathcal{A}}_{\Lambda_{2}}\right)^{\prime\prime}\cap\pi\left({\mathcal{A}}_{\Lambda_{1}^{c}\cap\Gamma}\right)^{\prime}.

(B.5)

For any
\xi,\eta\in{\mathcal{H}}
, we have

\displaystyle\left|\left\langle\eta,(x-z)\xi\right\rangle\right|=\lim_{\alpha}\left|\left\langle\eta,(x-z_{n_{\alpha}})\xi\right\rangle\right|\leq\varepsilon\left\|\eta\right\|\left\|\xi\right\|.

(B.6)

Hence we conclude
\left\|x-z\right\|\leq\varepsilon
.
By the construction,
z
is self-adjoint if
y
is self-adjoint.

\square

Appendix C
O_{\mathop{\mathrm{bd}}}^{\mathop{\mathrm{bu}}}
in
O^{rU}_{\mathop{\mathrm{bd}}}

In this section, we show that
O_{\mathop{\mathrm{bd}}}^{\mathop{\mathrm{bu}}}
can be regarded as a center of
O^{rU}_{\mathop{\mathrm{bd}}}

in a certain sense.
Recall in Lemma
3.11
we obtained

\iota^{(\Lambda_{0},\Lambda_{r0})}\left(\rho_{\Lambda_{0}}:-\right):O^{rU}_{\mathop{\mathrm{bd},\Lambda_{r0}}}\to{\mathcal{U}}({\mathcal{H}}_{\mathop{\mathrm{bd}}})

for each

{\rho_{\Lambda_{0}}}\in O_{\mathop{\mathrm{bd}},\Lambda_{0}}^{\mathop{\mathrm{bu}}}
,
satisfying the naturality Lemma
3.14
.
For each
u\in{\mathcal{U}}({\mathcal{A}}_{\Lambda_{0}^{c}\cap\Lambda_{r0}})
,

\sigma_{u}:=\pi_{\mathop{\mathrm{bd}}}\mathop{\mathrm{Ad}}\nolimits u
defines an element in
O^{rU}_{\mathop{\mathrm{bd},\Lambda_{r0}}}
.
For any
{\Lambda_{r2}}\in{\mathcal{C}}_{{\mathop{\mathrm{bd}}}}^{r}
with
\Lambda_{0}\leftarrow_{r}{\Lambda_{r2}}
,
{\Lambda_{r2}}\subset\Lambda_{r0}
,
t\geq 0
,
we have
\pi_{\mathop{\mathrm{bd}}}(u^{*})\in{\mathcal{V}}_{\sigma_{u}{\Lambda_{r2}}(t)}^{{\mathop{\mathrm{bd}}}U}
.
Therefore, we have
\iota^{(\Lambda_{0},\Lambda_{r0})}\left(\rho_{\Lambda_{0}}:\sigma_{u}\right)=\pi_{\mathop{\mathrm{bd}}}(u)T_{\rho_{\Lambda_{0}}}^{\mathrm{(l)}\Lambda_{r0}\mathbb{I}}\left(\pi_{\mathop{\mathrm{bd}}}(u^{*})\right)=\pi_{\mathop{\mathrm{bd}}}(u){\rho_{\Lambda_{0}}}(u^{*})=\mathbb{I}
.
We further note the following asymptotic property
of this map.

Lemma C.1
.

Consider the setting in subsection
1.1
and assume Assumption
1.6
,
1.7
.
Let
(\Lambda_{0},\Lambda_{r0})\in\mathcal{PC}
and
{\rho_{\Lambda_{0}}}\in O_{\mathop{\mathrm{bd}},\Lambda_{0}}^{\mathop{\mathrm{bu}}}
.
Then for any
{\Lambda_{r2}}\in{\mathcal{C}}_{{\mathop{\mathrm{bd}}}}^{r}
with
\Lambda_{0}\leftarrow_{r}{\Lambda_{r2}}
,
{\Lambda_{r2}}\subset\Lambda_{r0}

we have

\displaystyle\begin{split}\lim_{t\to\infty}\sup_{\sigma\in O^{rU}_{\mathop{\mathrm{bd},{\Lambda_{r2}}(t)}}}\left\|\iota^{(\Lambda_{0},\Lambda_{r0})}\left({\rho_{\Lambda_{0}}}:\sigma\right)-\mathbb{I}\right\|=0.\end{split}

(C.1)

Fix
t\geq 0
.
For any
\sigma\in O^{rU}_{\mathop{\mathrm{bd},{\Lambda_{r2}}(t)}}
,
by (
3.40
), we have

\displaystyle\iota^{(\Lambda_{0},\Lambda_{r0})}\left({\rho_{\Lambda_{0}}}:\sigma\right)=\lim_{t_{2}\to\infty}\left(V_{\sigma,{\Lambda_{r2}}(t_{2})}\right)^{*}T_{\rho_{\Lambda_{0}}}^{\mathrm{(l)}\Lambda_{r0}\mathbb{I}}\left(V_{\sigma,{\Lambda_{r2}}(t_{2})}\right),

(C.2)

and for each
t_{2}\geq t
,

V_{\sigma,{\Lambda_{r2}}(t_{2})}\in\pi_{\mathop{\mathrm{bd}}}\left({\mathcal{A}}_{({\Lambda_{r2}}(t))^{c}\cap\mathop{\mathrm{H}_{U}}}\right)^{\prime}\cap{\mathcal{F}}_{{\mathop{\mathrm{bd}}}}^{U}
,
hence

\displaystyle\begin{split}\left\|\iota^{(\Lambda_{0},\Lambda_{r0})}\left({\rho_{\Lambda_{0}}}:\sigma\right)-\mathbb{I}\right\|\leq\left\|\left.\left(T_{\rho_{\Lambda_{0}}}^{\mathrm{(l)}\Lambda_{r0}\mathbb{I}}-\mathop{\mathrm{id}}\nolimits\right)\right|_{\pi_{\mathop{\mathrm{bd}}}\left({\mathcal{A}}_{({\Lambda_{r2}}(t))^{c}\cap\mathop{\mathrm{H}_{U}}}\right)^{\prime}\cap{\mathcal{F}}_{{\mathop{\mathrm{bd}}}}^{U}}\right\|.\end{split}

(C.3)

Combining with Lemma
3.6
, we obtain the claim.

\square

Lemma C.2
.

Consider the setting in subsection
1.1
.
Let
\rho\in O^{rU}_{\mathop{\mathrm{bd}}}
.
Suppose that for each

(\Lambda_{0},\Lambda_{r0})\in\mathcal{PC}
, there exists
\rho_{\Lambda_{0}}\in O^{rU}_{\mathop{\mathrm{bd},\Lambda_{r0}}}

with

\hat{\iota}(\rho_{\Lambda_{0}}:-):O^{rU}_{\mathop{\mathrm{bd},\Lambda_{r0}}}\to{\mathcal{U}}({\mathcal{H}}_{\mathop{\mathrm{bd}}})
satisfying the following.

(i)

There exists
{\Lambda_{r2}}\in{\mathcal{C}}_{{\mathop{\mathrm{bd}}}}^{r}
with
\Lambda_{0}\leftarrow_{r}{\Lambda_{r2}}
,
{\Lambda_{r2}}\subset\Lambda_{r0}

such that

\displaystyle\lim_{t\to\infty}\sup_{\sigma\in O^{rU}_{\mathop{\mathrm{bd},{\Lambda_{r2}}(t)}}}\left\|\hat{\iota}({\rho_{\Lambda_{0}}}:\sigma)-\mathbb{I}\right\|=0.

(C.4)

(ii)

For all
\sigma,\sigma^{\prime}\in O^{rU}_{\mathop{\mathrm{bd},\Lambda_{r0}}}
,

S\in(\sigma,\sigma^{\prime})_{U}
,

\displaystyle\hat{\iota}({\rho_{\Lambda_{0}}}:\sigma^{\prime})(\mathbb{I}_{\rho_{\Lambda_{0}}}\otimes S)=(S\otimes\mathbb{I}_{\rho_{\Lambda_{0}}})\hat{\iota}({\rho_{\Lambda_{0}}}:\sigma).

(C.5)

(iii)

For each
u\in{\mathcal{U}}({\mathcal{A}}_{\Lambda_{0}^{c}\cap\Lambda_{r0}})
,

\hat{\iota}(\rho_{\Lambda_{0}}:\sigma_{u})=\mathbb{I}
,
with
\sigma_{u}:=\pi_{\mathop{\mathrm{bd}}}\mathop{\mathrm{Ad}}\nolimits u
.

(iv)

\rho\simeq_{U}\rho_{\Lambda_{0}}
.

Then
\rho
belongs to
O_{\mathop{\mathrm{bd}}}^{\mathop{\mathrm{bu}}}
.

Remark C.3
.

We already saw that
\rho\in O_{\mathop{\mathrm{bd}}}^{\mathop{\mathrm{bu}}}
satisfies the assumptions of the Lemma,
under Assumption
1.6
and Assumption
1.7
.

For any
\Lambda\in{\mathcal{C}}_{{\mathop{\mathrm{bk}}}}^{U}
, there exists
(\Lambda_{0},\Lambda_{r0})\in\mathcal{PC}

such that
\Lambda_{0}\subset\Lambda\cap\mathop{\mathrm{H}_{U}}
.
For this
(\Lambda_{0},\Lambda_{r0})\in\mathcal{PC}
, consider the representation
\rho_{\Lambda_{0}}\in O^{rU}_{\mathop{\mathrm{bd},\Lambda_{r0}}}
in the assumption.
For
{\Lambda_{r2}}
in (i) and
any
\sigma\in O^{rU}_{\mathop{\mathrm{bd},\Lambda_{r0}}}
,
take
V_{\sigma,{\Lambda_{r2}}(t)}\in{\mathcal{V}}_{\sigma{\Lambda_{r2}}(t)}^{{\mathop{\mathrm{bd}}}U}
,
t\geq 0
.
Then we have
\sigma(t):=\mathop{\mathrm{Ad}}\nolimits\left(V_{\sigma,{\Lambda_{r2}}(t)}\right)\sigma\in O^{rU}_{\mathop{\mathrm{bd}{\Lambda_{r2}}(t)}}\subset O^{rU}_{\mathop{\mathrm{bd},\Lambda_{r0}}}
and

V_{\sigma,{\Lambda_{r2}}(t)}\in\left(\sigma,\sigma(t)\right)_{U}
.
Hence by the naturality (ii), we have

\displaystyle\hat{\iota}({\rho_{\Lambda_{0}}}:\sigma)=(V_{\sigma,{\Lambda_{r2}}(t)}\otimes\mathbb{I}_{\rho_{\Lambda_{0}}})^{*}\hat{\iota}({\rho_{\Lambda_{0}}}:\sigma(t))(\mathbb{I}_{\rho_{\Lambda_{0}}}\otimes V_{\sigma,{\Lambda_{r2}}(t)})=(V_{\sigma,{\Lambda_{r2}}(t)})^{*}\hat{\iota}({\rho_{\Lambda_{0}}}:\sigma(t))T_{\rho_{\Lambda_{0}}}^{\mathrm{(l)}\Lambda_{r0}\mathbb{I}}\left(V_{\sigma,{\Lambda_{r2}}(t)}\right).

(C.6)

Taking
t\to\infty
, from (i), we obtain

\displaystyle\begin{split}\hat{\iota}({\rho_{\Lambda_{0}}}:\sigma)=\lim_{t\to\infty}(V_{\sigma,{\Lambda_{r2}}(t)})^{*}T_{\rho_{\Lambda_{0}}}^{\mathrm{(l)}\Lambda_{r0}\mathbb{I}}\left(V_{\sigma,{\Lambda_{r2}}(t)}\right).\end{split}

(C.7)

For each

\sigma_{u}:=\pi_{\mathop{\mathrm{bd}}}\mathop{\mathrm{Ad}}\nolimits u
with
u\in{\mathcal{U}}({\mathcal{A}}_{\Lambda_{0}^{c}\cap\Lambda_{r0}})
,
and
\pi_{\mathop{\mathrm{bd}}}(u^{*})\in{\mathcal{V}}_{\sigma_{u}{\Lambda_{r2}}(t)}^{{\mathop{\mathrm{bd}}}U}
, we apply this formula.
Then, with (iii), we obtain

\displaystyle\begin{split}\mathbb{I}=\hat{\iota}({\rho_{\Lambda_{0}}}:\sigma_{u})=\pi_{\mathop{\mathrm{bd}}}(u)T_{\rho_{\Lambda_{0}}}^{\mathrm{(l)}\Lambda_{r0}\mathbb{I}}\left(\pi_{\mathop{\mathrm{bd}}}(u^{*})\right)=\pi_{\mathop{\mathrm{bd}}}(u)\rho_{\Lambda_{0}}(u^{*}),\end{split}

(C.8)

for all
u\in{\mathcal{U}}({\mathcal{A}}_{\Lambda_{0}^{c}\cap\Lambda_{r0}})
.
This, combined with
\rho_{\Lambda_{0}}\in O^{rU}_{\mathop{\mathrm{bd},\Lambda_{r0}}}
,
implies
\rho_{\Lambda_{0}}|_{{\mathcal{A}}_{\Lambda_{0}^{c}\cap\mathop{\mathrm{H}_{U}}}}=\pi_{\mathop{\mathrm{bd}}}|_{{\mathcal{A}}_{\Lambda_{0}^{c}\cap\mathop{\mathrm{H}_{U}}}}
,
hence
\rho_{\Lambda_{0}}|_{{\mathcal{A}}_{\Lambda^{c}\cap\mathop{\mathrm{H}_{U}}}}=\pi_{\mathop{\mathrm{bd}}}|_{{\mathcal{A}}_{\Lambda^{c}\cap\mathop{\mathrm{H}_{U}}}}
.
From (iv), we conclude
\rho\in O_{\mathop{\mathrm{bd}}}^{\mathop{\mathrm{bu}}}
.

\square
